She passed﻿ ﻿… and I felt a real joy, as though in refusing the love which she offered me in thought I had just now fulfilled a great duty.

She occupied my mind for a week. I resumed my furious walks through the moor, on the strand, and I wished I could conquer my passion. While walking, driven by the wind, carried along by that peculiar exaltation occasioned by rain pelting the sea shore, I imagined all sorts of romantic conversations with demoiselle Landudec and nocturnal adventures which took place in enchanted and lunar places. Like the characters in an opera, we vied with each other in sublime thought, in heroic sacrifices, in wonderful devotion; under the spell of the passionate rhythms and stirring recurrences of the song of the elements, we extended the boundaries of human self-denial. A sobbing orchestra accompanied the anguish of our voices.

===Chinese===
弥撒完了，幻象也消失了。教堂里响起一片挪椅子和沉重脚步的声音，唱诗班的孩子吹熄了祭坛上的细蜡烛。……那姑娘还跪着，还在祈祷。她的脸，我只分辨出一个侧影，隐没在白纱的暗影里。画过十字，她站起身来。我只好挪开椅子给她让路。她走过去了……我心里竟感到一阵真正的欢喜，仿佛方才在思想上拒绝了她献给我的爱，就像尽完了一桩重大的本分。

她在我心里盘桓了一个礼拜。我重新开始在荒原上、海滩上发疯似的奔走，一心想征服我的情欲。走着走着，被风驱赶着，被那种雨水抽打海岸时油然而生的异样亢奋裹挟着，我虚构出种种同朗迪德克小姐的浪漫对话，种种发生在迷人月境里的夜半奇遇。像歌剧中的人物一样，我们竞相抛出崇高的思想、英雄的牺牲、了不得的奉献；在风、雨、海那些元素之歌的热烈节奏和回环往复的魔力之下，我们把人类克己的疆界越推越远。一支呜咽的乐队，为我们声音里的苦楚伴奏。

===Original===
“I love you! I love you!”

“No, no! You must not love me!”

She, in a very long white gown, with a bewildered look and outstretched arms﻿ ﻿… I, gloomy, inexorable, the calves of my legs swelling under the violet silk tight garment, my hair disheveled by the wind.

“I love you! I love you!”

“No! No! You must not love me!”

And the violins emitted inaudible plaints, the wind instruments moaned, while the double basses and the dulcimers rumbled like tempest and peals of thunder.

===Chinese===
“我爱你！我爱你！”

“不，不！你千万不要爱我！”

她，穿着雪白的长裙，眼神迷乱，双臂张开……我，阴沉，决绝，紫色紧身绸衣下小腿绷得鼓胀，头发被风吹得散乱。

“我爱你！我爱你！”

“不！不！你千万不要爱我！”

于是小提琴发出听不见的哀诉，管乐器呜咽，而低音提琴和扬琴轰鸣如风暴、如阵阵雷霆。

===Original===
Oh, the tragicomedy of sorrow!

A curious thing! Demoiselle Landudec and Juliette became one; I no longer separated them, I confused them in my dreams, extravagant and melodramatic. Both were too pure for me.

“No! No! I am a leper, leave me alone!”

They passionately kissed my wounds, spoke of death and cried: “I love you! I love you!”

And vanquished, subdued, redeemed by love I fell at their feet. The old father, dying, spread his arms over us and blessed us, the three of us!

===Chinese===
啊，这悲伤的悲喜剧！

说来奇怪！朗迪德克小姐和朱丽叶特渐渐合成了一个人；我再也分不开她们，在那些荒诞而夸张的梦里，我把她们混作了一处。两个人对我来说，都太纯洁了。

“不！不！我是麻风病人，别管我！”

她们狂热地亲吻我的伤口，谈论着死亡，喊道：“我爱你！我爱你！”

于是我被战胜了，被降服了，被爱赎回了，倒在她们脚下。那位垂死的老父亲向我们张开手臂，为我们祝福——为我们三个人！

===Original===
This trance did not last long; I soon found myself on the dune, face to face with Juliette.

There were no violins, no wind instruments any longer, only the howl of anguish and revolt, the cry of a captured stag craving the female of its species.

“Juliette! Juliette!”

===Chinese===
这迷狂没有持续多久；我很快就发现自己还是在沙丘上，同朱丽叶特面对面。

不再有小提琴，不再有管乐器，只有痛苦和反抗的嗥叫，一头被擒的牡鹿渴求同类的嘶鸣。

“朱丽叶特！朱丽叶特！”

===Original===
One evening I returned home more despondent than ever, my mind obsessed with dismal projects, my arms and hands in some manner agitated by a mad desire to kill, to strangle. I would have liked to feel something alive writhing, rattling, dying under the pressure of my fingers. Mother Le Gannec was standing at the threshold, darning the never failing pair of stockings. She said to me:

“How late you are today, friend Mintié! I have prepared some nice sea-crab for you!”

“Leave me alone, you drivelling woman!” I shouted. “I don’t want your sea-crab, I don’t want anything, do you hear me?”

And sputtering angry words, I brutally made her step aside to let me pass. The poor kindly woman, stupefied by my action, lifted her arms to heaven and moaned.

“Ah! My Lord! Ah, Jesus!”

===Chinese===
一天傍晚，我回到家里，比任何时候都更沮丧，脑子里塞满了阴惨的盘算，两条胳膊和一双手不知怎么总躁动着一股要杀、要掐的疯狂欲望。我真想觉出有个活物在我手指的钳制下扭动、咯咯地喘、死去。勒加内克大妈站在门口，补着那双永远补不完的袜子。她对我说：

“你今天回来得多晚啊，我的曼蒂埃朋友！我给你备好了好吃的海蟹！”

“别来烦我，你这个唠叨婆子！”我嚷道，“我不要你的海蟹，我什么都不要，你听见没有？”

我骂骂咧咧，粗暴地把她推开，好让我过去。可怜的好女人被这一下惊呆了，朝天举起双臂，呜咽起来。

“啊！我的天主！啊，耶稣啊！”

===Original===
I went to my room and locked myself in. At first I rolled on the bed, smashed two chairs, beat my head against the wall. Then, I suddenly sat down to write a letter to Juliette, exalted, raging, full of terrible threats and humble entreaties; a letter in which I spoke of killing her, of forgiving her, in which I begged her to come to see me before I died, describing to her in tragic detail the cliff from which I was going to throw myself into the sea. I compared her to the lowest women in the brothel and two lines further I compared her to the Holy Virgin. More than twenty times I started this letter over again, excited, weeping, in turn delirious with rage and swooning with tenderness. Presently I heard a noise behind the door like the scratching of a mouse. I opened it. Mother Le Gannec was standing there, trembling and pale; she looked at me with her kind, bewildered eyes.

“What are you doing here?” I shouted. “Why do you spy on me? Go away!”

“Friend Mintié,” muttered the sainted woman, “don’t be angry. I can see that you are unhappy and I came to know if I can help you!”

“Well, suppose I am unhappy! Does that concern you? Here, take this letter to the post office and leave me in peace.”

===Chinese===
我回到自己房里，把门锁上。起先我在床上打滚，砸坏了两把椅子，拿头往墙上撞。随后，我突然坐下来给朱丽叶特写信；写得意气昂扬，怒气冲冲，满纸可怕的威胁和卑贱的哀求；信里我说要杀她，又说饶恕她；求她在我就死之前来看我一眼，还悲悲惨惨地给她细细描绘我要从哪一处悬崖跳进海里。前一刻我把她比作窑子里最下贱的女人，隔两行又把她比作圣洁的圣母。这封信我重起了二十几遍，兴奋着，流着泪，一时狂怒得说胡话，一时又柔情得几乎晕过去。过了一阵，我听见门后有动静，像老鼠的窸窣。我开了门。勒加内克大妈站在那儿，发着抖，脸色发白；她用她那双善良而惶惑的眼睛望着我。

“你在这儿干什么？”我嚷道，“你为什么窥探我？走开！”

“我的曼蒂埃朋友，”这位圣徒一样的女人讷讷地说，“别生气。我看得出你不幸，我来是想看看能不能帮上你！”

“好啊，就算我不幸！跟你有什么相干？喏，把这封信送到邮局去，让我清静清静。”

===Original===
For four days I did not leave my room. Mother Le Gannec came to make my bed and serve my meal. She was humble, timid, more attentive than ever, sighing:

“Ah! What a misfortune! My Lord, what a misfortune!”

I realized that I was not acting as I should; she had been so kind to me; I wanted to ask forgiveness for my rudeness. Her white coif, her black shawl, her sad figure of an afflicted mother touched me. But a sort of foolish pride threw a damper on this effusion. She walked near me, resigned, with an air of infinite motherly pity; from time to time she repeated:

“Ah! What a misfortune! My Lord! What a misfortune!”

===Chinese===
有四天我没出房门。勒加内克大妈来给我铺床、送饭。她谦卑，怯生生的，比以往任何时候都更尽心，一面叹着气：

“啊！多大的不幸啊！我的天主，多大的不幸啊！”

我明白自己做得不像话；她一直待我那么好；我想为自己的粗鲁赔个不是。她那白色的头巾，那黑披肩，那副受难母亲似的愁苦身影，打动了我。可是一种愚蠢的骄傲，又给这股热劲浇了冷水。她在我跟前走动，逆来顺受，带着一副无尽的慈母般的怜悯神气；时不时地重复一句：

“啊！多大的不幸啊！我的天主！多大的不幸啊！”

===Original===
The day drew to a close. While Mother La Gannec, after having mailed the letter, was sweeping the room, I sat at the window, my elbows resting on the ledge. The sun had disappeared behind the horizon line, leaving of its dazzling glory only a reddish transparency on the sky, and the sea, grown dark, dull, no longer reflecting light, assumed a sad hue. Night came, silent and slow, and the air was so calm that one could hear the rhythmic noise of oars striking the water of the wharf and the distant creaking of halliards on the masts’ tops. The beacon light was turned on, its red light turning in space like some irrational astral body.﻿ ﻿… And I felt very unhappy!

Juliette did not answer me!﻿ ﻿… Juliette would not come!﻿ ﻿… My letter, no doubt, had frightened her. She had recalled furious, savage, strangling scenes. She was afraid and would not come! And besides, were there not races, banquets, dinners, a line of impatient men at her door, waiting for her, claiming her, men who had paid in advance for the promised night? Why should she come, after all? There was no Casino on this desolate beach; in this Godforsaken corner of the coast there was no one to whom she could sell herself.

As for me, she had taken all my money, my brains, my honor, my future, everything! What more could I give her? Nothing. Why then should she come? If I had only told her that I had ten thousand francs left she would have come running. But to what purpose? Ah! Let her not come! My anger subsided, self-disgust replaced it, a frightful disgust! How could it be possible that a man who was not bad, whose past aspirations lacked neither nobility of character nor ardor, should fall so low, in such a short time, into a mire so deep that no human force could lift him out of it!﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===
这一天到了尾声。勒加内克大妈寄完信回来，正扫着屋子；我坐在窗前，双肘支在窗台上。太阳已经沉下天际线，它那炫目的荣光只在天空留下一抹淡红的透明；海暗了，浊了，不再反光，换上一副愁惨的颜色。夜来了，无声而缓慢；空气那么沉静，听得见船桨一下一下拍打码头水面的声响，听得见远处桅顶上升降索的吱嘎。灯塔的灯点起来了，红光在太空里旋转，像一颗没有理性的星体。……我觉着自己真是不幸极了！

朱丽叶特没有回信！……朱丽叶特不会来了！……我的信，不用说，把她吓着了。她回想起了那些狂怒的、野蛮的、掐人脖子的场面。她害怕了，不来了！再说，不是还有赛马、筵宴、晚宴，不是还有门口那一队急不可耐的男人——等着她、索求她、预付过那一夜定钱的男人们么？说到底，她为什么要来？这片荒凉的海滩上没有娱乐场；在这处天不管地不问的海岸角落，没有一个她可以卖身的人。

至于我，她拿走了我全部的钱、我的脑子、我的名誉、我的前途，一切！我还能给她什么呢？什么也没有。那她还来做什么？当初我要是告诉她我还有一万法郎，她早就飞奔来了。可那又图什么呢？啊！让她别来吧！我的怒气平息了，自厌接了上来，一种可怕的厌恶！一个并不坏的人，一个过去的志向里既不乏品格之高、也不乏热血之诚的人，怎么可能在这样短的时日里，跌进这样深的一摊泥里——深到人间的力量再也拔不出他来！……这怎么可能！

===Original===
What I now suffered from was not so much my own follies, my own disgrace and crimes as the misery which I had caused those around me. Old Marie!﻿ ﻿… Old Felix!﻿ ﻿… Oh, the poor couple! Where were they now? What were they doing? Did they have anything to eat, at least? Had I not compelled them to beg their bread when I expelled them﻿—so old, so kind, so confiding, more feeble and desolate than homeless dogs! I saw them bent over their staffs, horribly thin, coughing, harassed, spending nights in chance lodgings. And the sainted Mother Le Gannec who took care of me as a mother her child, who lulled me to sleep with her warm caresses like those bestowed on little ones! Instead of kneeling before her, of thanking her, did I not treat her brutally, did I not almost beat her! Ah, no! Let her not come! Let her not come!

Mother Le Gannec lit the lamp, and I was about to close the window when I heard the tinkling of small bells upon the road, then the trundling of a carriage. I mechanically looked out. Indeed a carriage had ascended the steep hill of this place, it was a sort of stage which appeared very high and loaded with trunks. A fisherman passed by. The postman asked him:

“Will you please tell us where the house of Madame Le Gannec is?” “It is in front of you,” answered the fisherman, who indicated the house with a motion of hand and continued on his way.

I grew very pale﻿ ﻿… and I saw by the light of the lantern a small gloved hand resting on the handle of the stage door.

===Chinese===
如今折磨我的，与其说是我自己的荒唐、我自己的丢脸和罪过，不如说是我加在我身边那些人身上的苦难。老玛丽！……老费利克斯！……啊，这对可怜的老人！他们如今在哪儿？在做什么？至少，还有东西吃么？我撵走他们的时候，不是逼得他们讨面包去了么——他们那么老，那么善良，那么信人，比丧家的狗还孱弱、还孤苦！我看见他们拄着拐杖，佝偻着，瘦得吓人，咳着，被穷困追逼着，夜夜投不着一个准宿处。还有圣徒般的勒加内克大妈，母亲待孩子一般地照料我，用她温暖的抚爱哄我入睡，像哄小孩子那样！我不曾跪在她面前谢她，反而那样粗暴地待她，我岂不是差点动手打了她！啊，不！让她别来吧！让她别来吧！

勒加内克大妈点上了灯；我正要关窗，忽听得路上传来小铃铛的叮当声，接着是马车的轱辘声。我机械地朝外望去。果真有一辆车爬上了村里那道陡坡；是一种公共驿车的样子，车身显得很高，堆满了行李箱。一个渔夫打门前经过。邮差问他：

“劳驾，您能告诉我们勒加内克太太的家在哪儿吗？”“就在您眼前，”渔夫答道，用手一指那座房子，接着赶他的路去了。

我的脸刷地白了……借着车灯的光，我看见一只戴手套的小手，正搭在驿车车门的把手上。

===Original===
“Juliette! Juliette!” I shouted like a madman. “Mother Le Gannec, it’s Juliette!﻿ ﻿… Quick, quick﻿ ﻿… it’s Juliette!”

Running, tumbling down the stairway, I dashed to the street: “Juliette! My Juliette!”

Arms embraced me, lips pressed against my cheek, a voice breathed in my ears:

“Jean! My dear little Jean!”

And I swooned into the arms of Juliette.

===Chinese===
“朱丽叶特！朱丽叶特！”我疯子似的喊起来。“勒加内克大妈，是朱丽叶特！……快，快……是朱丽叶特！”

我跑着，跌跌撞撞冲下楼梯，奔到街上：“朱丽叶特！我的朱丽叶特！”

一双臂膀搂住了我，嘴唇贴上我的脸颊，一个声音在我耳边呼吸：

“让！我亲爱的小让！”

我晕倒在朱丽叶特的怀抱里。

===Original===
It did not take me long to regain my senses, however. They put me to bed and Juliette, bent over me, embraced me, crying:

“Ah! Poor little thing. How you frightened me! How pale you still are! Is it all over, tell me? Speak to me, my Jean!”

I did nothing but look at her. It seemed as though my whole being, inert and rigid, smitten by a powerful blow, by some great suffering or happiness﻿—I did not know which﻿—had brought back and crowded into my glance all the life forces leaving me, dripping from my limbs, my veins, my heart, my brains.﻿ ﻿… I was looking at her! She was still beautiful, a little paler than in the past, but on the whole the same as ever, with her beautiful, sweet eyes, her lovely mouth, her deliciously childish voice. In her countenance, her gestures, the movements of her body, her words I wanted to find some sorrowful traces of her unknown existence, some blemish, some evidence of depravity, something new and more withered. But no, she was paler, and that was all. And I burst into tears.

===Chinese===
不过我很快便苏醒过来。他们把我扶上床，朱丽叶特俯下身搂着我，喊着：

“啊！可怜的小宝贝。你把我吓坏了！你的脸色到现在还这么白！都过去了么，你说呀？跟我说句话，我的让！”

我只是望着她。仿佛我整个的存在——僵直而麻木，被一下重击、被某种巨大的痛苦或幸福击中（我分不清是哪一种）——把正在离我而去的全部生命力都召回拢来，塞进了我的目光里；它们从我的四肢、我的血脉、我的心、我的脑子里滴落。……我望着她！她依旧美丽，比从前略略苍白些，但大体上还是老样子：那双美丽温柔的眼睛，那张可爱的嘴，那副甜得像孩子似的嗓音。在她的面容里、手势里、身体的举动里、话语里，我想找出她那段无人知晓的生活留下的愁苦痕迹，找出一点瑕疵、一点堕落的凭据、一点新添的憔悴。可是没有，她只是苍白了一点，仅此而已。于是我放声哭了。

===Original===
“Sit still, I want to look at you more, my little Juliette!”

She drank in my tears and wept, holding me in a close embrace.

“My Jean! Ah, my adored Jean!”

Mother Le Gannec rapped at the door of the room. She did not speak to Juliette, pretending not to see her.

===Chinese===
“坐好别动，我要多看看你，我的小朱丽叶特！”

她饮着我的眼泪，也哭了，紧紧地搂着我。

“我的让！啊，我心爱的让！”

勒加内克大妈在房门上敲了敲。她不同朱丽叶特说话，装作没有看见她。

===Original===
“What shall I do with the trunks, friend Mintié?” she asked.

“Have someone bring them up here, Mother Le Gannec.”

“You could not bring them all up here,” the old woman harshly replied.

“Have you got many of them, dearie?”

“Many? Why no! There are only six. These people are stupid!”

“Well, Mother Le Gannec,” I said, “keep them downstairs tonight. We shall see tomorrow.”

===Chinese===
“这些箱子怎么办理，我的曼蒂埃朋友？”她问。

“叫人把它们搬上来吧，勒加内克大妈。”

“可不能都搬到这儿来，”老太太硬邦邦地回了一句。

“你带了很多箱子来么，亲爱的？”

“很多？才不呢！只有六只。这些人真是蠢！”

“那就这样吧，勒加内克大妈，”我说，“今晚先把它们搁在楼下。明天再说。”

===Original===
I got up, while Juliette examined the room, occasionally exclaiming:

“Why, it’s so nice here! There’s a lot of fun here, my dear. And you have a bed, too, a real bed. And I thought they slept in wardrobes in Brittany! Ah! What is that? Don’t stir, Jean, don’t stir.”

From the mantelpiece she took a large shell and put it to her ear.

“Wait!” she said with disappointment. “Wait now, it does not make that sh-sh-sh sound. Why is that?”

===Chinese===
我起了床；朱丽叶特正打量着房间，不时发出惊叹：

“咦，这儿真不错呀！这儿多有意思呀，亲爱的。你还有一张床，一张真正的床。我还当布列塔尼人睡在柜子里呢！啊！那是什么？别动，让，别动。”

她从壁炉台上拿起一只大海螺，贴到耳边。

“等等！”她失望地说。“等等，它怎么不发出那种嗤——嗤——嗤的声音了？这是怎么回事？”

===Original===
She suddenly rushed into my arms and covered me with kisses.

“Ah! your beard! You are growing whiskers, you villain! Ah how long your hair is! And how thin you are! And I, have I changed much! Am I still beautiful?”

She placed her arms around my neck and rested her head on my shoulder:

“Tell me what you have been doing here, how you have spent your time, what you have been thinking about. Tell it all to your little wifie. And don’t tell lies. Tell her everything, everything.”

===Chinese===
她突然扑进我的怀里，用亲吻把我盖了个满身。

“啊！你的胡子！你长络腮胡子了，你这个坏家伙！啊，你的头发多长呀！你多瘦呀！我呢，我变了很多么？我还漂亮么？”

她搂住我的脖子，把头靠在我肩上：

“告诉我，你在这儿做了些什么，日子是怎么过的，心里都在想些什么。统统讲给你的小媳妇儿听。不许撒谎。什么都告诉她，什么都讲。”

===Original===
Then I described my furious walks, my prostrations on the dune, my sobbing, the fact that I had been seeing her everywhere, calling her like a madman in the wind, in the tempest.

“Poor little thing!” she sighed. “And you probably have not even a raincoat.”

“And you? you, my Juliette? Did you ever think of me?”

“Ah! When I found you gone from the house I thought I would die. Celestine told me that a man had come to take you away! Still I waited.﻿ ﻿… He will come, he will come.﻿ ﻿… But you did not come back. The next morning I ran to Lirat! Oh, if you only knew how he received me!﻿ ﻿… how he treated me! And I asked everybody: ‘Do you know where Jean is?’ And no one could answer me. Oh, you naughty boy! To leave me like that﻿ ﻿… without a word! Don’t you love me anymore? Then, you understand, I wanted to forget myself. I was suffering too much.”

===Chinese===
于是我便讲起我那些发疯的奔走，我在沙丘上的扑倒，我的哭泣，讲起我怎样处处看见她，怎样在风里、在暴风雨里像疯子一样喊她。

“可怜的小宝贝！”她叹道，“你恐怕连件雨衣都没有吧。”

“你呢？你呢，我的朱丽叶特？你想过我么？”

“啊！发现你从家里走了，我以为自己要死了。塞莱斯特告诉我，来了一个男人把你带走了！我还是等。……他会回来的，他会回来的。……可你没有回来。第二天一早我就跑到利拉家去！啊，你要知道他是怎么接待我的！……怎么对待我的！我见人就问：‘你知道让在哪儿么？’没有一个人答得上来。啊，你这淘气包！就这样丢下我……一句话也没有！你不再爱我了吗？后来，你明白么，我只想忘掉自己。我太苦了。”

===Original===
Her words had a sharp, curt ring in them:

“As for Lirat, you may rest assured, my dear, I’ll get even with him. You’ll see! It’ll be a farce! What a mean person your friend Lirat is! But you’ll see.”

One thing tormented me: how many days or weeks would Juliette stay with me? She had brought six trunks with her; hence she intended to remain at Ploch for a month at least﻿—perhaps longer. Together with the great anticipated joy of possessing her without fear or obstacle, there mingled a keen uneasiness. I had no money, and I knew Juliette too well not to realize that she would not resign herself to a life like mine, and I foresaw expenditures which I was not in a position to make. What was to be done? Not having enough courage to ask her directly, I answered:

“We have plenty of time to think of it, my dear. In about three months from now when we shall go back to Paris.

===Chinese===
她的话里带着一种尖利、生硬的调子：

“至于利拉，你放心好了，亲爱的，我要跟他算这笔账。你瞧着吧！有好戏看的！你的朋友利拉是个多卑鄙的人！你瞧着吧。”

有一件事折磨着我：朱丽叶特会陪我待多少天、多少个礼拜？她随身带来了六只箱子；这么说，她打算在普洛赫至少住上一个月——也许更久。想到即将可以无所畏惧、无所阻拦地拥有她，那份巨大的欢喜里，掺进了一股尖锐的不安。我没有钱；我对朱丽叶特太了解了，不会不明白她是决计安不了我这种日子的；我预见到一笔笔我根本无力应付的开销。怎么办呢？我没有勇气直接问她，只好答道：

“咱们有的是工夫慢慢商量，亲爱的。大概三个月以后，等咱们回巴黎去再说。”

===Original===
“Three months! Why no, my poor little thing, I leave in a week. I am so sorry.”

“Stay here, my little Juliette, I implore you, stay here altogether. Stay longer! A fortnight!”

“It is impossible, really. Oh, don’t be sad, my dear! Don’t cry! If you cry I won’t tell you something very nice.”

She became more affectionate, nestled and resumed:

“Listen, my dear. I have only one thought, and that is to live with you! We shall leave Paris, we shall move into a small house, hidden so well, you see, that no one will know that we are living. All we need is an income of twenty thousand francs.”

===Chinese===
“三个月！才不呢，我可怜的小宝贝，我一个星期之内就走。真对不起。”

“留下吧，我的小朱丽叶特，我求你了，干脆长住下来吧。多待些日子！两个礼拜！”

“不可能的，真的。啊，别难过，亲爱的！别哭！你要是哭，我就不告诉你一件顶好的事了。”

她更加亲热起来，偎过来，接着说：

“听着，亲爱的。我心里只有一个念头，就是同你一起生活！我们要离开巴黎，搬进一座小房子，藏得好好的，你明白么，藏到没有人知道我们还在世上。咱们只需要两万法郎的年金。”

===Original===
“Where do you expect me to get that much now?” I exclaimed discouraged.

“Now, listen to me,” continued Juliette. “We need only twenty thousand francs. Well, I have figured it all out! In six months we shall have it.”

Juliette looked at me with a mysterious air and repeated:

“We shall have it!”

===Chinese===
“你叫我如今上哪儿去弄这么一大笔？”我灰心绝望地喊道。

“哎，听我说，”朱丽叶特接着讲，“咱们只缺两万法郎。喏，我全盘算好了！六个月之内，咱们就有了。”

朱丽叶特带着一种神秘的神气望着我，重复道：

“咱们就有了！”

===Original===
“Please don’t talk like that, my dear. You don’t know how you hurt me.”

Juliette raised her voice, the wrinkle on her forehead grew rigid.

“Then you want me always to belong to others?”

“Oh! keep still, Juliette! Keep still! Never talk to me like that, never!”

“You are so funny! Come now, be nice and embrace me!”

===Chinese===
“求你别这么说，亲爱的。你不知道这话多么伤我。”

朱丽叶特提高了嗓门，额头上那道皱纹绷紧了。

“这么说，你要我永远属于别的人？”

“啊！别说了，朱丽叶特！别说了！永远别这样同我讲，永远别！”

“你真逗！来吧，乖一点，抱抱我！”

===Original===
The next morning, while dressing in the midst of opened trunks and scattered dresses, very much disconcerted by the absence of her chambermaid, she made all sorts of plans for the day. She wanted to take a walk on the jetty, to visit the lighthouse, to fish, to walk to the dune and sit down on the spot where I had cried so much. She said she enjoyed watching the pretty Breton girls in braided and embroidered dresses, like those in the theatre, drinking fresh milk on the farms!

“Are there any boats here?”

“Yes.”

“Lot of them?”

“Certainly.”

“Ah! What a chance. I like boats so much!”

===Chinese===
第二天早晨，她在一堆打开的箱子和散乱的衣裙中间梳妆，为贴身女仆不在跟前而大为狼狈，一边给这一天定下种种计划。她要到防波堤上去散步，去参观灯塔，去钓鱼，要一路走到沙丘上，坐在我哭过那么多回的那个地方。她说，她顶喜欢看布列塔尼的漂亮姑娘——穿着镶辫绣花裙子的，同戏台上的一样——在农庄里喝新鲜牛奶！

“这儿有船么？”

“有。”

“多么？”

“当然多。”

“啊！太好了。我可喜欢船啦！”

===Original===
Then she gave me news of Paris. Gabrielle no longer lived with Robert. Malterre was married. Jesselin was on a trip. He had had several duels. And gossip about everybody. All this bad odor of Paris brought back my melancholy and bitter memories. Seeing me sad, she interrupted herself and embraced me, assuming an air of distress:

“Ah! Perhaps you suppose I like this life!” she said plaintively, “and that I only think of amusing myself, of flirting. If you only knew! There are certain things that I can’t tell you. But if you knew what a torture it is to me! You think you are unhappy! How about me? Why, if I did not have the hope of living with my Jean I would kill myself, so often do I feel disgusted with life.”

===Chinese===
接着她给我讲巴黎的消息。加布里埃尔（Gabrielle）不再同罗贝尔（Robert）一起住了。马尔特尔结婚了。热瑟兰出门旅行去了。他打过好几场决斗。还有关于每个人的闲话。巴黎的这股浊臭，勾回了我忧郁而辛酸的回忆。她见我难过，便打住话头，搂住我，装出一副愁苦的样子：

“啊！你大概以为我喜欢这种生活吧！”她哀声说，“以为我一心只想寻欢作乐、卖弄风情。你要是知道就好了！有些事我没法对你说。可你要知道这对我是一场怎样的折磨！你觉得自己不幸！那我呢？唉，要不是盼着有一天同我的让一起生活，我早自杀了——我常常厌世厌到这个地步。”

===Original===
And, dreaming and wheedling, she would revert to the subject of farming, of hidden paths covered with verdure, of the peace and sweetness of a retired life amid flowers, domestic animals and love. Ah! devoted, humble, eternal love, love that was to brighten our life like the dazzling sun!

We went out after the breakfast which Mother Le Gannec sullenly served us, without once opening her mouth. We were hardly out, when the wind freshened; it disheveled Juliette’s hair. She wanted to return to the house.

“Ah! The wind, dear! I can’t stand the wind. It spoils my hair and makes me sick.”

She was bored all day and our kisses could not dispel the feeling of emptiness. Just as in the past, in my study, she spread a napkin on her dress, placed a few small nail brushes and files on the napkin, and gravely began to polish her nails. I suffered cruelly, and the vision of the old man at the window obsessed me.

===Chinese===
于是她出了神，软语温存，又回到种田度日的话题上来：绿荫覆盖的幽径，退隐的日子在花木、家畜和爱情中间那种安宁与甘美。啊！忠诚的、谦卑的、永恒的爱，像那耀眼的太阳一样照亮我们生命的爱！

早饭后我们出了门——那顿饭勒加内克大妈阴沉着脸端给我们，一句话也没说。我们刚一出门，风就紧了；吹乱了朱丽叶特的头发。她就想回屋里去。

“啊！这风，亲爱的！我受不了风。它毁我的头发，还叫我难受。”

她整天闷闷不乐，我们的亲吻也驱不散那股空虚之感。同从前在我的书房里一样，她在裙子上铺开一方餐巾，把几把小指甲刷和锉刀摆在餐巾上，一本正经地修起指甲来。我痛苦极了，那个守在窗前的老人的幻影缠住了我。

===Original===
The next day Juliette announced that she had to leave that very evening.

“Ah! What a misfortune, my dear! I have forgotten! Quick, quick, get me a carriage. Oh! what a misfortune!”

I made no effort to detain her. Sunk in my chair motionless, gloomy, my head buried in my hands, I sat throughout the preparations for her departure without uttering a single word or making a single request. Juliette went out, returned, folding her gowns, arranging her dressing case, locking her trunks; I heard nothing, saw nothing, knew nothing. Men came in; their heavy steps caused the floor to creak. I understood that they were taking the trunks away. Juliette sat on my lap.

“My poor little dear,” she cried, “you suffer because I leave so soon. You should not feel hurt﻿ ﻿… be sensible. Besides, I’ll come back shortly and stay a long time. Don’t act so. I’ll come back. I promise you. I’ll bring Spy along. I’ll also bring a horse to ride on, yes? You’ll see how well your little wifie rides on horseback. Now embrace me, my Jean! Why don’t you embrace me? Come on, Jean! Goodbye! I adore you! Goodbye!”

===Chinese===
第二天，朱丽叶特宣布她当天晚上就得动身。

“啊！真是不巧，亲爱的！我忘了！快，快，给我雇辆车。啊！真是不巧！”

我没有想法子留她。我一动不动地陷在椅子里，阴沉沉的，头埋在两只手中；她动身的种种准备，我从头到尾坐在那儿，一句话不说，一个请求也不提。朱丽叶特出去了，又回来，叠她的裙子，收拾她的梳妆盒，锁她的箱子；我什么也没听见，什么也没看见，什么也不知道。男人们进来了；沉重的脚步踩得地板咯吱作响。我明白，他们在搬箱子了。朱丽叶特坐到我的膝盖上。

“我可怜的小宝贝，”她喊道，“我这么快就走，你伤心了。别这样难受……要懂事。再说，我很快就会回来，住上好长一阵子呢。别这样。我会回来的。我向你保证。我把斯派也带来。我还带一匹马来骑，好不好？你瞧瞧你的小媳妇儿骑马骑得多好。来抱抱我，我的让！你怎么不抱我？来呀，让！再见！我爱你！再见！”

===Original===
It was growing dark when Mother Le Gannec came into my bedroom. She lit the lamp and gently approached.

“Friend Mintié! Friend Mintié!”

I lifted my eyes; she was so sad, there breathed such merciful pity from her that I threw myself into her arms.

“Ah! Mother Le Gannec! Mother Le Gannec!” I sobbed. “That is what is killing me!”

Mother Le Gannec murmured:

“Friend Mintié, why don’t you pray to the merciful Lord? That will relieve you.”

===Chinese===
天擦黑的时候，勒加内克大妈走进我的卧室。她点上灯，轻轻地走近来。

“我的曼蒂埃朋友！我的曼蒂埃朋友！”

我抬起眼睛；她是那么忧伤，周身散出那么一种慈悲的怜悯，我不由得扑进了她的怀里。

“啊！勒加内克大妈！勒加内克大妈！”我呜咽着，“就是这个在要我的命啊！”

勒加内克大妈低声说：

“我的曼蒂埃朋友，你怎么不向慈悲的天主祈祷呢？那会让你好受些的。”

## V / 五

===Original===
I wish I did not have to continue this story. I wish I could stop here.﻿ ﻿… Ah, how I wish I could do that! At the thought that I am about to disclose so much ignominy, my courage fails me, I blush for shame, a feeling of cowardice instantly seizes me and agitates the pen in my hand.﻿ ﻿… And I sue for mercy from myself.﻿ ﻿… Alas! I must clamber to the top of this ascending, sorrowful Golgotha, even though my flesh be torn to bleeding pieces, even though my living body be broken against the rocks and stones! Sins like mine, which I am not trying to justify by hereditary defects or by the pernicious effects of an education so contrary to my nature, call for terrible atonement, and the atonement which I have chosen is a public confession of my life.

I say to myself that merciful and noble hearts will think kindly of my self-imposed humiliation and I also say to myself that my example will perhaps serve as a lesson to others.﻿ ﻿… Even if there were only one young man who, on the verge of falling, should happen to read these pages and feel so horrified and so disgusted as to be forever saved from evil, it seems to me that the salvation of his soul would signify the beginning of the redemption of my own. And then again, I hope, although I no longer believe in God, I hope that in the depth of those sanctuaries of peace where in the silence of soul-redeeming nights there rises to heaven the sad and soothing chant of those who pray for the dead, I hope that there, too, I may be granted my share of compassion and of Christian forgiveness.

I had an income of twenty-two thousand francs; furthermore, I was certain that by doing literary work I could earn an equal sum, at least. Nothing seemed difficult to me, the path lay straight before me without a single obstacle, I had but to march on.﻿ ﻿… My shyness, my fears, my doubts, exhaustingly painful efforts, spiritual agonies oh, those things no longer mattered! A novel, two novels a year, a few plays for the theatre.﻿ ﻿… What did that amount to for a young man in love as I was?﻿ ﻿… Weren’t people talking about X﻿ ﻿… and Z﻿ ﻿… two hopeless and notorious idiots who in a few years amassed a large fortune?﻿ ﻿… Ideas for a novel, a comedy, a dramatic play came to me in droves﻿ ﻿… and I indicated their arrival by a broad and haughty gesture.﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===
我但愿不必把这个故事再讲下去。但愿能到此为止。……啊，我多想就此打住！一想到即将披露这许多耻辱，我的勇气便泄了，羞得满脸发热，一股怯懦攫住我，弄得我手里那支笔直打颤。……于是我只得向自己讨饶。……唉！我必须攀上这座步步升高的、凄怆的各各他（Golgotha），哪怕皮肉被撕得血淋淋地碎裂，哪怕活生生的身子在岩头石块上撞得粉身碎骨！像我这样的罪——我并不想拿遗传的缺陷、拿一种与我的天性如此相悖的教育的恶果来为自己开脱——是须以可怕的苦赎来抵偿的；而我选定的赎罪，便是把我的生平当众忏悔出来。

我对自己说，仁慈而高贵的心，会善待我自加于身的这番羞辱；我又对自己说，我的前车之辙，或许还能给别人当个教训。……哪怕只有一个年轻人，正站在堕落的边缘，碰巧读到这几页文字，惊骇厌恶之余，从此永免于罪恶，那么在我看来，他灵魂的得救，便算是我自己救赎的开端。再说，虽然我已不再信上帝，我却仍然希望——希望在那些安宁圣所的深处，在救赎灵魂的静夜寂寥之中，为亡人祈祷的人们唱起忧戚而慰人的圣歌、袅袅升上天庭的地方——我希望在那里，也能分给我一份怜悯、一份基督徒的宽恕。

我有两万两千法郎的年息；而且我确信，笔耕至少还能挣来同样一笔。没有什么在我眼里是难的，道路笔直铺展在面前，没有一处障碍，我只消往前走。……我的羞怯、我的恐惧、我的疑虑、那劳形苦心的挣扎、那精神上的熬煎——哦，这些都算不得什么了！一年一部小说、两部小说，再给剧院写几个剧本。……这对一个像我这样热恋中的年轻人又算得了什么？……人家不正在谈论X……和Z……么？两个不可救药、臭名远扬的蠢货，几年工夫就攒下一大笔家私。……小说的构思、喜剧的构思、正剧的构思，成群结队朝我涌来……我只把手一挥，傲然示意知道了它们的光临。……

===Original===
I saw myself already monopolizing all the libraries, all the theatres, all the magazines, the attention of the whole world.﻿ ﻿… In the hours when inspiration should prove slow and painful, all I would need to do would be to look at Juliette and masterpieces would come forth from her eyes as in a fairytale. I did not hesitate to demand Malterre’s departure and complete charge of Juliette’s affairs. Malterre wrote heartrending letters, begged, threatened and finally departed. Later on Jesselin, displaying his usual vaunted tact, told us that Malterre, grief-stricken, had taken a trip to Italy.

“I accompanied him as far as Marseilles,” he told us. “He wanted to kill himself and was crying all the time. You know I am not a gullible sort of a chap﻿ ﻿… but he actually made me feel sorry for him. Now really!”

And he added:

“You know. He was ready to fight you.﻿ ﻿… It was his friend, Monsieur Lirat, who kept him from doing that.﻿ ﻿… I, too, dissuaded him from it because I believe only in a duel to death.”

Juliette listened to all these details silently and with apparent indifference. From time to time she drew her tongue across her lips, and in her eyes there was something resembling a reflection of inner joy. Was she thinking of Malterre? Was she happy to learn that someone was suffering on account of her? Alas! I was no longer in a position to ask myself such questions.

===Chinese===
我仿佛已经看见自己独占了所有的书局、所有的剧院、所有的杂志，独占了全世界的注目。……遇到灵感迟滞而痛苦的时辰，我只消看一看朱丽叶特，杰作便会像童话里那样，从她的眼睛里涌出来。我毫不迟疑地要求马尔特尔走人，把朱丽叶特的事务整个儿接掌过来。马尔特尔写来一封封令人心碎的信，哀求，威胁，末了还是走了。后来热瑟兰（Jesselin）凭着素来自诩的那份圆滑，告诉我们说，马尔特尔伤心欲绝，到意大利旅行去了。

“我一路把他送到马赛，”他对我们说，“他一路想寻死，哭个没完。您知道，我可不是个好糊弄的人……可他竟真叫我替他难受起来。您说说看！”

接着他又补上一句：

“您知道。他本来是要同您决斗的。……是他的朋友利拉先生劝住了他。……我也劝过他别打这个主意，因为我只认拼到死的决斗。”

这些细节，朱丽叶特一声不响地听着，面上漠然。她不时伸舌头舔一舔嘴唇，眼睛里有点什么东西，像是心底欢喜的反光。她在想马尔特尔么？得知有人正为她受苦，她觉得快意么？唉！这样的问题，我已经没有资格向自己提出了。

===Original===
A new life began.

I did not like the apartment where Juliette lived; there were in her house neighbors whom I did not like, and above all the apartment concealed memories which I thought it more convenient to forget. For fear that my plans might not be agreeable to Juliette I did not dare to reveal them too abruptly, but at the very first words I said about the matter she grew enthusiastic.

“Yes! yes!” she cried out with joy. “I have been thinking of it myself, dearie. And do you know of what else I have been thinking? Guess, guess quickly, what your little wifie has been thinking of?”

She placed both hands on my shoulders, and smiling:

“Don’t you know?﻿ ﻿… Really you don’t?﻿ ﻿… Well! she has been thinking of having you come and live with her.﻿ ﻿… Oh! It’ll be so nice to have a pretty little apartment where we shall be alone, just the two of us, to love each other, isn’t that right, my Jean?﻿ ﻿… You’ll work and I’ll sit right next to you and do some needle work without making a stir, and from time to time, I’ll embrace you to inspire you with great ideas.﻿ ﻿… You shall see, my dear, whether I am a good housekeeper or not, whether I can take care of all your little matters.﻿ ﻿… In the first place, I’ll arrange your things in the bureau. Every morning you will find a fresh flower on it.﻿ ﻿… Then Spy will also have a nice little niche, all new, with red topknots.﻿ ﻿… And then we shall hardly go out at all.﻿ ﻿… And we’ll sleep as late as we wish.﻿ ﻿… And then﻿ ﻿… and then.﻿ ﻿… Oh, how wonderful it will be!﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
一种新的生活开始了。

我不喜欢朱丽叶特住的那套公寓；她那幢房子里有几个我不喜欢的邻居，尤其那套公寓里还藏着一些旧事，我以为还是忘掉为妙。我生怕自己的打算不合朱丽叶特的心意，不敢贸然说破；可这件事我刚一开口，她就兴高采烈起来。

“对！对！”她欢喜得叫出声来，“我自个儿也正想着这个呢，亲爱的。你可知道我还想到了什么？猜猜，快猜猜，你的小媳妇儿在想些什么？”

她把双手搭上我的肩头，含笑说道：

“你不知道？……真的不知道？……嘿！她一直在想，要你搬来和她一起住。……噢！有一套漂亮的小公寓，只咱们俩待着，相亲相爱，那该多好，是不是，我的让？……你干活儿，我就坐在你旁边，做点针线，不出一点声响；过一阵子，我就来抱抱你，好让你生出伟大的灵感。……你等着瞧吧，亲爱的，看我会不会当家，能不能把你那些零零碎碎的事都照料得好好的。……头一件，我要把你的东西归置到书桌上。每天早晨，你都会在桌上发现一朵鲜花。……然后，斯派（Spy）也会有一个漂亮的小窝，全新的，缀着红绒球。……往后咱们几乎不出门。……爱睡到多晚，就睡到多晚。……然后呢……然后呢。……噢，那该多美呀！……”

===Original===
Then getting serious again, she said in a grave voice:

“Not to mention the fact that it will be a good deal cheaper. Just about half!”

We rented an apartment on the Rue de Balzac and we busily fixed it up. That was an important task. We were shopping the whole day, examining rugs, choosing hangings, discussing arrangements and estimating things. Juliette would have liked to buy everything she saw, but she professed a preference for elaborate furniture, for loud-colored draperies and heavy embroidery. The glitter of new gold, the dazzling effect of harsh colors attracted, fascinated her. Whenever I ventured to remark something to her, she would say at once:

“How do men come to know about these things?﻿ ﻿… Women know better.”

She was obdurate in her desire to buy a kind of Arabian chest, frightfully daubed up, set with mother-of-pearl, ivory imitation stones, and of immense size.

===Chinese===
说着她又板起面孔，拿出一本正经的嗓音说：

“且不说这样一来还能省一大笔呢。差不多省一半！”

我们在巴尔扎克街（Rue de Balzac）租下一套公寓，风风火火地布置起来。这可是件大事。我们整天在外面采买，看地毯，挑帷幔，商量陈设，估量价钱。朱丽叶特恨不得把看见的东西统统买下，不过她自称偏爱繁复的家具、艳色的帘幔和厚重的绣品。新金的闪光，刺目颜色那晃眼的效果，引着她，迷着她。每逢我斗胆提一句意见，她立刻就答：

“男人家哪里懂得这些？……女人家才在行呢。”

她铁了心要买一只阿拉伯式的箱子，涂得花里胡哨，镶着珠母、象牙和假宝石，块头大得吓人。

===Original===
“You can see for yourself that it’s too large, that it won’t get into our house at all,” I said to her.

“Do you really think so? Well how about sawing off the legs, dearie?”

And more than twenty times during the day she stopped in the middle of her conversation to ask me:

“Well, do you really think it is too large?﻿ ﻿… That beautiful chest I mean.”

===Chinese===
“你自己也看得出来，它太大了，根本进不了咱们家的门，”我对她说。

“你真这么想？那把箱子腿锯掉，不就行了么，亲爱的？”

一天里总有二十好几回，她话说一半便停住，问我：

“哎，你真觉得它太大么？……我是说那只漂亮的箱子。”

===Original===
In the carriage, as soon as she got in, Juliette nestled close to me, offered me her lips, smothered me with caresses, happy, radiant.

“Oh! you naughty boy, who never said a word to me, and who stood just looking at me, with his sad eyes﻿ ﻿… yes, your beautiful sad eyes that I love﻿ ﻿… you naughty!﻿ ﻿… I had to start it all myself!﻿ ﻿… hadn’t I?﻿ ﻿… otherwise you would have never dared, would you?﻿ ﻿… Were you afraid of me, tell me? Do you remember when you took me in your arms, that evening? I did not know where I was, I could no longer see anything.﻿ ﻿… My throat, my chest felt as though I had swallowed something very hot﻿ ﻿… isn’t that funny.﻿ ﻿… I thought I was going to die﻿ ﻿… burned by you.﻿ ﻿… It was so sweet, so sweet!﻿ ﻿… Why, I have loved you since the first day we met.﻿ ﻿… No, I was in love with you before.﻿ ﻿… Ah, you are laughing!﻿ ﻿… You don’t believe then that you can love someone without knowing or seeing him?﻿ ﻿… Well I do!﻿ ﻿… I am sure of it!﻿ ﻿…”

My heart was beating so fast, these words were so new to me, that I could not find anything to say in reply; I was choking with happiness. All I could do was to clasp Juliette in my arms, mutter some inarticulate words and weep with joy. Suddenly she became thoughtful, the furrow on her forehead deepened, she withdrew her hand from mine. I was afraid I had offended her:

===Chinese===
上了马车，朱丽叶特一坐进来便偎到我身边，送过嘴唇来，把我爱抚得透不过气，欢喜得满面生辉。

“噢！你这个坏东西，从来不肯对我先开一句口，只会站在那儿瞧着我，用你那双忧伤的眼睛……对，你那双好看的、招人疼的忧伤眼睛……你真坏！……什么事都得我自己先来！……是不是？……不然你永远不敢，对不对？……你是怕我，你说呀？你还记得那天晚上你把我搂进怀里么？我简直不知道自己身在何处，什么也看不见了。……我的喉咙、我的胸口，好像吞下了什么滚烫的东西……好笑不好笑。……我以为自己要死了……叫你烧死了。……那滋味真甜，真甜！……嘿，打咱们头一回见面，我就爱上你了。……不，还没见面我就爱上你了。……啊，你笑！……你不信一个人能爱上一个素不相识、素未谋面的人么？……我可相信！……我敢肯定！……”

我的心跳得那样急，这些话于我又这样新鲜，我竟找不出一句话来回答；幸福把我噎住了。我只有把朱丽叶特紧紧搂在怀里，含含糊糊咕哝几个字，欢喜得直落泪。忽然她若有所思起来，额上的纹路深了，把手从我的手里抽走。我怕是冒犯了她：

===Original===
“What’s the matter, my Juliette?” I asked her. “Why do you look so?﻿ ﻿… Have I hurt you?”

And Juliette, disconsolate, said with a sigh:

“The corner-buffet, my dear!﻿ ﻿… The corner-buffet for the parlor which we have entirely forgotten.”

She quickly passed from laughter, from kisses to sudden gravity, mingled words of endearment with ceiling measurements, confused love with tapestry.﻿ ﻿… It was delightful.

===Chinese===
“怎么啦，我的朱丽叶特？”我问她。“你干吗这副神气？……我伤了你的心么？”

朱丽叶特愁眉不展，叹了口气说：

“角柜，亲爱的！……客厅用的角柜，咱们把它忘了个干净。”

她一会儿欢笑，一会儿亲吻，转眼又陡然正经起来，情话里夹着量天花板，恩恩爱爱同挂毯纠缠成一团。……真是有趣极了。

===Original===
In our room, in the evening, all this pretty childishness disappeared. Love stamped upon the face of Juliette something austere, deliberate and ferocious which I could not explain; it changed her entirely. She was not depraved; on the contrary, her passion showed itself to be strong and normal, and in her caresses there was awe-inspiring nobility and courage. Her body trembled as if in terrible labor.

My happiness lasted but a short time.﻿ ﻿… My happiness!﻿ ﻿… It is really remarkable that never, never have I been permitted to enjoy anything fully, and that invariably anxiety came to disturb the brief periods of my happiness. Defenseless and powerless against suffering, not sure of myself and timid in the hours of happiness﻿—such I have been all my life. Is it a tendency peculiar to my nature? A strange perversion of my sensibilities?﻿ ﻿… Or is it rather that happiness in my own case as well as in the case of everybody else is really deceptive, and that it is nothing but a more tormenting and more refined form of universal suffering?﻿ ﻿…

Now this for example.﻿ ﻿… The faint glimmer of the night-lamp flickers feebly upon the curtains and the furniture; Juliette is asleep, early in the morning, the morning after our first night. One of her arms, bare, rests upon the sheet; the other, also bare, is gracefully coiled up under her nape. All around her face﻿—which reflects the pallid light of the bed, a face which looks like that of a murdered person, with eyes encircled by dark rings﻿—her loose black hair is scattered, sinuous and flowing like waves! I contemplate her eagerly.﻿ ﻿… She is sleeping close to me, with a deep calm sleep, like a child. And for the first time possession occasions no regret, no disgust in me; for the first time I am able to look at a woman who has just given herself to me. I cannot express my feelings at this moment. What I feel is something indefinable, something exceedingly sweet and at the same time very grave and holy, a sort of religious ecstasy similar to the one which I experienced at the time of my first communion.﻿ ﻿… I recognize the same mystic transport, the same great and sacred awe; it is like another revelation of God taking place in the transplendent light of my soul.﻿ ﻿… It seems to me that God has come down to me for the second time.﻿ ﻿… She sleeps, in the silence of the room, with her mouth half-open, her nostrils motionless; she sleeps with a sleep so gentle that I cannot even hear her breathing.﻿ ﻿… A flower on the mantelpiece is there, withering, and a whiff of its dying fragrance reaches me. I can’t hear Juliette at all, she is only asleep, she is breathing, she is alive and yet I can’t hear her. I move nearer to her and gently bend over her, almost touching her with my lips, and in an almost inaudible voice I call her.

===Chinese===
到了晚上，在我们自己房间里，这些可爱的孩子气便都消失了。爱情在朱丽叶特的脸上烙下一种冷峻的、蓄意的、凶悍的东西，我解释不来；爱情把她整个人都改变了。她并不堕落；恰恰相反，她的情欲显得强健而正常，她的爱抚里有一种令人敬畏的高贵与大胆。她的身子颤动着，仿佛在经历一场骇人的临盆。

我的幸福只维系了很短的时日。……我的幸福！……真是咄咄怪事：从来不曾、从来不曾容我把一样东西安然受用到底，总有一份惶恐来搅扰我幸福的短促时辰。面对苦难全无抵御、无能为力，面对幸福又心虚胆怯——我一辈子就是这样。这是我的天性独有的倾向么？是我的感受力的一种古怪乖张么？……还是说，幸福这个东西，在我身上正如在人人身上一样，本来就靠不住，它不过是普遍苦难的一种更折磨人、更精致的形态？……

就说眼下这一刻吧。……夜灯微弱的光晕，淡淡地闪动在帷幔和家具上；朱丽叶特睡熟了，时当清晨，是我们头一夜之后的那个清晨。她的一条胳膊裸着，搁在床单上；另一条也裸着，柔婉地蜷在颈后。她的脸四周——那张脸映着床铺惨白的光，一张像被谋害者的脸，眼圈乌青——披散的黑发漫开来，曲曲折折，像波浪一样流泻！我贪婪地凝视她。……她睡在我身边，睡得深沉、安稳，像个孩子。头一回，占有没有在我心里引起悔恨，没有引起嫌恶；头一回，我能够端详一个刚刚把自己交给我的女人。此刻的心情我无从表达。我所感到的是一种说不出的东西，一种极其甜美的东西，同时又极庄重、极神圣，一种近乎宗教性的狂喜，同我初领圣体那回的体验相仿。……我认出了同样的神秘震颤，同样伟大而神圣的敬畏；仿佛上帝在我的灵魂那澄澈超绝的光里，又显现了一回。……我觉得，上帝第二次降临到了我身上。……她睡着，在房间的静寂里，嘴半张着，鼻翼纹丝不动；她睡得那样轻柔，连呼吸声我也听不见。……壁炉台上有一朵花，正在凋谢，一缕垂死的芬芳飘到我这里。朱丽叶特的声息我全然听不见，她只是睡着，她在呼吸，她活着，可我听不见她。我朝她挪近些，轻轻俯下身去，嘴唇几乎碰到她，用几乎听不见的声音唤她。

===Original===
“Juliette!”

Juliette does not stir. But I feel her breath, fainter than that of the flower, her breath always so fresh, with which at this moment there is mingled, like a waft of warmth, her fragrant breath which blends with an imperceptible odor of decay.

“Juliette!”

Juliette does not stir. But the sheet which follows the curves of her body, showing the shape of her limbs, loosens itself into a rigid crease, and the sheet looks to me like a shroud. And the thought of death suddenly comes to my mind and lingers there. I begin to be afraid that Juliette is dead.

===Chinese===
“朱丽叶特！”

朱丽叶特一动不动。可我觉出她的呼吸，比那朵花的气息还要微弱；她的呼吸向来那样新鲜，此刻其中却掺进一缕暖气似的馨息，那馨息里又融着一丝难以察觉的腐味。

“朱丽叶特！”

朱丽叶特一动不动。可是那床贴着她身体的曲线、显出她四肢形状的床单，松脱开来，凝成一道僵直的折痕；那床单在我眼里，就像一领裹尸布。死亡的念头突然涌上心头，赖着不走了。我开始害怕朱丽叶特已经死了。

===Original===
“Juliette!”

Juliette does not stir. My whole being is now plunged into a frenzy of fear, and while in my ears the distant knell resounds, around the bed I see the light of a thousand funeral tapers trembling under the vibrations of a de profundis prayer. My hair stands on end, my teeth chatter and I shout, I shout:

“Juliette! Juliette!”

At last Juliette moves her head, heaves a sigh and murmurs, as if in a dream:

“Jean!﻿ ﻿… My Jean!”

===Chinese===
“朱丽叶特！”

朱丽叶特一动不动。我整个的人陷进了疯狂的恐惧；耳中鸣响着远处的丧钟，床榻四周，我看见千支丧烛的光，在一篇《我自深处向你呼求》（De profundis）祷文的震颤里摇曳。我毛发倒竖，牙齿打战，我喊，我喊：

“朱丽叶特！朱丽叶特！”

朱丽叶特终于动了动头，长叹一声，像说梦话似的喃喃道：

“让！……我的让！”

===Original===
Forcefully I grasp her into my arms as if to defend her against someone; I draw her toward me and trembling, with my blood running cold, I beg her:

“Juliette!﻿ ﻿… My own Juliette﻿ ﻿… don’t sleep.﻿ ﻿… Oh, please don’t sleep!﻿ ﻿… You frighten me!﻿ ﻿… Let me see your eyes; talk to me, talk to me!﻿ ﻿… And pinch me, pinch yourself, too, pinch me hard.﻿ ﻿… But don’t sleep anymore, please.﻿ ﻿…”

She cuddles into my arms, whispers some unintelligible words and falls asleep again, her head hanging on my shoulder.﻿ ﻿… But the apparition of death, stronger than the awakening of love, persists, and although I feel the regular beating of Juliette’s heart against my own, it does not vanish until day.

===Chinese===
我猛地把她搂进怀里，仿佛要护着她抵挡什么人；我把她拽向自己，浑身发抖，血都凉了，恳求她：

“朱丽叶特！……我的朱丽叶特……别睡了。……噢，求求你，别睡了！……你吓着我了！……让我看看你的眼睛；跟我说话，跟我说话呀！……掐掐我，也掐掐你自己，使劲掐我。……可别再睡了，求求你。……”

她偎进我怀里，咕哝了几句听不清的话，头垂在我肩上，又睡着了。……可是死亡的幻影，比爱情的苏醒更有力量，赖着不走；虽然我觉出朱丽叶特的心正贴着我的心规则地跳动，那幻影却直到天亮才消散。

===Original===
How often since that time, when with her, I have felt the frigid touch of death in her fiery kisses!﻿ ﻿… And how often in the midst of rapture there appeared to me the sudden and capering image of the singer at the Bouffes!﻿ ﻿… How many times did his lustful laugh drown the ardent words of Juliette!﻿ ﻿… How often I have heard him say to me, while his image kept leaping above me: “Go ahead, glut yourself upon this imbecile body, upon this unclean body which I defiled!﻿ ﻿… Go on!﻿ ﻿… Go on!﻿ ﻿… wherever you touch your lips you will breathe the impure odor of my own; wherever your caresses may wander upon this body of a prostitute they will encounter the filthy marks of my own manhandling.﻿ ﻿… Go ahead, wash her, your Juliette, wash her in the lustral water of your love, cleanse her with the acid of your mouth.﻿ ﻿… Strip off her skin with your teeth, if you will; you will efface nothing, never, because the mark of infamy with which I have branded her is ineffaceable.”

And I often had a passionate desire to question Juliette about this singer whose vision haunted me so much. But I had not the courage. I contented myself with trying to get at the truth in an ingenious, roundabout way: often, in the midst of conversation I would mention a name unexpectedly, hoping, yes hoping that Juliette would be a little put out by it, that she would blush, would feel embarrassed and would say: “Yes, that’s the one.” I thus exhausted the list of names of all the singers in all the theatres, without gaining the least evidence of perturbation in Juliette’s impenetrable attitude.

===Chinese===
打那以后，我陪着她的时候，有多少回，从她火热的吻里觉出了死亡冰冷的触感！……又有多少回，正当神魂颠倒之际，布夫剧院那个歌手的身影，突然手舞足蹈地蹦现在我眼前！……他淫荡的笑声，多少次淹没了朱丽叶特炽热的话语！……多少回我听见他对我说话，那身影在我上方不住地跳跃：“上吧，拿这具痴愚的肉体纵情享用，拿这具被我糟蹋脏了的肉体！……上呀！……上呀！……你的嘴唇碰到哪里，哪里就有我留下的秽气；你的爱抚在这娼妓身上游走到哪里，哪里就撞得见我作践过的污痕。……上吧，洗她吧，你的朱丽叶特，拿你爱情的净水洗她，拿你嘴里的酸液涮净她。……高兴的话，就用牙齿剥下她的皮；你什么也抹不掉，永远抹不掉，因为我烙在她身上的耻辱之印，是除不掉的。”

我常常起了一股热望，想就这个老缠着我的歌手盘问朱丽叶特。可我没有这份勇气。我只满足于用乖巧的迂回法子套话：常常在谈话中间，冷不丁抛出一个名字，指望——是指望朱丽叶特会有一点着慌，会脸红，会局促，会说：“对，就是他。”就这样，我把各家剧院所有歌手的名单数了个遍，也没能在朱丽叶特那张滴水不漏的神态里搅起一丝慌乱。

===Original===
It took us almost three months to install ourselves completely. The upholsterers could never get through with their work and Juliette’s caprices often called for changes that took a long time to accomplish. Every day she would come back from her shopping with new ideas about the decoration of the parlor, or the dressing room. The hangings in the bedroom had to be entirely changed three times because she did not like them.

Finally one nice morning we took possession of our apartment on the Rue de Balzac.﻿ ﻿… It was high time we did.﻿ ﻿… All this unsettled existence, this continuous hurry, these open trunks yawning like coffins, this brutal scattering of dear and intimate things, these heaps of linen, these pyramids of boxes turned upside down, these cut-up pieces of string which dragged all over, all this disorder, this chaos, this trampling underfoot of things with which are associated the dearest memories or most tender regrets, and above all this feeling of uncertainty, of terror, and the sad reflections which the act of leaving a place occasions﻿—all this made me uneasy, dejected and, must I say it, remorseful.

While Juliette was moving about, bustling amidst bundles, I was asking myself whether I had not committed some irreparable folly. Of course, I loved her.﻿ ﻿… Ah! I loved her with all the power of my soul. So far, nothing except this passion which obsessed me more and more every day, interested me at all. Still, I regretted that I had yielded so easily and quickly to an infatuation that was perhaps fraught with the gravest consequences for her and myself. I was dissatisfied with myself for not having been able to resist Juliette’s wish, expressed in such delicious fashion, that I live together with her.﻿ ﻿… Could we not love each other just as well if each of us lived separately and avoid the possible clashes over such sordid things as wallpaper, for example.

And while the splendor of all this plush and the insolence of all these gilt objects in the midst of which we were now going to live frightened me, I felt a sorrowful attachment for my own scanty furniture placed without order, for my little apartment, austere yet tranquil, and now empty﻿—an attachment one has for beloved things that are dead. But Juliette would pass by, busy, agile and charming, would embrace and kiss me on her way, and there was such a life-giving joy in her whole being, a joy so easily mingled with astonishment and childish despair at anything lost, that my morose thoughts vanished as do the night owls at sunrise.

===Chinese===
我们差不多花了三个月，才把新居完全安顿妥当。装潢匠的活儿总也收不了尾，朱丽叶特一时一个主意，常常要大费周章地返工。每天她采买回来，对客厅或是梳妆室的装饰总有新的想法。卧室的帷幔整个换了三回，就因为她不喜欢。

终于，一个晴好的早晨，我们住进了巴尔扎克街的公寓。……也早就该住进来了。……这种动荡不定的日子，这种连日的奔波，这些敞着口打哈欠、活像棺材的箱子，这种把贴身爱物横冲直撞乱撒一气的粗暴，这一堆堆的衬衣床单，这一座座底朝天的匣子，这满地拖挂的断绳残线，这种种狼藉、这种种混乱，这些被踩在脚下的、系着最珍贵记忆或最温软追怀的东西，尤其是那种没着没落的惶恐，还有搬离一处居所勾起的愁绪——这一切叫我心神不定，叫我颓丧，而且——我不得不说——叫我悔愧。

朱丽叶特在包裹箱笼中间奔忙张罗，我却在一旁自问：我是不是干下了一桩无可挽回的荒唐事。当然，我爱她。……啊！我用灵魂的全部力量爱着她。到那时为止，除了这桩一天比一天更缠住我的情欲，再没有什么能叫我上心。话虽如此，我仍后悔自己太轻易、太迅疾地屈从了一时的痴迷，它说不定给我和她都埋下了最严重的后果。朱丽叶特以那样娇美可人的方式提出要我同她一起生活，我竟没能抵住，我对自己不满。……倘若各住各的，难道就不能照样相爱，还省得为糊墙纸之类卑琐物件磕磕碰碰么？

我们今后就要活在一片长毛绒的豪华和这些镀金物件的傲气之中，这叫我心惊；而同时，我又对自己那几件散乱无序的简陋家具、对我那套俭朴而安宁、如今空了的小公寓，生出一种悲凉的眷恋——就像人们对已经亡故的心爱之物怀有的那种眷恋。可是朱丽叶特跑来跑去，忙碌、轻快、娇俏，路过便搂住我亲一亲；她周身上下有一股那样蓬勃的欢劲，一种动辄就为丢了什么东西而惊讶、而孩子气地撒气的欢劲，于是我那些阴郁的念头，便像日出时的夜枭，四散而逝了。

===Original===
Ah! the happy days that followed our moving from the Rue Saint Petersbourg!﻿ ﻿… First we had to test every piece of furniture down to the smallest details. Juliette sat on every divan, lounge and sofa, causing the springs to creak.

“You try it also, my dear,” she would say to me. She examined every piece of furniture, scrutinized the hangings, tried the strings of the door curtain, moved a chair to a different place, smoothed a crease in the draperies. And every instant cries of admiration, of ecstasy were heard!

Then she wanted to start the inspection of the apartment all over again with the windows closed and the lights burning, in order to see the effect produced at night, never tiring of examining a thing more than once, running from one room to another, marking down every defect on a piece of paper. Then it was the wardrobe where she put her linen and mine with meticulous care and elaborate nicety and the consummate skill of a stall keeper. I chided her for assigning to me the better scentbags.

“No! no! no! I want to have a little husband who uses perfume!”

===Chinese===
啊！搬出圣彼得堡街（rue Saint-Petersbourg）之后的那些快活日子！……头一件，得把每件家具都试到最细的地方。每一张软榻、躺椅、长沙发，朱丽叶特都要坐一坐，坐得弹簧咯吱直响。

“你也来试试，亲爱的，”她总对我说。她把每件家具检视一遍，细看帷幔，扯一扯门帘的拉绳，把一把椅子挪个地方，抹平帘幔上一道褶皱。赞叹声、狂喜声，时时刻刻不绝于耳！

随后她又要关上窗、点起灯，把整套公寓从头再查验一遍，好看看夜里的效果；一件东西看多少遍也不腻，从这间屋跑到那间屋，把每一处毛病都记在一张纸上。接着轮到衣柜：她把她的衬衣和我的衬衣收进去，那份细致、那份讲究，纯熟得像个摊贩。她把较好的香袋都分给我，我还说了她几句。

“不嘛！不嘛！不嘛！我就要有个洒香水的小丈夫！”

===Original===
Of her old furniture and old knickknacks, Juliette had kept only the terra cotta statue of Love which again took its place of honor on the mantelpiece in the parlor. I, on the other hand, had brought over only my books and two very beautiful sketches by Lirat which I thought it a duty to hang up in my study. Scandalized, Juliette cried with indignation:

“What are you doing there, my dear?﻿ ﻿… Such horrible things in our new apartment!﻿ ﻿… Please put these horrible things away somewhere! Oh, put them away!”

“My dear Juliette,” I answered somewhat provoked. “You have kept your terra cotta statue of Love, not so?”

“Certainly I have.﻿ ﻿… But what has that to do with this?﻿ ﻿… My terra cotta statue is very, very lovely. Whereas that thing there﻿ ﻿… why really!﻿ ﻿… And then it’s improper!﻿ ﻿… Besides, every time I look at the paintings of that fool Lirat I feel a pain in my stomach.”

Before that I used to have the courage of my artistic convictions and I defended them with fire. But now it seemed puerile on my part to engage in a discussion of art with Juliette, so I contented myself with hiding the pictures inside a press without much regret.

===Chinese===
旧家具和旧摆设里，朱丽叶特只留下那尊陶土的爱神像；它又荣登宝座，端坐在客厅的壁炉台上。我呢，只搬来了我的书，还有利拉的两幅很美的素描，我觉得有义务把它们挂进我的书房。朱丽叶特大惊小怪，愤愤地嚷道：

“你这是干什么呀，亲爱的？……这么吓人的东西，竟挂进咱们的新家！……快把这些吓人的东西收到哪儿去吧！噢，收起来吧！”

“亲爱的朱丽叶特，”我有点上火地答道，“你不也留下了你那尊陶土的爱神像么？”

“留下是留下了。……可这跟那有什么相干？……我的陶土像美得很，美得很。你那玩意儿……哼，真是的！……再说它还不成体统！……还有，我一瞧那个蠢货利拉的画，胃口就疼。”

换了从前，我对自己的艺术信念是有胆气坚持的，还会起劲地捍卫。可如今，同朱丽叶特讨论艺术，在我看来未免孩子气，于是我把画塞进一口柜橱，倒也没觉着怎么可惜。

===Original===
Finally the day arrived when everything was in admirable order; everything in its place, the smallest objects resting smartly on the tables, console tables, windows; the stands decorated with large leafed plants; the books in the library within reach; Spy in his new niche and flowers everywhere.﻿ ﻿… Nothing was missing, nothing, not even a rose, whose stem bathed in a long thin glass vase standing on my desk. Juliette was radiant, triumphant; she repeated without end:

“Look, look again how well your little wife has worked!”

And resting her head on my shoulder with a tender look in her eyes and a genuinely agitated voice, she murmured:

“Oh, my adored Jean, at last we are in our own home, our own home, just think of it!﻿ ﻿… How happy we shall be here, in our pretty nest!﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
终于到了那样一天：一切都布置得妥妥帖帖；样样各就其位，最细小的物件也款款地安卧在桌上、独柱小桌上、窗台上；花架上缀着阔叶的绿植；书房里的书伸手可及；斯派待在它的新窝里；处处是鲜花。……什么也不缺，什么也不缺，连一朵玫瑰也不缺，花茎浸在我书桌上一只细长的玻璃瓶里。朱丽叶特满面生辉，得意洋洋；她没完没了地重复：

“瞧呀，再瞧呀，你的小媳妇儿干得多漂亮！”

她把头靠在我肩上，眼里含着柔情，声音也真动了心，喃喃地说：

“噢，我心爱的让，咱们总算有了自己的家，自己的家，你想想吧！……咱们在这个漂亮的小窝里，会多么幸福呀！……”

===Original===
The next morning Juliette said to me:

“It has been a long time since you saw Monsieur Lirat. I don’t want him to think that I keep you from visiting him.”

It was true, nevertheless. It really seemed as if for the last five months, I had forgotten all about poor Lirat. But had I really forgotten him? Alas no!﻿ ﻿… Shame kept me from going to him.﻿ ﻿… Shame alone estranged me from him.﻿ ﻿… I assure you that I would have never hesitated to announce to the whole world: “I am Juliette’s lover!”; but I had not the courage to utter these words in Lirat’s presence.

At first I had a notion to confess all to him, no matter what happened to our friendship.﻿ ﻿… I would say to myself: “all right, tomorrow I am going to see Lirat”﻿ ﻿… I would make up my mind firmly.﻿ ﻿… And the next day: “Not now﻿ ﻿… there is nothing pressing﻿ ﻿… tomorrow!﻿ ﻿…” Tomorrow, always tomorrow!﻿ ﻿… And days, weeks, months passed.﻿ ﻿… Tomorrow!

Now that he had been told all about these things by Malterre, who even before my departure used to come and make his sofa groan, how could I broach the subject to him?﻿ ﻿… What could I say to him?﻿ ﻿… How endure his look, his contempt, his anger.﻿ ﻿… His anger, perhaps!﻿ ﻿… But his contempt, his terrible silence, the disconcerting sneer which I already saw taking shape at the corner of his mouth.﻿ ﻿… No, no, really I did not dare!﻿ ﻿… To try to mollify him, to take his hand, to ask his forgiveness for my lack of confidence in him, to appeal to the generosity of his heart!﻿ ﻿… No! It would ill become me to assume such a part, and then Lirat with just one word could throw a damper on me and stop my effusion.﻿ ﻿… What’s the use!﻿ ﻿… Each day that passed separated us further, estranged us from each other more and more﻿ ﻿… a few more months and there would no longer be any Lirat to reckon with in my life!﻿ ﻿… I should prefer that rather than cross his threshold and face him in person.﻿ ﻿… I replied to Juliette:

===Chinese===
第二天早晨，朱丽叶特对我说：

“你很久没去看利拉先生了。我不想让他以为，是我拦着不叫你上门。”

话说得也是。这五个月来，我真的好像把可怜的利拉忘了个干净。可我真忘了他么？唉，没有！……是羞耻拦着我，不叫我去找他。……单单一个羞耻，就把我从他身边隔开了。……我向您担保，“我是朱丽叶特的情人！”这句话，向全世界宣布我也决不迟疑；可当着利拉的面，这几个字我就是没有勇气说出口。

起初我还打算向他统统招认，管它我们的友谊落个什么下场。……我对自己说：“好，明天就去看利拉。”……下决心下得斩钉截铁。……可到了第二天：“先不忙……没什么要紧的……明天吧！……”明天，永远是明天！……于是一天一天、一星期一星期、一个月一个月地过去。……明天！

如今这些事，马尔特尔早都告诉他了——早在我搬走之前，那人就常来，把他的沙发压得吱吱呻吟——这种情形教我怎么向他开口？……我对他说什么好呢？……他的目光、他的轻蔑、他的愤怒，我怎么受得住。……愤怒，倒也罢了！……可他的轻蔑、他那可怕的沉默、那撇我已经看见在他嘴角边成形的、叫人进退失据的讥诮。……不，不，我真是不敢！……去温存他，去握他的手，去求他饶恕我对他的不信任，去乞援于他心胸的大度！……不行！这样一个角色，我扮演不来；况且利拉只消一句话，就能兜头浇我一盆冷水，堵死我的倾吐。……何苦呢！……过去一天，我们就隔远一分，彼此越来越生分……再过几个月，我的生活里就再没有一个利拉可数了！……我宁可这样，也不肯跨进他的门槛、去当面见他。……我回答朱丽叶特：

===Original===
“Lirat?﻿ ﻿… Oh yes.﻿ ﻿… I think I’ll do that some of these days!”

“No, no!” insisted Juliette.﻿ ﻿… “Today! You know him, you know how mean he is. God knows how many ugly things he must have said about us!”

I had to make up my mind to see him. From the Rue de Balzac to Rodrigue Place is but a short distance. To postpone as long as possible the moment of this painful interview I made a long detour on my way, walking as far as the shop district of the Saint Honoré suburb. And I was thinking to myself: “Suppose I don’t go to see Lirat at all. I can tell her, when I come back, that we have quarrelled, and I can invent some sort of a story that will forever relieve me of the necessity of this visit.” I felt ashamed of this boyish thought.﻿ ﻿… Then I hoped that Lirat was not at home! With what joy could I then roll up my card into a tube and slip it through the keyhole! Comforted by this thought I at last turned in the direction of Rodrigue Place and stopped in front of the door of the studio﻿—and this door seemed to fill me with fear. Still I rapped at it and presently a voice, Lirat’s voice, called:

===Chinese===
“利拉？……噢，是啊。……这几天里我想我会去的！”

“不，不！”朱丽叶特执意道。……“今天就去！你了解他，知道他那个人多刻薄。天知道他说过咱们多少难听话了！”

我只得横下心去见他。从巴尔扎克街到罗德里格斯广场不过几步路。为了把这难堪的一晤尽量往后拖，我绕了个大弯，一直步行到圣奥诺雷郊区的商铺区。我一路想：“干脆别去见利拉了。回来就对她说，我们闹翻了，再编出一套话，叫这趟拜访从此一劳永逸地免了。”想到这样孩子气的念头，我自己都臊得慌。……接着我又指望利拉不在家！那我就欢天喜地，把名片卷成一个小筒，从钥匙孔里塞进去！靠这个念头壮着胆，我终于拐向罗德里格斯广场，在那扇画室门前站住——那扇门仿佛叫我胆战。我还是敲了门，随即里面一个声音，利拉的声音，喊道：

===Original===
“Come in!”

My heart beat furiously, a bar of fire stopped my throat﻿—I wanted to flee.﻿ ﻿…

“Come in!” the voice repeated.

I turned the door knob.

“Ah! Is that you, Mintié,” Lirat exclaimed. “Come on in.”

===Chinese===
“进来！”

我的心狂跳着，一条火棍堵住了喉咙——我想逃。……

“进来！”那声音又喊了一遍。

我转动了门把。

“啊！是你呀，曼蒂耶，”利拉嚷道。“快进来。”

===Original===
Lirat was seated at his table, writing a letter.

“May I finish this?” he said to me. “Just two more minutes and I’ll be through.”

He resumed writing. It was a relief not to feel upon myself the chill of his look. I took advantage of the fact that his back was turned to unburden my soul to him.

“I have not seen you for such a long time, my good Lirat.”

“Why, yes, my dear Mintié!”

===Chinese===
利拉坐在桌前写信。

“让我把这封写完行么？”他对我说。“再有两分钟就完了。”

他接着写下去。身上不再挨他目光的寒气，倒松了口气。我趁他背对着我的当口，向他倾吐心怀。

“好久没见着你了，我的好利拉。”

“可不是嘛，亲爱的曼蒂耶！”

===Original===
“I have moved.”

“Ah, is that so!”

“I live on the Rue de Balzac.”

“Nice place!”

I was suffocating.﻿ ﻿… I made a supreme effort to gather all my strength﻿ ﻿… but by a strange aberration I thought I would succeed better by adopting a flippant method of approach. Upon my word of honor! I railed, yes, railed at myself.

===Chinese===
“我搬家了。”

“啊，是么！”

“我住在巴尔扎克街。”

“好地段！”

我透不过气来。……我拼尽全力，做最后的努力……却鬼使神差地想岔了，以为拿轻浮的口气起个头，效果会更好些。我拿名誉起誓！我咒骂了自己，真的，咒骂了自己。

===Original===
“I have come to tell you some news which will amuse you.﻿ ﻿… Ha!﻿ ﻿… Ha!﻿ ﻿… which will amuse you﻿ ﻿… I am sure﻿ ﻿… I﻿ ﻿… I﻿ ﻿… live with Juliette.﻿ ﻿… Ha!﻿ ﻿… Ha!﻿ ﻿… with Juliette Roux﻿ ﻿… Juliette, you know.﻿ ﻿… Ha! Ha!”

“Congratulations!” He uttered this word “congratulations” in a perfectly calm, indifferent voice.﻿ ﻿… Was it possible! No hiss, no anger, no jumping at me!﻿ ﻿… Just “Congratulations!﻿ ﻿…” As one might say: “how do you expect that to interest me?” And his back bent over the table remained motionless without straightening up, without stirring!﻿ ﻿… His pen did not slip from his hand; he continued to write! What I told him just now he had known long ago.﻿ ﻿… But to hear it out of my own mouth!﻿ ﻿… I was stupefied﻿—and shall I confess it?﻿—I was wounded by the fact that the matter did not seem to affect him at all!﻿ ﻿… Lirat rose and rubbing his hands:

“Well! what’s new?” he asked.

I could not stand it any longer. I rushed toward him with tears in my eyes.

===Chinese===
“我来告诉你一桩新闻，准叫你乐。……哈！……哈！……准叫你乐……我保险……我……我……同朱丽叶特同居了。……哈！……哈！……同朱丽叶特·鲁同居了……朱丽叶特，你知道的。……哈！哈！”

“恭喜！”这个“恭喜”，他说得平静极了，淡漠极了。……这怎么可能！没有嘘声，没有动怒，没有朝我扑过来！……只有一句“恭喜！……”就像在说：“这种事，与我何干？”他俯在桌上写字的脊背一动不动，没有直起来，没有挪一挪！……笔没有从他手里滑落；他继续写着！我刚才告诉他的事，他早就知道了。……可是，听我亲口说出来！……我呆住了——而且要不要承认呢？——这件事好像全然打不动他，这反倒伤了我的心！……利拉站起身来，搓着双手：

“嘿！有什么新鲜事？”他问。

我再也忍不住了。我眼里噙着泪，朝他扑过去。

===Original===
“Listen to me,” I shouted sobbing. “Lirat, for God’s sake, listen to me.﻿ ﻿… I did not act fairly toward you.﻿ ﻿… I know it﻿ ﻿… and I ask your forgiveness.﻿ ﻿… I should have told you all.﻿ ﻿… But I did not have the courage to.﻿ ﻿… You frighten me.﻿ ﻿… And then﻿ ﻿… you remember Juliette, the one you told me about, right here﻿ ﻿… you remember﻿ ﻿… she is the one who kept me from doing that.﻿ ﻿… Do you understand?”

“My dear Mintié,” interrupted Lirat, “I did not want you to tell me anything. I am neither your father nor your confessor. Do what you please, that does not concern me in the least.”

I became excited.

“You are not my father, that is true﻿ ﻿… but you are my friend﻿ ﻿… and I owe you all the confidence in the world.﻿ ﻿… Forgive me!﻿ ﻿… Yes, I live with Juliette, and I love her and she loves me!﻿ ﻿… Is it a crime to seek a little happiness?﻿ ﻿… Juliette is not the kind of a woman you thought she was﻿ ﻿… she has been calumniated most odiously, Lirat.﻿ ﻿… She is kind and honest.﻿ ﻿… Oh, don’t smile﻿ ﻿… she is honest!﻿ ﻿… She has some childish ways about her that would touch even you, Lirat. You don’t like her because you don’t know her.﻿ ﻿… If you only knew how kind and considerate she is to me! Juliette wants me to work.﻿ ﻿… She ardently believes in me, in my ability to create.﻿ ﻿… Why it was she who sent me here to see you. I was ashamed and afraid.﻿ ﻿… Yes she made me do that! Have a little consideration for her, Lirat. Love her a little, I beg of you!”

===Chinese===
“听我说，”我抽抽噎噎地喊道，“利拉，看在上帝分上，听我说。……我待你不地道。……我知道……我求你饶我。……我本该把一切都告诉你。……可我没有这份勇气。……你叫我怕。……再说……你记得朱丽叶特么，就是你在这儿同我说起过的那一个……你记得……正是她，不叫我说出口。……你明白么？”

“亲爱的曼蒂耶，”利拉打断我，“我本就不要你告诉我什么。我既不是你的父亲，也不是你的听告解神父。你爱做什么做什么，同我毫不相干。”

我激动起来。

“你不是我的父亲，这不错……可你是我的朋友……我欠你的是全副的信任。……饶了我吧！……是的，我同朱丽叶特同居，我爱她，她也爱我！……寻一点幸福，难道是罪么？……朱丽叶特不是你心目中的那种女人……她遭人恶毒透顶的诽谤，利拉。……她善良，她正经。……噢，别笑……她正经！……她身上有几分孩子气，连你也会动心的，利拉。你不喜欢她，是因为你不了解她。……你要知道她待我多么善良、多么体贴就好了！朱丽叶特要我工作。……她热切地信我，信我的创作才能。……可不是她叫我来看你的么。我又羞又怕。……是她逼着我来的！你就体恤她一点吧，利拉。也多少爱她一点，我求求你！”

===Original===
Lirat became grave. He put his hand on my shoulder and, looking at me wistfully:

“My dear child!” he said to me in a trembling voice, “why do you tell me all that?”

“Because it is the truth, my dear Lirat!﻿ ﻿… because I love you and I want to remain your friend.﻿ ﻿… Show me that you are my friend no matter what happens﻿ ﻿… Here now, come to have dinner with us this evening, as we used to in the past, in my own house. Oh, please come!”

“No,” he said.

===Chinese===
利拉神色庄重起来。他把手搭在我肩上，凄然望着我：

“我亲爱的孩子！”他声音发颤地对我说，“你何必对我说这些呢？”

“因为是实话呀，亲爱的利拉！……因为我爱你，我想继续做你的朋友。……请你向我证明，不管出什么事，你都是我的朋友……这样吧，今晚来我们家吃晚饭，像从前那样，在我自己家里。噢，来吧，求你了！”

“不，”他说。

===Original===
And this “no” was relentless, final, curt, like a gunshot.

Lirat added:

“But you come often!﻿ ﻿… And whenever you feel like crying﻿ ﻿… the sofa is there﻿ ﻿… you know.﻿ ﻿… The tears of poor devils are quite known to it.”

When the door was shut behind me, it seemed that something huge and heavy had closed itself upon my past, that walls higher than the sky and darker than the night had separated me forever from my decent life, from my dreams of art. There was anguish in my whole being.﻿ ﻿… For a minute I stood there, stupefied, with swinging arms, with eyes inordinately distended, staring at that prophetic door behind which something had just come to a close, something had just died.

===Chinese===
而这个“不”字，冷酷，决绝，干脆，像一声枪响。

利拉又补了一句：

“不过你常来！……什么时候想哭了……沙发就在那儿……你是知道的。……可怜虫的眼泪，它见得多了。”

门在我身后关上时，我仿佛觉得有什么庞大而沉重的东西，在我的往世上阖拢了；一堵堵比天还高、比夜还黑的墙，把我永远隔在了正派日子和艺术梦想之外。我周身一阵苦痛。……有一分钟，我呆立在那里，双臂垂荡，眼睛瞪得老大，直勾勾盯着那扇先知一般的门——门后面，有什么刚刚闭合了，有什么刚刚死了。

## VI / 六

===Original===
Juliette was not long in wearying of this beautiful apartment where she had promised herself so much peace and happiness. Having arranged her wardrobes and put her knickknacks in order, she did not know what to do next and was surprised at this discovery. The tapestry no longer excited her admiration, reading afforded her no distraction. She passed from one room to another, without knowing what to do, what to busy her mind with, yawning, stretching herself. She shut herself up in her room where she spent hours in dressing herself, in trying on new clothes in front of the looking glass, in turning the faucet of the bath tub, which occupation amused her for a while, in combing Spy and in making elaborate bows for him from the bands of her old hats.

Managing the house might have filled the void of her idle days, but I soon realized with chagrin that Juliette was not at all the housekeeper she had boasted she was. She was careless, had no taste, was preoccupied only with her linen underwear and her dog; everything else was of no importance to her, and things took their own course or rather went according to the wishes of the servants. Our renewed staff of domestics consisted of a cook, an old, sloppy woman, grasping and ill-tempered, whose cooking talents did not extend beyond tapioca pudding, hashed veal with white sauce and salad; a chambermaid, Celestine, impudent and depraved, who respected only people who spent large sums of money, and a housekeeper, Mother Sochard, who prayed incessantly and often used to get frightfully drunk in order to forget her troubles, as she said: her husband who beat her and took away her money and her daughter who was good for nothing.

===Chinese===
朱丽叶特不久便对这套漂亮公寓厌倦起来——她原本指望在这里得到那么多安宁和幸福。衣橱收拾停当，小摆设一一归置就绪，接下来该做些什么，她不知道了，而且为这一发现暗暗诧异。挂毯再也引不起她的赞叹，读书也宽解不了她的心绪。她从一间屋子踱到另一间屋子，不知道做什么好，不知道拿什么来占住心思，打着哈欠，伸着懒腰。她把自己关在房间里，一待就是几个钟头：穿衣裳，对着穿衣镜一件件试新装，拧浴缸的放水龙头——这营生能逗她乐上一阵；再不然就给斯派（Spy）梳毛，用旧帽子的缎带给它打讲究的蝴蝶结。

操持家务本可以填补她闲散日子的空虚，可是我不久便怅然发觉，朱丽叶特根本不是她自己夸口的那种当家人。她马虎，没有品位，心里只惦记自己的贴身内衣和她那条狗；其余一切在她眼里都无足轻重，事情任其自流，或者说，全凭仆人们的心意去办。我们重新雇的一班仆人是：一个厨娘，邋里邋遢的老太婆，贪财又乖戾，烹调的本事超不出木薯布丁、白汁小牛肉末和生菜沙拉；一个贴身女仆塞莱斯特（Célestine），无礼而又品行不端，只敬重挥金如土的人；还有一个女管家索夏尔大妈（Mother Sochard），祷告不停，又常常喝得烂醉——照她的说法，是为了忘掉她的苦处：打她抢她钱的丈夫，还有那个一无是处的女儿。

===Original===
The waste was enormous, our table very bad and the rest correspondingly so. Whenever we happened to have visitors, Juliette would order from Bignon the rarest and most elaborate dishes. I viewed with displeasure the uncommon intimacy, a sort of bond of friendship, which had sprung up between Juliette and Celestine. When dressing her mistress, the maid told her stories which the former enjoyed immensely; she disclosed improper secrets of the homes where she had served and advised Juliette in all matters. “At Mme. K’s they do it this way﻿—at Mme. V’s they do it that way.” That they were “swell places” goes without saying. Juliette often went into the linen room where Celestine was sewing and stayed there for hours, seated on a heap of bedsheets, listening to the inexhaustible gossip of the servant-girl.﻿ ﻿… From time to time an argument would arise over some stolen thing or some neglected duty. Celestine would get excited, hurling the grossest insults, knocking the furniture, screaming in her squeaky voice:

“Well!﻿ ﻿… Many thanks to you!﻿ ﻿… This is some dirty place!﻿ ﻿… A goose like that has the nerve to accuse one!﻿ ﻿… Well look here, my pretty one, I am going to shake myself free from you and your boob over there who has the face of a dunce.”

Juliette would tell her to get out immediately, not wishing her even to stay out her week.

“Yes, yes! Pack up at once, you nasty girl﻿ ﻿… right away!”

===Chinese===
浪费大得惊人，我们的饭桌很坏，其余的也就等而下之。家里偶尔来了客，朱丽叶特便向比尼翁（Bignon）订购最稀罕、最讲究的菜。朱丽叶特和塞莱斯特之间不知不觉生出一种亲密，一种近乎交情的默契，我看着很不痛快。女仆替女主人梳妆时讲些故事给她听，后者听得津津有味；她抖落自己服侍过的各个人家的暧昧隐私，还凡事给朱丽叶特出主意：“K夫人家是这么办的……V夫人家是那么办的。”不用说，人家都是“体面人家”。朱丽叶特常常往缝衣间跑，塞莱斯特正在那儿做针线；她一待就是几个钟头，坐在一堆被单上，听那女仆永不见底的长舌。……时不时地，两人会为一桩偷窃或一件失职的活计吵起来。塞莱斯特火气上来，骂出最粗鄙难听的话，摔打家具，扯着她那尖利的嗓门嚷：

“哼！……多谢您啦！……这算个什么脏窝子！……这样一只蠢鹅，也敢来栽赃人！……好哇，您听着，我的美人儿，我这就撇下您，还有您那边那位长了张呆子脸的蠢货，撒手走人！”

朱丽叶特便叫她立刻滚蛋，连一个礼拜也不愿让她做满。

“对，对！马上卷铺盖，你这个贱丫头……这就走！”

===Original===
She would come to sulk in my presence, pale and trembling:

“Ah! my dear, that vile creature, that wretched woman!﻿ ﻿… And I who was so kind to her!﻿ ﻿…”

In the evening they would make up again, and amidst laughter which resounded louder than ever, Celestine’s voice would bawl out:

“I should say the Countess was a rude slut!”

===Chinese===
她便到我跟前来怄气，脸色发白，浑身发抖：

“啊！亲爱的，那个下贱东西，那个恶婆娘！……我待她多好啊！……”

到了晚上，她们又和好了，笑声比先前更响，塞莱斯特的嗓门在笑声里大嚷：

“要叫我说，伯爵夫人可真是个粗鲁的贱货！”

===Original===
One day Juliette said to me:

“Your little wifie has nothing to put on. She is as naked as a new born child, the poor thing!”

And so there were new visits to the dressmaker’s, to the milliner’s, to the linen shop; and she again became gay, vivacious, affectionate. The shadow of boredom which had crossed her countenance disappeared.﻿ ﻿… In the midst of materials, laces, among plumes and gewgaws, her whole being expanded and shone forth. Her tender fingers experienced a physical delight in handling satin, in touching crepe, in stroking velvet, in losing themselves in the milky white waves of fine batiste. The smallest piece of silk, when she draped it into something, at once assumed the pretty appearance of a living thing; out of braid and lace trimmings she could draw the most exquisite harmonies. Although I was very much alarmed by these expensive whims, I could not refuse Juliette anything, and I abandoned myself to the joy of seeing her so happy, to the delight of seeing her so charming﻿—her, whose beauty rendered all inanimate objects about her beautiful, her, who put the breath of gracious life into everything she touched!

===Chinese===
一天，朱丽叶特对我说：

“您的小媳妇儿没衣裳穿了。她光得跟个刚出世的娃娃似的，可怜见的！”

于是又是一轮新的奔波：上裁缝铺，上女帽店，上内衣店；她又快活起来，活泼、多情。一度掠过她面容的烦闷阴影消失了。……在衣料和花边中间，在羽毛和小玩意儿中间，她整个人舒展开来，容光焕发。她那双纤纤十指摸着缎子、抚着绉纱、拂着天鹅绒、没入细麻纱乳白色的波纹里，体验着一种肉体般的快感。最小的一块丝绸，经她一披一裹，立刻就有了活物般的俏丽模样；从滚边和花边饰带里，她能调和出最精致的韵味。这些花费惊人的心血来潮虽然叫我胆战，可我什么也拒绝不了朱丽叶特，我只顾沉醉在见她如此幸福的喜悦里，沉醉在见她如此动人的欢愉里——她呀，她的美使她周围一切无生命的东西都变得美了；她呀，她给她指尖碰到的一切都吹进了优雅生命的气息！

===Original===
For more than a month packages and strange cases were being delivered to us every evening.﻿ ﻿… Dresses followed dresses, hats followed cloaks, umbrellas and embroidered chemises; the most expensive linens accumulated in heaps and filled all the drawers, presses, wardrobes.

“You see, my dear,” Juliette explained to me, discerning amazement in my glance. “You see I did not have anything.﻿ ﻿… This is all I need. From now on, all I’ll have to do is to receive people.﻿ ﻿… Ah don’t be afraid!﻿ ﻿… I am very economical. See here, I have had a high body made in all my gowns for everyday use on the street, and a décolleté to wear at the Opera! Just figure out how many dresses that will save me.﻿ ﻿… One﻿ ﻿… two﻿ ﻿… three﻿ ﻿… four﻿ ﻿… five﻿ ﻿… dresses, my dear!﻿ ﻿… You see now!”

===Chinese===
一个多月里，每天傍晚都有包裹和稀奇古怪的箱子送到我们家来。……裙子接着裙子，帽子接着斗篷，还有阳伞和绣花衬衫；最贵的内衣堆成小山，塞满了所有的抽屉、衣橱、衣柜。

“您瞧，亲爱的，”朱丽叶特看出我眼里的惊讶，向我解释道，“您瞧，我从前可什么都没有。……这些全是我要的。往后呢，我什么事也不用干，只管会客。……啊，别怕！……我会过日子着呢。瞧，我所有的裙袍都做了高领口，平日上街穿；另配低领袒胸的（décolleté），上歌剧院穿！您算算这能替我省下多少条裙子。……一条……两条……三条……四条……五条……条裙子哪，亲爱的！……您这回明白了吧！”

===Original===
For the first appearance at the theatre she put on a gown that was the sensation of the evening. As long as the tormenting affair lasted I was the most miserable man in the world.﻿ ﻿… I felt the covetous glances of the entire audience directed on Juliette, glances that devoured her, that disrobed her, glances that defile the woman one adores. I would have liked to hide Juliette deep in the loge and throw a thick dark woolen cloak on her shoulders, and with heart clawed by hatred I wished the theatre had sunk into the ground through some sudden cataclysm, that by a sudden collapse of its ceiling and chandeliers it had crushed to a powder all these men, each of whom was stealing a little of Juliette’s chastity, a little of her love from me. She, on the other hand, triumphant, seemed to say: “I love you all, gentlemen, for thinking me beautiful. You are nice people.”

Scarcely did we enter our house when I drew Juliette toward me and for a long, long time held her pressed to my heart, repeating without end: “You love me, Juliette, don’t you?” but the heart of Juliette was no longer listening to me. Seeing that I was sad, noticing that from my eyelids tears were about to fall upon her cheek, she freed herself from my embrace and said somewhat angrily:

“What! I was the prettiest, the most beautiful of them all!﻿ ﻿… And you are not satisfied yet?﻿ ﻿… And you are crying yet!﻿ ﻿… That is not nice at all!﻿ ﻿… What more do you want?”

===Chinese===
头一回上剧院露面，她穿了一条当晚轰动全场的长裙。这场煎熬从头到尾，我是世上最不幸的人。……我觉得全场贪婪的目光都射在朱丽叶特身上，那目光吞噬着她，剥光着她，那目光亵渎着人们心爱的女人。我真想把朱丽叶特深深藏进包厢，往她肩上罩一件厚厚的黑呢斗篷；我心头被仇恨抓挠着，直盼着剧院让一场突如其来的地陷吞进地里，盼着天花板和吊灯一齐坍塌，把所有这些男人碾成齑粉——他们每一个，都在偷走朱丽叶特的一点贞洁，从我这儿偷走她的一点爱情。她呢，正得意洋洋，仿佛在说：“诸位觉得我美，我爱你们大家。你们都是好人。”

我们刚进家门，我就把朱丽叶特搂到身边，久久地、久久地把她贴在我的心口，没完没了地重复：“你爱我的，朱丽叶特，是不是？”可朱丽叶特的心再也不听我的了。她见我伤心，又看见我的睫毛上泪水就要滴到她的脸颊，便从我的怀抱里挣脱出去，带着几分恼怒说：

“怎么！我是她们中间最标致、最漂亮的！……您还不满足？……您还哭！……这可太不像话了！……您还要怎么样？”

===Original===
Our first disagreeable quarrel arose over Juliette’s friends. Gabrielle Bernier, Jesselin and some other people, formerly brought over to our house at the Rue de Saint Petersbourg by Malterre, again began to pursue us at the Rue de Balzac. I frankly told her so; she seemed very much surprised.

“What have you against Monsieur Jesselin?” she asked me. She used to call the others by their Christian names﻿ ﻿… but she pronounced the name Monsieur Jesselin with great respect.

“I certainly have nothing against him, my dear.﻿ ﻿… But I don’t like him, he gets on my nerves﻿ ﻿… he is ridiculous. Here, then, I think are good reasons for not wishing to see that idiot.”

Juliette was shocked. That I should have called a man of Monsieur Jesselin’s importance and reputation an idiot was quite incomprehensible to her. She looked at me with fear as if I had just uttered a terrible blasphemy.

“Monsieur Jesselin, an idiot!﻿ ﻿… He﻿ ﻿… such a gentleman, so serious minded, and who has been to India!﻿ ﻿… Don’t you know that he is a member of the Geographical Society?”

“What about Gabrielle Bernier?﻿ ﻿… Is she also a member of the Geographical Society?”

===Chinese===
我们第一场不痛快的争吵，起于朱丽叶特的朋友们。加布里埃尔·贝尔尼耶（Gabrielle Bernier）、热瑟兰（Jesselin），还有另外几个人——从前是马尔特尔领到圣彼得堡街我们家里来的——如今又到巴尔扎克街来纠缠我们了。我坦率地跟她挑明了；她显得万分惊讶。

“您跟热瑟兰先生有什么过不去的？”她问我。旁人她都直呼教名……唯独热瑟兰先生这个名字，她说起来满怀敬意。

“我跟他当然没什么过不去的，亲爱的。……可我不喜欢他，他让我心烦……他可笑。喏，我看这些理由尽够了，足够不想再见那个蠢货了。”

朱丽叶特震惊了。我竟把热瑟兰先生这样有身份、有名声的人唤作蠢货，在她看来简直不可思议。她恐惧地望着我，仿佛我方才说出了一句可怕的渎神之言。

“热瑟兰先生，蠢货！……他……这样一位绅士，这样一位端正人物，还去过印度！……您不知道他是地理学会的会员么？”

“那加布里埃尔·贝尔尼耶呢？……她也是地理学会的会员么？”

===Original===
As a rule Juliette never lost her temper. When she was angry her look became severe, the wrinkle on her forehead deepened, her voice lost a little of its sweet sonorousness. She answered simply:

“Gabrielle is my friend.”

“That’s just what I object to.”

There was a moment of silence. Juliette, seated in an armchair, was fingering the lace of her morning gown, thinking. An ironic smile wandered on her lips.

“Do you mean to say that I must not see anyone?﻿ ﻿… Is that what you want?﻿ ﻿… Well that’s going to be very amusing.﻿ ﻿… We shall never go out anymore﻿ ﻿… we shall live like beasts!﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
朱丽叶特平日从不失态。她动气的时候，目光变得严厉，额上的皱纹加深，嗓音失去一点甜润的响亮。她只简单地答道：

“加布里埃尔是我的朋友。”

“我反对的恰恰就是这个。”

一时沉默。朱丽叶特坐在扶手椅里，手指捻着晨衣上的花边，想心事。一丝讥诮的笑意在她唇边游走。

“您是想说，我什么人都不能见了？……您要的就是这个？……好啊，那可有意思极了。……咱们再也别出门了……咱们像牲口一样过日子！……”

===Original===
“That’s not the question at all, my dear.﻿ ﻿… I have some friends.﻿ ﻿… I’ll ask them to come.﻿ ﻿…”

“Oh yes, I know your friends.﻿ ﻿… I can see them right before me, writers, painters﻿ ﻿… people whom one doesn’t understand when they talk﻿ ﻿… and who borrow money from us.﻿ ﻿… Thank you very much!﻿ ﻿…”

I felt offended and quickly replied:

“My friends are honest people, do you hear, with talent, whereas that idiot and that nasty woman!﻿ ﻿…”

“I think we have had enough of this,” Juliette imperiously said. “Is that your wish?﻿ ﻿… All right. I shall close my door to them. Only when you insisted on my living with you, you should have told me that you wanted to bury me alive. I would have known what to do then.﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
“根本不是这么回事，亲爱的。……我有几个朋友。……我会请他们来的。……”

“哦，是了，您那几位朋友我可认得。……我这就能瞧见他们那副模样：作家呀，画家呀……说话没人听得懂的人……还跟咱们借钱。……多谢多谢！……”

我觉得受了冒犯，立刻回敬道：

“我的朋友都是正派人，你听见没有，都有才华；倒是你那个蠢货和那个脏女人！……”

“我看这套话说够了吧，”朱丽叶特倨傲地说，“您就认了这个命吧？……好吧。我就对他们闭门不纳。只是当初您非要我同您一起过日子，就该告诉我，您是想把我活活埋掉。那样我自然知道该怎么办。……”

===Original===
She rose. I was not even thinking of telling her that, on the contrary, it was she who had wished that we keep house together. Realizing that it was useless to argue any further I took her hand:

“Juliette,” I entreated her.

“Well, what do you want?”

“Are you angry?”

===Chinese===
她站起身来。我甚至没想到对她说，恰恰相反，当初是她自己要同我一起过日子的。我明白再争下去也是枉然，便拉起她的手：

“朱丽叶特，”我央求她。

“哼，您要怎么样？”

“你生气了？”

===Original===
“I, on the contrary, I am very much contented.﻿ ﻿…”

“Juliette!”

“Come, let go of me﻿ ﻿… quit﻿ ﻿… you hurt me.”

===Chinese===
“我？正相反，我称心得很。……”

“朱丽叶特！”

“得了，放开我……松手……您弄疼我了。”

===Original===
Juliette was sulky all day; when I said something to her she did not answer or contented herself with articulating monosyllables curtly and with irritation. I was unhappy and angry at the same time; I would have liked to embrace her and to beat her, to shower kisses and kicks on her. At dinner she still kept the air of an offended woman, with her lips firmly closed and a disdainful look in her eyes. In vain did I try to appease her by humble conduct and sad repentant looks; her assumed sullenness remained unchanged, on her brow there was still that dark furrow which made me uneasy. At night, in bed, she took a book and turned her back to me. And the back of her perfumed neck to which my lips loved to cling with rapturous joy, now seemed to me harder than a stone wall.﻿ ﻿… Within me deep resentment was stirring, but I forced myself not to betray it. In the measure that I was filled with rage, my voice sought sweeter accents, it grew gentler and more beseeching.

===Chinese===
朱丽叶特怄了一整天的气；我跟她说话，她不答理，或者只又生硬又恼怒地吐出几个单音节的字。我又难受又气恼，直想搂她，又想打她，把亲吻和脚踢一齐雨点般泼到她身上。吃晚饭时，她还端着那副受气女人的架势，双唇紧闭，眼里含着轻蔑。我低声下气、满脸悔意地讨她的好，全是白费；她那装出来的愠色纹丝不动，额上始终横着那道让我心神不安的暗纹。夜里上了床，她拿起一本书，把背对着我。她那香气袭人的后颈——我的嘴唇从前多么喜欢带着狂喜贴上去——此刻在我眼里竟比一面石墙还硬。……我心底翻涌着深深的怨愤，但我强忍着不露出来。我胸中的火气越盛，嗓音就越发去找甜蜜的调子，变得越来越温柔，越来越哀恳。

===Original===
“Juliette! my Juliette!﻿ ﻿… Speak to me, please!﻿ ﻿… Speak to me! Did I offend you, was I too harsh with you? I know I was.﻿ ﻿… Well, I am sorry and I ask your forgiveness.﻿ ﻿… But only speak to me.”

My impression was that Juliette was not listening to me at all. She was cutting the pages of the book, and the noise of the friction of the knife against the paper annoyed me terribly.

“My Juliette.﻿ ﻿… Please understand me.﻿ ﻿… It is because I love you that I said that.﻿ ﻿… It is because I wish to see you pure, respected, and because it seems to me that all those are unworthy of you.﻿ ﻿… If I did not love you, would that make any difference to me?﻿ ﻿… And you think that I don’t want you to go out!﻿ ﻿… Why no.﻿ ﻿… We shall go out often, every evening.﻿ ﻿… Ah, please don’t be like that!﻿ ﻿… I was wrong!﻿ ﻿… Scold me, strike me.﻿ ﻿… But only speak to me, please speak to me!”

===Chinese===
“朱丽叶特！我的朱丽叶特！……跟我说说话，求你了！……跟我说说话！我得罪你了么，我对你太狠了么？我知道我是的。……喏，我错了，我求你饶我。……可你要跟我说话呀。”

我的感觉是，朱丽叶特根本没在听。她在裁书页，刀刃蹭着纸面的声响吵得我心烦意乱。

“我的朱丽叶特。……你要体谅我。……我是因为爱你才说那些话的。……是因为我要看你清白、受人敬重，是因为在我看来，那些人都配不上你。……我若是不爱你，这一切与我何干？……你倒想着我不许你出门！……哪儿的话。……咱们要常常出门，天天晚上都出去。……啊，求你别这样！……是我错了！……骂我吧，打我吧。……可你要跟我说话，求你跟我说呀！”

===Original===
She continued turning the pages of the book. The words were throttled in my throat.

“It is not fair to act the way you do, Juliette. It is not nice at all to be like that.﻿ ﻿… Since I admitted my guilt! Ah, what pleasure do you get out of torturing me like this?﻿ ﻿… Didn’t I say I was sorry? Come on, Juliette, I admit I was wrong!”

Not a muscle in her body moved in response to my supplication. Her nape exasperated me more than ever. Amidst locks of silky hair I now saw eyes which railed at me and a mouth which mocked me. And I had an impulse to strike her, to belabor her with my fists, to beat her till she bled.

===Chinese===
她继续翻着书页。那些话全哽在我的喉咙里。

“你这样子可不公平，朱丽叶特。使这样的性子太不像话了。……我都认错了！啊，你这样折磨我，有什么可乐的？……我不是说过对不住么？来吧，朱丽叶特，我认错，是我不好！”

我这样哀告，她身上没有一块肌肉动一动。她那后颈比任何时候更让我火冒三丈。在丝绸般的发绺中间，我此刻看见一双斥责我的眼睛、一张讥笑我的嘴。我起了一股冲动，想打她，想抡起拳头揍她，想把她打到见血。

===Original===
“Juliette!” I shouted.

And my fingers, shriveled, spread apart and hooked like talons of a bird of prey, came close to her, in spite of myself, ready to claw this nape, impatient to tear it to pieces.

“Juliette!”

Juliette slowly turned her head, looked at me with contempt, without fear.

“What do you want?” she asked.

“What do I want?﻿ ﻿… What do I want?”

===Chinese===
“朱丽叶特！”我吼起来。

我的手指抽缩着，张开来，弯成猛禽的利爪，不由自主地向她逼近，就要抠住这后颈，急不可耐地要把它撕成碎片。

“朱丽叶特！”

朱丽叶特缓缓回过头来，轻蔑地望着我，毫无惧色。

“您要怎样？”她问。

“我要怎样？……我要怎样？”

===Original===
I was going to threaten her.﻿ ﻿… I half arose in bed, I was gesticulating violently.﻿ ﻿… And suddenly my rage subsided, I came close to Juliette, crouched before her, filled with repentance, and kissing that perfumed and beautiful nape:

“What I want, my dear, is that you should be happy.﻿ ﻿… That you shall receive your friends.﻿ ﻿… It was so foolish on my part to demand of you what I did!﻿ ﻿… Aren’t you the best of women?﻿ ﻿… Don’t I love you?﻿ ﻿… Ah, hereafter I shall have no other wish than yours, I promise you!﻿ ﻿… And you’ll see how nice I am going to be to them!﻿ ﻿… Wait.﻿ ﻿… Why should you not invite Gabrielle for dinner?﻿ ﻿… And Jesselin also?”

===Chinese===
我正要威吓她。……我在床上半支起身子，狠狠地打着手势。……可我的怒气忽然消了，我凑近朱丽叶特，蹲到她面前，满心悔愧，吻着那香气袭人的美丽的后颈：

“亲爱的，我要的，就是你幸福。……就是你能接待你的朋友。……我先前竟要求你那样的事，多糊涂啊！……你不是天下最好的女人么？……我不是爱你么？……啊，往后除了你的意愿，我再没有别的意愿了，我向你保证！……你会看见我待他们多好的！……等等。……干吗不请加布里埃尔来吃晚饭呢？……把热瑟兰也请上？”

===Original===
“No! No! You say it now, but tomorrow you’ll reproach me for it.﻿ ﻿… No! No!﻿ ﻿… I don’t want to force upon you people whom you despise﻿—nasty women and idiots!”
