“I don’t know where my head was when I said that.﻿ ﻿… I don’t despise them at all﻿ ﻿… on the contrary I like them very much.﻿ ﻿… Invite both of them.﻿ ﻿… And I’ll go and get a box at the Vaudeville.”

“No!”

“I implore you!”

Her voice became less harsh, she closed the book.

“Well! We’ll see tomorrow.”

===Chinese===
“不！不！您现在这么说，明天又要怪我了。……不！不！……我不愿把您瞧不起的人硬塞给您——脏女人和蠢货！”

“我不知道说那话时自己脑子里灌了什么。……我一点也不瞧不起他们……恰恰相反，我很喜欢他们。……把两位都请来吧。……我这就去沃德维尔剧院（Vaudeville）订个包厢。”

“不！”

“我求你了！”

她的嗓音缓和了些，合上了书。

“好吧！明天再说。”

===Original===
Really, at that moment I loved Gabrielle, Jesselin, Celestine﻿—I even thought I loved Malterre.

===Chinese===
说真的，那一刻我爱加布里埃尔，爱热瑟兰，爱塞莱斯特——我甚至觉得，我爱马尔特尔。

===Original===
I no longer worked. Not that love of work deserted me, but I no longer had the creative faculty in me. I used to sit down at my desk every day, with blank sheets of paper before me, searching for ideas, and failing to find them, I would again relapse into anxieties of the present, which meant Juliette, into dread of the future, which again meant Juliette!﻿ ﻿… Just as a drunkard clutches and turns his empty bottle to get the last drop of liquor out of it, so I searched my brains in the hope of squeezing the least bit of an idea out of it.﻿ ﻿… Alas! My head was empty!

===Chinese===
我不再工作了。倒不是勤奋离我而去，而是我身上再没有创造的才具。每天我照旧在书桌前坐下，面前摊着白纸，搜寻着构思；搜不到，便又跌回对眼下的焦虑里——眼下就是朱丽叶特——跌回对前途的恐惧里，而前途，还是朱丽叶特！……就像酒鬼抓着空瓶子翻来倒去，想倒出最后一滴酒，我也这样翻搅自己的脑子，巴望从里头榨出一点思想的残汁。……唉！我的脑子空空如也！

===Original===
It was empty and weighed upon my shoulders like an enormous ball of lead!﻿ ﻿… My mentality was always slow in getting started: it required stimulation, it had to be lashed with a whip. Because of my ill-balanced sensibility, my passive nature, I easily yielded to intellectual or moral influences, whether good or bad. And again, Lirat’s friendship was quite useful to me in the past. My own ideas melted in the warmth of his spirit; his conversation opened for me new horizons hitherto unsuspected; whatever confused ideas I had were cleared up, they assumed a more definite form which I endeavored to express; he taught me how to see, to understand things and made me delve with him into the mysteries of life.

Now, the clear horizons toward which I was led shrunk and were shut off before me daily, almost hourly, and night was coming, black night, which to me was not only visible but tangible, for I could actually touch this monstrous night, I felt its darkness stuck fast in my hair, glued to my fingers, coiled around my body in clammy rings.﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===
它空空如也，却像个巨大的铅球压在我肩上！……我的头脑一向启动得慢：它需要刺激，需要拿鞭子抽。由于我失衡的感受力和被动的天性，我很容易屈从于智识上或道德上的种种影响，无论好坏。从前，利拉的友谊于我大有益处。我自己的念头在他精神的暖意里融化；他的谈吐为我打开种种先前未曾料想的新天地；我那些含混的想法澄清了，有了更明确的形态，我便努力去表达它们；他教会我去看，去理解事物，还带着我同他一起探究生命的奥秘。

如今，我被引向的那些明朗的地平线，一天天、几乎一个钟头一个钟头地在我面前收缩、遮断；黑夜来了，漆黑的长夜，它对我不仅看得见，而且摸得着，因为我当真摸得到这狰狞的黑夜：我觉出它的暗色牢牢粘在我的头发上，胶在我的指头上，一圈圈冰凉地缠在我身上。……

===Original===
My study room opened into a yard, or rather a little garden shaded by two large plane-trees and bounded by a wall that had latticework and was covered with ivy. Behind this wall, in the midst of another garden, the grey and very high façade of a house rose, accosting me with five rows of windows. On the third floor an old man sat near the window opening which encased him like a picture frame. He wore a cap of black velvet, a checkered morning robe, and he never stirred. Shrunk into himself, his head drooping on his chest, he seemed asleep. Of his face I could see only wrinkles of yellowish, wrinkled flesh, dark cavities and locks which looked like tufts of a soiled beard, resembling some strange vegetation sprouting on the trunks of dead trees. Sometimes the profile of a woman would bend over him sinisterly, and this profile had the appearance of an owl perched upon the aged man’s shoulder; I could discern its hooked bill and round eyes, cruel, avaricious and bloodthirsty. When the sun shone into the garden, the window opened and I heard a shrill, piercing, angry voice which never ceased screaming reproaches. Then the old man would shrink into himself still farther, his head would begin to oscillate slightly, then he would become motionless again, still more buried in the folds of his morning robe, still deeper sunk in his armchair.

===Chinese===
我的书房朝一方院子开窗，说得更确切些，是朝一座小花园：两株高大的悬铃木遮出浓荫，四围一道花格墙，爬满常春藤。墙外另一座园子的当央，耸起一堵灰色的、极高峻的房屋正面，五排窗子迎面对着我。三层楼上，一个老人坐在窗口，窗洞像画框似的把他框在其中。他戴一顶黑丝绒便帽，穿一件格子晨衣，一动不动。整个人缩成一团，头垂到胸前，像睡熟了一样。他的脸，我只看得见一褶褶泛黄的皱皮、黑黢黢的凹窝，还有几绺头发，活像一蓬脏污的须髯，好似枯树干上生出的什么怪异草木。有时，一个女人的侧影会阴森森地俯向他；那侧影活像一只栖在老头肩头的猫头鹰：我辨认得出它钩曲的喙和圆滚滚的眼睛，残忍，贪婪，嗜血。太阳照进园子的时候，窗子开了，我听见一个尖利刺耳、怒气冲冲的嗓子，骂声不绝。于是老头愈发缩紧身子，脑袋开始微微地摇晃，随后又归于不动，更深地埋进晨衣的褶皱，更深地陷进他的扶手椅。

===Original===
I used to sit for hours and watch the unhappy man, and I fancied terrible tragedies, some fatal love affair, a noble life bungled, crushed and ruined by that woman with the owl face. I pictured to myself this living corpse as beautiful, young and strong.﻿ ﻿… Perhaps he had been an artist once upon a time, a scientist, or simply a happy and kindhearted man. And tall and upright, with a gaze full of hope, he marched towards glory or happiness.﻿ ﻿… One day he met that woman at the house of a friend﻿ ﻿… and that woman, too, wore a perfumed veil, a small muff, an otter skin cap, a heavenly smile, and an air of angelic sweetness.﻿ ﻿… And forthwith he fell in love with her.﻿ ﻿… I followed step by step the development of his love affair, I counted up his weaknesses, his moments of cowardice, his growing downfalls up to the time of his sinking into this armchair for cripples and paralytics.

===Chinese===
我一坐几个钟头，望着这个不幸的人，幻想着种种可怕的悲剧：一段致命的情事，一场被那个猫头鹰脸女人断送、碾压、毁掉的体面人生。我把这具活尸想象成当年的俊美、年轻、强壮。……或许他从前是个艺术家，或者科学家，再不然就是个快活和善的人。他高大挺拔，目光里盛满希望，向着光荣或者幸福进发。……有一天，他在一位朋友家里遇见了那个女人……那女人也蒙着香喷喷的面纱，揣着小小的手筒，戴一顶水獭皮帽，带着天仙般的微笑和天使似的温柔神气。……他当下便爱上了她。……我一步步追随这段情事的发展，数点着他的软弱、他怯懦的时刻、他一步步的堕落，直到他陷进这把残废人和瘫子用的扶手椅。

===Original===
And what I imagined his life was to him, my own life was to me, those were my own feelings, it was my own dread of the future, my own anguish.﻿ ﻿… Little by little my hallucination took on a singular physical form, and it was myself that I saw in this velvet cap, in this morning robe with this battered body, this murky beard, and Juliette who stood over my shoulder like an owl.﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===
我替他想象出来的那份人生，正是我自己的人生；那些是我自己的感受，是我自己对前途的恐惧、自己的痛苦。……渐渐地，我的幻觉获得了奇特的肉身，我看见的正是我自己：戴着这顶丝绒便帽，穿着这件晨衣，拖着这具破败的身躯，留着这蓬暗浊的须髯，而朱丽叶特像猫头鹰一样，立在我的肩头。……

===Original===
Juliette!﻿ ﻿… She walked about in the study, weary of body, her whole figure betraying boredom, yawning and sighing. She could not think of anything to distract her. Most often she would place the card table not far from me and lose herself in the card combinations of a complicated “patience,” or she would stretch herself out on the sofa, spread a napkin over her dress, place upon it some tiny instruments of tortoise shell, microscopic containers of ointment, and begin polishing her nails, fiercely filing them and making them shine more lustrously than agate. She would examine them every five minutes, looking for the reflection of her image in the polished surfaces:

“Look my dear! Aren’t they beautiful! And you, too, Spy, look at your mistress’ pretty nails.”

===Chinese===
朱丽叶特！……她在书房里走来走去，身子倦怠，整个人透着烦闷，打着哈欠，叹着气。她想不出什么来消遣。多数时候，她把牌桌支在离我不远的地方，沉入一局复杂的“单人纸牌”的牌阵；再不然就伸展四肢往长沙发上一躺，往裙子上铺一方餐巾，摆上些玳瑁壳的小器具、小得像显微镜底下的油膏盒，然后修起指甲来，狠狠地锉着，锉得它们比玛瑙还莹润生光。她每隔五分钟就察看一回，在锉亮的甲面上照自己的影子：

“瞧，亲爱的！漂不漂亮！还有你，斯派，也来看看你女主人的漂亮指甲。”

===Original===
The light friction of the nail brush, the imperceptible creaking of the sofa, Juliette’s remarks, her conversation with Spy﻿—all this was sufficient to put to rout the few ideas which I strove to bring together. My thoughts would turn immediately to ordinary matters, and I meditated upon painful things and lived sorrowful things over again.﻿ ﻿… Juliette.﻿ ﻿… Did I love her? Many times this question arose in my mind, pregnant with horrible doubt.﻿ ﻿… Had I not been deceived by the stupefaction of my senses?﻿ ﻿… Was not this thing which I took for love, the ephemeral and fleeting manifestation of a pleasure as yet untasted?﻿ ﻿… Juliette!﻿ ﻿… Of course I loved her.﻿ ﻿…

But this Juliette whom I loved, was she not altogether different, was she not the Juliette that I had myself created, that had been born of my own imagination, that had originated in my own brains, whom I had endowed with a soul, with a spark of divinity, whom I had fashioned into being with the ideal essence of angels?﻿ ﻿… And did I not still love her as one does a beautiful book, a beautiful verse, a beautiful statue, a visible and tangible realization of an artist’s dream!﻿ ﻿… But this other Juliette!﻿ ﻿… This one here?﻿ ﻿… This pretty, senseless, ignorant animal, this knickknack, this piece of cloth, this nothing?﻿ ﻿…

I studied her carefully while she was polishing her nails.﻿ ﻿… Oh, how I would have liked to break this neck and sound its emptiness, to open this heart and probe its nothingness! And I said to myself: “What sort of a life will mine be with this woman whose tastes are only for pleasure, who is happy only when she is dressed up, whose every wish costs a fortune, who in spite of her chaste appearance, has an instinctive predilection for vice; who used to leave unhappy Malterre every evening, without a single regret, without a single thought; who will leave me tomorrow, perhaps; this woman who is a living denial of my aspirations, of my ideals; who will never, never enter into my intellectual life; and lastly this woman who already weighs upon my intelligence like folly, upon my whole being like a crime.”

===Chinese===
指甲锉的轻响，沙发隐约的吱呀，朱丽叶特的评语，她同斯派的闲谈——这些就足以把我好不容易聚拢起来的那点想法冲得七零八落。我的念头立刻转向俗务，我心里盘算着痛苦的事情，把伤心事重新过一遍。……朱丽叶特。……我爱她么？多少次，这个问题涌上我的心头，怀着可怕的疑团。……我不是被感官的迷乱欺骗了么？……这件被我当作爱情的东西，难道不是一种从未尝过的快感的朝生暮死的表现？……朱丽叶特！……我当然是爱她的。……

可是我爱的这个朱丽叶特，难道不是截然不同的另一个？她难道不是我亲手创造的朱丽叶特，从我的想象里诞生、源出于我的头脑、由我赋予灵魂、赋予一星神性、用天使的理想质料塑成的那个？……我爱她，难道不像人爱一本好书、一句好诗、一尊好雕像，爱一个艺术家的梦那看得见摸得着的实现！……可这另一个朱丽叶特！……眼前这一个呢？……这只漂亮、无知、愚钝的动物，这件小摆设，这块衣料，这个空无？……

她修着指甲，我细细端详她。……啊，我多想折断这根脖颈，听听它空不空；剖开这颗心，探探它有多空！我暗自说：“同这个女人一起，我的日子将是什么日子？她的口味只在享乐，她只有打扮起来才快活，她每个心愿都要花掉一笔家财；她虽是一副贞洁的相貌，骨子里却本能地亲近堕落；她当初每天傍晚撇下不幸的马尔特尔，没有一丝惋惜，没有一点牵挂；她明天或许也会这样撇下我；这个女人，她活着就是对我的志向、对我的理想的否定；她永远，永远不会踏进我的精神生活；还有——这个女人，她已经压在我的智识上，像一场癫狂，压在我的整个存在上，像一桩罪孽。”

===Original===
I had a notion to flee, to tell Juliette: “I am going out, I’ll be back in an hour,” and never to return to this house where the very ceiling was more oppressive to me than the lid of a coffin, where the air stifled me, where the very furniture seemed to say to me: “Leave this place”﻿ ﻿… But no!﻿ ﻿… I loved her, and it was this very Juliette that I loved, not the other one who has gone the way of all dreams!﻿ ﻿… I loved her with all her qualities which made me suffer, I loved her in spite of all her lack of understanding, I loved her with all her frivolity, with all her suspected perversions; I loved her with that tormenting love which a mother has for her afflicted child.

Have you ever met a poor creature huddled up behind the door on some wintry day, a wretched human being with chapped lips and chattering teeth, shivering in his tattered rags?﻿ ﻿… And when you met him, were you not carried away by a feeling of keen pity, and did you not have a desire to take him and warm him against your breast, give him something to eat, cover his shivering body with warm clothes?﻿ ﻿… That is how I loved Juliette; I loved her with an immense pity﻿ ﻿… ah, don’t laugh, with a mother’s pity, with an endless pity!﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===
我动过逃跑的念头，想对朱丽叶特说：“我出去一趟，一个钟头就回来，”然后永远不再回这幢房子——在这里，连天花板都压得我喘不过气，比棺材盖还沉重；这里的空气叫我窒息；这里的家具仿佛都在对我说：“离开这儿吧”……可是不！……我爱她，我爱的正是这个朱丽叶特，不是随着一切美梦逝去的另一个！……我连同那些叫我受苦的品性一起爱她，我全然不顾她的不解风情而爱她，我连同她的全部轻浮、她那些疑似的不端一起爱她；我爱她，用那种母亲疼爱病残孩子的、备受煎熬的爱。

你可曾在某个寒冬的日子遇见过一个缩在门背后的可怜人，一个嘴唇皴裂、牙齿打战、裹着破衣烂衫瑟瑟发抖的不幸家伙？……你遇见他的时候，难道不曾被一阵尖锐的怜悯攫住，不曾想过把他搂过来贴在你胸口焐热，给他东西吃，拿暖和的衣裳裹住他打颤的身子？……我就是这样爱朱丽叶特的；我爱她，怀着无边的怜悯……啊，别笑，怀着母亲般的怜悯，怀着无止无休的怜悯！……

===Original===
“Aren’t we going out, my dear? It would be so nice to take a stroll through the Bois.”

And casting her eyes on the blank sheet of paper on which I had not written a line:

“Is that all you wrote?﻿ ﻿… Well!﻿ ﻿… You do not seem to have worked very hard.﻿ ﻿… And here I have been sitting around all this time to inspire you to work!﻿ ﻿… Oh, well, I know you won’t get anywhere.﻿ ﻿… You are too lazy!”

===Chinese===
“咱们不出去走走么，亲爱的？到布洛涅林园去散散步，多好。”

说着，她把目光落到我那一个字也没写的白纸上：

“您就写了这么点？……好嘛！……瞧您干活的样子，可真不见得卖力。……我还一直守在这儿给您打气，好让您来灵感呢！……唉，算了，我知道您是成不了事的。……您太懒了！”

===Original===
Ere long we began going out every day and every evening. I did not resist any longer, almost happy to escape from the deadly aversion and despondent thoughts with which our apartment inspired me, escape from the symbolic vision of the old man, from myself.﻿ ﻿… Ah, above all from myself. In a crowd, in the tumult, in this feverish haste of a pleasure-hunting life I hoped to find forgetfulness, to be able to dull my feeling, to subdue my rebellious spirit, to suppress the voice of my past which I heard grumbling within me. And since I could not raise Juliette to my level I lowered myself down to her own.

===Chinese===
没过多久，我们就白天黑夜都往外跑了。我不再抵抗，几乎庆幸能躲开这套公寓令我窒息的厌恶和消沉的念头，躲开那个老人的象征性的幻影，躲开我自己。……啊，头一件是躲开我自己。在人群里，在喧嚣里，在这种寻欢作乐的生活的狂热匆促里，我指望找到遗忘，指望麻木我的感受，降服我倔强的精神，压住我身内那个抱怨着咕哝的过去的声音。既然我不能把朱丽叶特提到我的高度，我就把自己降到她的高度去。

===Original===
Ah, those serene heights where the sun was reigning and toward which I had been climbing slowly with such terrific effort!﻿ ﻿… I must descend into the pit at one dash, in a single, instantaneous, inevitable downfall, even if I crushed my head against the rocks or disappeared in the bottomless mire. With me it was no longer a question of escape. If occasionally the idea did pierce the haze of my mind, if, in the errings of my willpower, I sometimes did perceive a distant way out where duty seemed to call me, I, in order to break away from the idea, in order not to rush hastily toward that end, clung tenaciously to the false pretenses of honor.﻿ ﻿… Could I leave Juliette! I who insisted that she leave Malterre!﻿ ﻿… What will become of her when I am gone?﻿ ﻿… Why no, no!﻿—I was lying to myself.﻿ ﻿… I did not want to leave her because I loved her, because I pitied her, because.﻿ ﻿… But was it not myself that I loved, myself that I pitied?﻿ ﻿… Ah, I no longer knew! I no longer knew!

And then again you should not think that the abyss into which I had fallen was a sudden revelation to me.﻿ ﻿… Don’t you believe it! I saw it from afar, I saw its black opening yawning fearfully, and I ran toward it. I leaned over the edge to inhale the infected odor of its filth, I said to myself: “There is where wasted lives and corrupted beings are dashed and swallowed up.﻿ ﻿… Here one can never come up again, never!” And I plunged into it.﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===
啊，那些太阳君临其上的宁静高地，我曾何等艰难、何等缓慢地向它们攀登！……我却必须一步跌进深坑，一记仓促的、转瞬的、不可挽回的坠落，哪怕脑袋砸碎在岩石上，哪怕灭顶于无底的泥沼。对我来说，问题已不再是逃脱。偶尔，那念头当真刺破我心头的迷雾；偶尔，在意志的迷误里，我当真望见远处一条出路，责任仿佛在那里召唤我——我便死死抱住荣誉的虚假托词，好斩断这念头，好不让自己仓皇扑向那个结局。……我怎么能离开朱丽叶特！我，当初非要她离开马尔特尔不可的人！……我走了，她会落得怎样？……不，不！——我在骗我自己。……我不想离开她，因为我爱她，因为我怜她，因为……可我爱的、我怜的，难道不正是我自己么？……啊，我再也弄不清了！再也弄不清了！

再说，你也别以为我坠入的这个深渊，对我是一种突如其来的启示。……休想！我远远地就望见了它，望见它黑黢黢的洞口可怕地张着，而我偏朝它奔去。我俯在渊边，吸着它秽物里蒸腾出来的臭气，对自己说：“虚掷的生命、腐坏的人，就是在这里撞得粉碎、被吞没的。……从这里头，人再也上不来，永远上不来！”于是我纵身跳了进去。……

===Original===
Despite the threatening sky overcast with clouds, the balcony of the café is crowded with people. There is not a vacant table, the cabarets, the circus shows, the theatres have poured forth the scum of their habitués here. Everywhere are bright-colored dresses and black frock coats, ladies adorned with plumes like horses in a parade, weary, sick looking and sallow; flurried fops with heads drooped upon their buttonholes without flowers, and nibbling the ends of their canes with apelike gestures. Some of them with legs crossed in order to show their black silk socks embroidered with red flowerets, hats pushed over slightly toward the back, are whistling the latest hit﻿—the air which has just now been sung at the Ambassadeur, to the accompaniment of the creaking of seats, the clatter of glasses and bottles.

===Chinese===
尽管阴云四合、天色逼人，咖啡馆的露台上还是黑压压坐满了人。一张空桌也找不到：卡巴莱酒馆、马戏场、剧院，把它们常客中的渣滓全倾泻到了这里。满眼是鲜亮的裙衫和黑礼服大衣；太太们插着羽毛，像阅兵式上的马，倦怠、带病容、面色蜡黄；轻浮的纨绔子们垂着头凑在自己的扣眼上——扣眼里连朵花也没有——用猿猴般的姿势啃着手杖头。有几个跷起二郎腿，好亮出绣着小红花的黑丝袜，帽子微微斜向后脑，吹着口哨哼时下最新的调子——就是方才在阿姆巴萨德尔（Ambassadeur）唱过的那支——和着座椅的吱呀、杯盘瓶盏的磕碰。

===Original===
The last of the lights in front of the opera has been extinguished. But all around it the windows of the clubhouses and brothels are a red blaze, like openings into hell. On the street, parked near the curb, are worn-out and dilapidated open coaches strung out in triple file. Some of the drivers are drowsing in their seats; others gathering into small groups which present a comical appearance in their ill-fitting liveries, are munching cigar stubs, and talking with loud bursts of laughter, telling salacious stories about their clients. One incessantly hears the shrill voice of the newspaper vendors who run back and forth shouting, in the midst of their crisp outcries, the name of some well-known woman, or some scandalous piece of news, while street arabs, gliding between the tables, cunning as cats, are selling obscene pictures, half revealed, to awaken dormant passions, to stir up curiosities gone to sleep. And little girls whose premature depravity has already blighted their gaunt, childish faces are offering for sale bouquets of flowers, smiling with a dubious smile, charging their glances with the ripe and hideous immodesty of old prostitutes. Inside the cabaret all the tables are taken.﻿ ﻿… There is not a single vacant place.﻿ ﻿… People are drinking champagne without really wanting it and munching sandwiches without in the least caring for them. Occasionally curious people enter the place, before going to their clubs or to bed, by force of habit or from a mere desire to show off or to see if there is “anything doing” there. Slowly and slouching in their walk, they slink about the groups of guests, stopping to chat with their friends here and there and, waving their hands in greeting to someone at a distance, look at themselves in the mirrors, fix their white cravats which stick out from under their light overcoats, then leave, their minds enriched with a few new slang expressions of the underworld, with a few more scandals picked up here and upon which their idleness will thrive for a whole day.

===Chinese===
歌剧院门前的灯，最后几盏已经熄了。可它四周，俱乐部和妓院的窗子烧着一片红焰，像通到地狱里去的洞口。街上，沿路缘石，破旧寒酸的四轮敞篷车摆开三行。有的车夫在座位上打盹；有的凑成一小堆一小堆，那身不合体的号衣让他们显得滑稽，嚼着雪茄烟蒂，放声谈笑，讲着主顾们的淫秽故事。报贩尖利的嗓音不绝于耳，跑来跑去，在脆亮的吆喝里喊着某位名女人的名字，或某件骇人的丑闻；野孩子们猫一般狡黠，在桌椅间穿梭，兜售半遮半掩的淫画，好唤醒沉睡的情欲，撩拨睡熟的猎奇心。还有小女孩们，早熟的堕落已经把她们瘦削的娃娃脸毁得没了样子，正在叫卖花束，挂着暧昧的笑，眼睛里充着老妓女那种熟透而丑陋的浪荡。酒馆里，所有的桌子都坐满了。……没有一个空位。……人们喝着香槟，其实并不想喝；嚼着三明治，其实毫无胃口。偶尔有好事之徒进来——去俱乐部或上床之前，出于习惯，或纯粹为了摆摆派头，来看看这儿“有没有热闹”。他们拖拖沓沓，慢吞吞地在客人堆里逡巡，走走停停，同熟人聊上几句，朝远处什么人挥挥手，照照镜子，理一理浅色大衣底下翘出来的白领结，然后走人，脑子里添了几句下流社会的新切口，添了几桩在这儿拾来的丑闻——够他们的懒散就此咀嚼一整天。

===Original===
The women with elbows resting on the table, an ice cream soda in front of them, their weak faces, hatched with fine pink lines, supported by long gloved hands, assume a languid air, a suffering mien and a sort of consumptive dreaminess. They exchange mysterious winks and imperceptible smiles with their neighbors at nearby tables, while the gentleman accompanying them, silent and affectedly courteous, strikes the point of his shoes with the tip of his cane. The gathering presents a brilliant spectacle variegated with lace and baubles, bright trimmings and pompons, tinged plumes and flowers in full bloom, curls of blond hair, tresses of dark hair and the glitter of diamonds. Everyone is at his fighting post, the young and the old, beginners with beardless faces, grey-haired veterans, naive gulls and crafty spongers, here where social scandals, false situations, riotous vice, base covetousness, shameful barter﻿—all the flowers of corruption which sprout﻿—mingle, grow and thrive in the dunghill warmth of Paris.

===Chinese===
女人们胳膊肘支在桌上，面前摆着一杯冰激凌苏打，那张虚弱的、划满细红纹的脸由戴长手套的双手托着，摆出一副慵懒的神气、受难的面相和一种痨病式的出神。她们同邻桌交换神秘的眼色和难以察觉的微笑；陪同的先生们则沉默寡言，做作地客气，用手杖尖敲着自己的鞋尖。这聚会呈现出一派辉煌的景象：花边与小饰物，亮片与绒球，染色的羽毛和盛开的花朵，金色鬈发，深色发辫，钻石的闪光，斑驳交织。人人都守在自己的战位上：年轻的和老年的，嘴上没毛的新手，头发花白的老将，天真的冤大头和狡猾的揩油客——在这里，社交界的丑闻、虚假的处境、放荡的恶行、卑劣的贪欲、无耻的买卖——一切滋生出来的腐败之花——掺和在一处，在巴黎这温热的粪堆上生长，繁茂。

===Original===
It was into this atmosphere charged with ennui, restlessness and heavy odors that we used thereafter to come every evening. During the day, visits to the dressmakers, the Bois, the races; in the evening, restaurants, theatres and fashionable gatherings. Wherever this special brand of society people came together, one was certain to see us; we were even made much of because of Juliette’s beauty which began to be the subject of people’s talk, and her dresses which called forth the envy and emulation of other women. We no longer dined at home. Our apartment served us as hardly anything more than a place to dress. When Juliette was dressing she grew harsh, even cruel. The wrinkle on her forehead cut into her skin like a scar. She uttered disjointed words, grew angry, seemed to be incensed to the point of breaking up things.

===Chinese===
此后每天晚上，我们来到的就是这样一片空气：充塞着厌倦、躁动和浓重的气味。白天，逛裁缝铺，跑布洛涅林园，看赛马；晚上，下馆子，上剧院，赶时髦聚会。凡是这路特殊的社交人物麇集的地方，保准看得到我们；我们甚至颇受抬举，因为朱丽叶特的美貌渐渐成了众人谈论的话题，她的裙裳惹得别的女人又羡又妒。我们不再在家吃饭。我们的公寓对我们来说，几乎只剩下一个更衣的地方。朱丽叶特梳妆的时候，脾气就变坏，甚至变得冷酷。额上那道皱纹像一道疤，割进她的皮肉。她说着颠三倒四的话，发起火来，仿佛怒不可遏，要把东西统统砸烂才罢休。

===Original===
All around her the room seemed as if it had been pillaged: trunks were opened, skirts thrown on the carpet, fans taken out of their cases and scattered on the chairs, opera glasses left on the furniture; muslin gowns were lying in heaps in the corners, the floor was strewn with flowers, towels, soiled with rouge, gloves, stockings, veils were hanging on the branches of the candlesticks. And in the midst of this confusion, Celestine, agile, brazen-faced, cynical, was going through all sorts of evolutions, jumping, sliding, kneeling at the feet of her mistress, sticking a pin here, adjusting the pleats there, knotting threads, her soft, flabby hands, made to handle filthy things, gliding all over Juliette’s body with affection. She was happy, she no longer replied to insinuating remarks, to bitter reproaches, and her eyes, persistently ironical, shining with a flame of vulgar depravity were riveted on me.

===Chinese===
她四周的房间像遭了洗劫：衣箱敞开，裙子扔在地毯上，扇子出了匣四散在椅子上，观剧镜撂在家具上；细麻纱长裙一堆堆摊在墙角，地板上撒满了花、沾了口红的毛巾、手套、长袜，面纱挂在烛台的枝杈上。就在这一片狼藉中间，塞莱斯特敏捷、厚脸皮、没心没肺，做着种种腾挪：跳着，滑着，跪在女主人脚边，这边别一根别针，那边理一理裙褶，打着线结；那双柔软松弛、天生摆弄脏东西的手，带着感情在朱丽叶特周身游走。她快活，人家影影绰绰的讥讽她不回嘴了，刻薄的责备她也不吭声了；她那双一意讥诮、闪着庸俗堕落之火的眼睛，死死钉在我身上。

===Original===
It was only in public, in the glare of lights, under the cross fire of men’s gazes that Juliette again found her smile and expression of joy mixed with a little wonderment and frankness which she reserved only for this repugnant crowd of debauchées. And we used to come to this cabaret accompanied by Gabrielle, by Jesselin, by people met I don’t know where, and presented to us by I don’t know whom, by idiots and princes, by the whole batch of international and street corner crooks whom we dragged along with us.

===Chinese===
只有到了人前，在灯光的照耀下，在男人们目光的交叉火力底下，朱丽叶特才重新找到她的笑容，找回那种欢喜的神情——里头掺着一点讶异和坦率，那是她单单为这伙令人作呕的放荡者保留的。而我们到这家酒馆来，总有加布里埃尔作陪，有热瑟兰，有不知在哪儿结识、也不知经谁引荐的人，有蠢货和王爷，有走到哪儿被我们拖到哪儿的那一整批国际的、街角的骗子。

===Original===
“What are you going to do tonight?”

“I am going with the Mintié crowd.”

Jesselin gave us information about the people in the place; he knew all the inside facts of high society life and spoke of it with a sort of admiration in spite of all the shameful or tragic details which he told us.

===Chinese===
“今晚您打算做什么？”

“我跟曼蒂埃这伙人一道。”

热瑟兰给我们介绍场中的人物；上流社会生活的种种内幕他无所不知，讲起来还带着几分叹服，尽管他告诉我们的一尽是些无耻或悲惨的细节。

===Original===
“That man there, who has a crowd of people around him and who is listened to respectfully, used to be a valet de chambre. His master fired him for theft. But he became the keeper of a gambling house, conducted all kinds of illegitimate joints, became cashier of the Club, then skilfully disappeared for a few years. At the present time he is part owner of many gambling houses, has an interest in the racetrack, has unlimited credit with the stockbrokers, owns horses and a mansion where he receives people. He used to loan money secretly at one hundred percent interest to ladies in financial straits whose gullible natures he would first test. Ostentatiously generous, buying pictures of the most expensive kind, he passes for an honorable man and patron of the arts. In the papers they speak of him with great respect.

===Chinese===
“那边那个人，身边围着一圈人、人人恭恭敬敬听他说话的，从前当过贴身男仆。主人因为偷窃把他辞退了。可他后来开了赌场，经营种种非法的场子，当上俱乐部的出纳，随后又机灵地销声匿迹了几年。如今他是好几家赌场的股东，赛马场里有股份，在证券经纪人那里有无限的信用，养着马，还有一座待客的公馆。他从前偷偷放债，一百厘的利息，专借给手头窘迫的太太们，先试试她们是否好骗。他出手豪阔，闹得人人皆知，买的画都是最贵的，人家当他是个体面人、艺术的赞助人。报纸提起他来，口气都恭而敬之。

===Original===
“And that other big, stout fellow whose fleshy, wrinkled face is eternally split by an idiotic laugh? He is but a child!﻿ ﻿… Hardly eighteen years old. He has a mistress known to all, with whom he appears in public every Monday at the Bois, and also has a teacher, an Abbot whom he takes to the lake every Tuesday in the same carriage. His mother has thus conceived of the education of her son, wishing him to lead a life of religious saintliness on the one hand and of gallant adventures on the other. Aside from that he gets drunk every evening, and horsewhips his old fool of a mother. A real type!” Jesselin summed up.

===Chinese===
“还有那个大块头的胖子，那张肥肉打褶的脸永远咧着一副傻笑？他还不过是个孩子！……勉强十八岁。他有个尽人皆知的情妇，每星期一带着她到布洛涅林园露面；还有一位教师，一位神父，每星期二用同一辆马车拉他去湖边。他母亲给儿子想定的就是这样的教育：一头过圣洁的教徒生活，一头过风流的艳遇生涯。此外，他每天晚上喝得烂醉，拿马鞭抽他那个老傻瓜母亲。一个典型！”热瑟兰总结道。

===Original===
“That duke over there, who bears one of the most illustrious names in France! Ah! that swell duke! the king of spongers! He comes in timidly like a frightened dog, looks through his monocle, takes in the smell of supper, sits down and devours some ham and minced liver pie. Perhaps he has not dined yet, this duke; undoubtedly he has just come back from an unsuccessful daily visit to the café Anglais, or the Maison Doré or Bignon’s, in quest of some friend who will treat him to a meal. Being on very good terms with women and horse dealers, he runs errands for the former and rides the horses for the latter. Instructed to say wherever he goes: ‘Oh! What a charming woman!’﻿ ﻿… ‘Oh! what a wonderful horse!’ he receives a few louis for this service with which he pays his valet.

===Chinese===
“那边那位公爵，顶着法兰西最显赫的姓氏之一！啊！这位阔气的公爵！揩油客之王！他怯生生地进来，像条受惊的狗，举着单片眼镜四下张望，嗅着夜宵的香味，坐下，便大嚼火腿和碎肝酱饼。这位公爵也许还没吃上晚饭；不用说，他刚跑完每天那趟徒劳的拜访——英吉利咖啡馆（café Anglais）、金屋餐馆（Maison Doré）或者比尼翁——想寻个肯请他吃饭的朋友。他同女人们和马贩子都处得极好：替前者跑腿，替后者骑马。人家教他走到哪儿说到哪儿：‘哦！多迷人的女人！’……‘哦！多出色的马！’他这份差事挣下几个金路易，拿去付他男仆的工钱。

===Original===
“Here is another great nobleman who is gradually and hopelessly sinking into the mire of illicit business and secret promotion of vice. Once upon a time that fellow was quite the rage of society. Despite his stoutness which is now evident, despite the puffiness of his flesh, he still has an elegant way of carrying himself, and the air of a gentleman. In the disreputable places and questionable circles which he frequents he plays the remunerative role which was played fifty years ago by head waiters at table d’hôtes. His courteousness and education were an asset to him, which he made use of to perfection. He knew how to take advantage of the dishonesty of others as well as of his own, for no one was as skilful in composing matrimonial difficulties of his patrons to the satisfaction of all concerned as he was.

===Chinese===
“这儿又是一位大贵人，正一步步地、无可救药地陷进非法生意和暗中纵恶的泥沼。当年这家伙在社交界也红过一阵。别看他如今这副发福的样子，别看他一身虚浮的肉，他的仪态依旧风流，还有一副绅士气派。在他出没的那些下等场所和可疑圈子里，他扮演着一个有利可图的角色，好比五十年前包伙饭店的领班侍者。他的殷勤和教养是一笔资产，他用到了炉火纯青。他懂得利用别人的不老实，也利用自己的不老实——论给主顾排解婚姻纠纷、办得人人满意，谁的手腕也不及他。

===Original===
“That livid face there, set in a frame of whiskers turning grey, that miniature mouth, that lustreless eye? No one knows who he really is! For a long time sinister rumors have been current about his person, rumors of bloody affairs. At first people were afraid of him and tried to keep away from him. It’s nothing but an old memory after all! For the rest, he spends a lot of money. What does it matter if a few drops of blood do roll on top of piles of gold! Women are crazy about it.

===Chinese===
“那边那张青灰的脸，镶在一圈花白的连鬓胡子里，那张小小的嘴，那双无光的眼睛？没人知道他的真正来历！很久以来，他身上流传着种种阴森的传闻，血案的传闻。起初人们怕他，躲着他。到头来，也不过是一段旧闻罢了！再说，他花钱花得海了去。几滴血滚在成堆的金子顶上，又有什么打紧！女人们为这个都疯了。

===Original===
“That young handsome man with mustache gracefully turned up? One day when he did not have a sou to his name, his parents having stopped his allowance, the ingenious notion occurred to him to pretend that he was repentant. He demonstratively quit an old mistress he had and returned to his parents. A young lady, his playmate when he was a child, adored him. She was rich. He married her. But on the very evening of his wedding day he left her, taking the dowry with him, and went back to his old mistress. She is an excellent woman,” added Jesselin, “she really is!

===Chinese===
“那个漂亮的年轻人，两撇小胡子翘得多潇洒？有一天，他一个苏也没有了——家里断了他的津贴——灵机一动，装出一副痛改前非的样子。他大张旗鼓地甩了旧情妇，回到父母身边。有位小姐，他小时候的玩伴，一心恋慕他。她有钱。他娶了她。可就在成亲当天的晚上，他卷了嫁妆离开她，回到旧情妇那里去了。她是个顶好的女人，”热瑟兰补了一句，“真的！”

===Original===
“And all those pimps and those who have been chased out of the clubs, expelled from universities, ruined at the stock exchange; and foreigners coming from the devil knows where, whom one scandal attracts to one place and another scandal draws off to another, and those living outside the pale of the law and bourgeois esteem, who claim to be the royalty of Paris, before whom everybody bows down﻿—they all swarm here, arrogant, free, disreputable!”

===Chinese===
“还有所有那些拉皮条的、被俱乐部赶出来的、被大学开除的、在证券交易所破了产的；还有天知道从哪儿冒出来的外国人，一桩丑闻把他们引到一处，另一桩丑闻又把他们勾到另一处；还有那些活在法律和体面之外、自称巴黎的王侯、人人见了都要哈腰的人物——他们全在这儿麇集，趾高气扬，自由自在，声名狼藉！”

===Original===
Juliette was listening, amused by the stories, attracted by this filth and crime, flattered by this ignoble homage which she felt the glances of these fools and criminals were paying her. But she preserved her modest bearing, her maiden charm, all her graces, self-conscious and inviting at one and the same time, for the sake of which, one day at Lirat’s, I earned damnation!

===Chinese===
朱丽叶特听着，让这些故事逗得乐不可支，让这片污秽和罪恶性住了，让这份下流的敬意奉承着——她觉出这些蠢货和罪犯的目光正向她行着这样的敬礼。可她仍旧守着她端庄的举止、她少女的风致、她全部的可人之处，又自觉、又撩人；而为了这一切，我当年在利拉的画室里，挣来了自己的万劫不复！

===Original===
Faces grow more pallid now, features become drawn out. Fatigue swells and colors the eyelids. One after another they leave the cabaret, tired and worried. Do they know what the next day has in store for them, what troubles await them, what disasters lie in ambush for them? Once in a while the report of a pistol shot creates a void in the ranks of this gang! Perhaps tomorrow will be their turn? Tomorrow! Perhaps it will be my turn, too? Ah! Tomorrow! The ever present menace of tomorrow! And we go home again, without saying a word to one another, sad and weary.

===Chinese===
这会儿，面孔更苍白了，五官拉长了。倦意把眼皮泡肿了，染上颜色。他们一个接一个离开酒馆，疲惫，忧心。他们可知道明天给他们存下了什么，什么烦恼在等着他们，什么灾祸在半路上埋伏着他们？偶尔一声枪响，会在这伙人的行伍里空出一个位置！也许明天就轮到他们？明天！也许也轮到我？啊，明天！这永远悬在头顶的明天的威胁！于是我们又回家去，彼此一句话也不说，又愁又乏。

===Original===
The boulevard was deserted. An immense silence was weighing heavily over the city. Only the windows of the brothel houses were aglare, like the eyes of some huge beasts crouching in the depth of night.

Without knowing exactly the state of my financial affairs, I felt that ruin was ahead of me. I had paid out considerable sums of money, debts were accumulating and, far from decreasing, Juliette’s whims became even more numerous and more expensive: money flowed like water from her hands, like a fountain, in one continuous stream. “She evidently thinks me richer than I am,” I thought to myself, in an effort to deceive myself. “I ought to warn her, perhaps show myself a little more reserved in yielding to her desires.” The truth was that I deliberately dismissed from my mind every notion of this kind, that I dreaded the probable consequences of such a challenge even more than the greatest possible misfortune in the world.

===Chinese===
林荫大道上阒无一人。一片浩大的沉默沉沉压在这座城上。只有妓院的窗子还亮着，像蹲伏在黑夜深处的巨兽的眼睛。

财务上我虽说不清确切的光景，却觉出破产就在前头。我已经付掉了大笔的钱，债越积越多；朱丽叶特的心思不但不见收敛，反而花样更繁多、开销更浩大：钱像水一样从她手里流走，像一道泉，连绵不断。“她显然把我看得比实际上有钱，”我暗自思忖，想骗过自己，“我该提醒她的，或许还该在满足她的心愿时收敛一点。”实情却是，这一类的念头我一概存心从脑子里赶出去：比起世上可能降临的最大不幸，我更怕这么一场摊牌可能招来的后果。

===Original===
In my rare moments of clear-mindedness, of frankness with myself, I understood that beneath her air of sweetness, beneath her naivete of a spoiled child, beneath the robust and vibrant passions of her flesh Juliette concealed a powerful desire to be always beautiful, adored, paid court to, concealed a fierce selfishness which would not flinch before any cruelty, before any moral crime!﻿ ﻿… I realized that she loved me less than the last piece of cloth, that she would have sacrificed me for a cloak or a cravat or a pair of gloves.﻿ ﻿… Once drawn into such a life she could not stop.﻿ ﻿… And then what?﻿ ﻿… Cold shivers passed up and down my frame from head to heels.﻿ ﻿… That she should leave me, no, no, that I did not want!

===Chinese===
在我难得清醒、难得对自己坦白的时刻，我懂得了：在她那副甜蜜的神气底下，在她那被宠坏的孩子似的天真底下，在她肉体那健旺而炽烈的情欲底下，朱丽叶特藏着一股强劲的欲念——要永远美丽，永远受人崇拜、受人追求——藏着一副凶狠的自私，什么残忍、什么丧德败行都吓不退它！……我懂了，她爱我，还不及爱一块衣料；为一件斗篷、一条领带或一副手套，她都肯把我牺牲掉。……一旦卷进这样的生活，她是拔不出脚的。……到那时怎么办？……一阵阵寒战从我周身滚过，从头顶到脚跟。……她离开我——不，不，这个我不要！

===Original===
The most painful moment to me was in the morning when I woke up. With eyes closed, pulling the cover over my head, my body huddled up into a ball, I used to ponder over my situation with terrible anguish. And the more faulty she appeared to me, the more desperately I clung to Juliette. No matter how often I said to myself that my money would soon be gone, that the credit on which I could dishonestly prolong the agony of hope against hope for another week or two, would eventually be denied to me; I clung to the present and rabidly evolved all sorts of impossible plans. I pictured myself accomplishing superhuman tasks in the course of one week. I dreamed of finding millions in some hackney coach. Fabulous inheritances dropped down from the skies for me. The idea of stealing haunted me.﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===
最难熬的是早晨醒来的那一刻。我闭着眼睛，把被子拉过头顶，身子缩成一团，怀着可怕的焦虑盘算自己的处境。她在我想象中显得过错越重，我就越是不顾死活地抓住朱丽叶特不放。任凭我对自己说多少遍：钱很快就要见底；我凭信用勉强拖着，把万分之一的指望再赖上一个礼拜、两个礼拜，信用终究也要跟我翻脸——我还是死抓住眼前不放，发了狂似的盘算出种种不可能的计划。我想象自己在一个礼拜之内完成超人的勋业。我梦见在哪辆出租马车里拾到百万巨款。天大的遗产从天上掉下来砸中我。偷窃的念头缠住了我。……

===Original===
Gradually all these insane notions took hold of my distracted mind. I was presenting Juliette with palaces and castles; I overwhelmed her with diamonds and pearls; gold streamed and glittered all around her, and I raised her high above the earth, upon dizzy, royal heights. Then the sense of reality would suddenly return. I buried myself deeper in the bed. I sought realms of nonexistence in whose depth I could disappear. I forced myself to sleep. And suddenly, out of breath, with sweat on my forehead and a haggard look in my eyes, I would snuggle up to Juliette, press her in my arms with all my strength, sobbing:

===Chinese===
渐渐地，这一切实疯狂的念头把我那失魂落魄的头脑攥在了手心。我把宫殿和城堡献给朱丽叶特；我用钻石和珍珠把她压得喘不过气；黄金在她四周流淌、闪耀，我把她高高举到大地之上，举上令人晕眩的帝王般的高处。随后，现实的感觉忽然回来了。我往被窝深处埋得更深。我寻觅种种“不存在”的疆域，好让自己消失在它们的深处。我强迫自己入睡。而突然，我气喘吁吁，额头冒汗，眼里带着惶惶的神色，蹭到朱丽叶特身边，用尽全身力气把她搂在怀里，呜咽着：

===Original===
“You’ll never leave me, will you, my Juliette! Tell me, tell me that you’ll never leave me. Because, you see.﻿ ﻿… I’ll die﻿ ﻿… if you do﻿—I’ll go crazy. I’ll kill myself! Juliette, I swear to you that I’ll kill myself!”

“Why, what has come over you? Why do you tremble so? No, my dear, I’ll never leave you. Are we not happy together? Besides, I love you so much! When you are nice as you are now!”

“Yes, yes! I’ll kill myself! I’ll kill myself!”

“You are so funny, my dear! Why do you tell me that?”

“Because.”

I was going to tell her everything.﻿ ﻿… But I had not the courage. And I said:

===Chinese===
“你永远不会离开我的，是不是，我的朱丽叶特！告诉我，告诉我你永远不会离开我。因为，你要知道。……你要是走……我会死的……我会发疯。我会自杀的！朱丽叶特，我向你发誓，我会自杀的！”

“咦，你怎么啦？干么抖成这样？不会的，亲爱的，我永远不会离开你。我们在一起不是挺快活么？再说我多爱你！尤其是你像现在这么乖的时候！”

“会的，会的！我会自杀的！我会自杀的！”

“你真逗，亲爱的！干么说这个呀？”

“不为什么。”

我本想把一切都告诉她。……可我没有那份勇气。于是我说：

===Original===
“Because I love you! Because I don’t want you to leave me! Because I don’t want to.”

Nevertheless I finally had to bring this matter to a head. Juliette had seen in the window of a jewelry store on the Rue de la Paix, a string of pearls of which she spoke without end. One day when we were in that neighborhood:

“Let’s go and see that beautiful jewel,” she said to me.

With her nose pressed against the windowpane and eyes shining, she looked at the string arranged in a triple circular row of pink pearls upon the velvet of the jewel case. I saw a tremor passing up and down her skin.

“Isn’t that beautiful? And it isn’t expensive at all! I have asked about the price﻿ ﻿… fifty thousand francs.﻿ ﻿… That’s an exceptional bargain.”

I tried to draw her further on. But coaxingly, hanging on my arm, she held me back. And she sighed:

===Chinese===
“因为我爱你！因为我不愿你离开我！因为我不愿意。”

尽管如此，这件事终究到了非了结不可的地步。朱丽叶特在和平街一家珠宝店的橱窗里见过一串珍珠，从此念叨个没完。有一天，我们正好走到那一带：

“咱们去看看那件漂亮首饰吧，”她对我说。

她鼻子贴着橱窗玻璃，眼睛发亮，望着首饰盒丝绒上那串粉红珍珠排成的三重珠圈。我看见一阵战栗顺着她的皮肤滚过。

“多美呀！而且一点儿也不贵！我问过价钱了……五万法郎。……真是难得捡的便宜。”

我想拉她往前走。可她撒着娇，挽住我的胳膊，把我拽住。她叹了口气：

===Original===
“Ah, how nice that would look on the neck of your little wifie!”

She added with an air of profound grief:

“Really! All the women have lots of jewels. Only I have none. If you were really nice, really kind to me, you would give them to your poor little Juliette.﻿ ﻿… There now!”

I stammered out:

“Certainly. I want to﻿—very much﻿ ﻿… but later﻿ ﻿… next week!”

===Chinese===
“啊，这戴在您小媳妇儿的脖子上，该多好看呐！”

她又摆出一副深切悲伤的神气，添上一句：

“真的！人家的女人个个都有好多首饰。就我一个人没有。您要是真心疼我、真心对我好，就该把首饰送给您可怜的小朱丽叶特。……喏！”

我结结巴巴地说：

“当然。我想送的——非常想……只是缓一缓……下礼拜！”

===Original===
Juliette’s face grew dark:

“Why next week? Can’t you do it now, right now!”

“Well you see﻿ ﻿… now﻿ ﻿… I am short of money.﻿ ﻿… I am a little hard up.”

“What? Already? You haven’t got a sou? Is that a fact? Where did all your money go? You have not a sou left?”

“Why yes, I have! Only I am a little short of cash temporarily.”

“Well if that’s the case it doesn’t matter. I have also made inquiries about the terms. They would agree to accept promissory notes. Five notes of six thousand francs each. That is not such a mighty matter!”

===Chinese===
朱丽叶特的脸沉了下来：

“干么要下礼拜？现在就不行么，马上！”

“这个……眼下……我手头紧。……有点儿拮据。”

“什么？这就没钱了？您一个苏也没有了？真的？您的钱都上哪儿去了？您一个苏也不剩了？”

“哪能呢，我有！只是眼下一时周转不开。”

“哦，要是这样，那不要紧。条件我也打听过了。他们答应收期票。五张，每张六千法郎。这算得什么了不得的大事！”

===Original===
“Undoubtedly. But a little later! I promise you. Is that all right?”

“Ah!” Juliette said simply.

I looked at her, the wrinkle on her forehead terrified me; I saw a hidden glimmer flare up in her eyes, and in the space of a second a world of extraordinary sensations hitherto unknown to me, took hold of me. Very clearly, with perfect understanding, with cruel indifference, with a startling conciseness of judgment I put the following question to myself: “Juliette and dishonor; Juliette and prison?” I did not hesitate.

===Chinese===
“自然。只是缓一缓！我向你保证。行不行？”

“啊！”朱丽叶特只应了一声。

我望着她；她额上那道皱纹吓着了我；我看见一点藏着的火光在她眼里蹿起来，而一秒钟之间，一个我从未见识过的、异样的感官世界把我攫住了。清清楚楚地，带着全然的了然、冷酷的淡漠、惊人的判断之简洁，我向自己提出了这样一个问题：“朱丽叶特，还是耻辱；朱丽叶特，还是监狱？”我没有犹豫。

===Original===
“Let’s go in,” I said.

She took the string of pearls away with her.

In the evening, wearing her pearls, she sat down on my lap, radiant, with her arms closed around my neck. She sat so for a long time, lulling me with her sweet voice.

“Ah, my poor sweetie,” she said, “I am not always sensible! Yes, I realize. I am a little foolish sometimes. But I am through now! I want to be a good, a serious-minded woman. And you shall work undisturbed, you’ll write a good novel﻿—a nice play. Then we shall be rich, very rich. And then if you should happen to be very much short of money we could sell this beautiful string of pearls! Because jewels are not like dresses, they are just as good as money. Press me in your arms strongly.”

Ah! how fast that night was gone! How the hours sped by, no doubt frightened to hear love shrieking with a horrible voice of one who is damned.

===Chinese===
“进去吧，”我说。

她把那串珍珠带走了。

当天晚上，她戴着珍珠，坐到我的膝盖上，容光焕发，双臂搂住我的脖子。她这样坐了很久，用她甜蜜的声音哄着我。

“啊，我可怜的心肝，”她说，“我并不总是懂事的！是，我知道。我有时候有点儿傻。可我这回改了！我要做个正经的、有脑子的女人。您呢，安安心心地工作，写一部好小说——一出好戏。到时候我们就富了，富得很。往后您要是真的手头太紧，咱们还可以把这串漂亮的珍珠卖掉！首饰可不比衣裳，跟现钱一样顶用。使劲把我搂紧点儿。”

啊，那一夜过得真快！时辰飞驰而过，想必是吓坏了——它们听见爱情正用一个受诅咒者的可怖嗓音嘶喊。

===Original===
Disasters followed one another and soon reached their climax. The promissory notes that I had given Juliette’s jeweler remained unpaid. I had a hard time borrowing enough money to satisfy our everyday needs. My father had left some uncollected debts at Saint-Michel. Generous and kindhearted, he liked to help out small farmers in a pinch. Pitilessly I started the process servers after these poor devils, causing them to sell their hovels, their piece of land, the things with the aid of which they made a miserable living, depriving themselves of everything. In the shops where I still had credit I bought things which I immediately resold at a very low price. I stooped to putting through the most questionable deals. My brains teemed with original plans of blackmail, and I tired Jesselin with my endless requests of money. Finally one day I went to see Lirat. I needed five hundred francs that evening, and I went to Lirat, deliberately, boldly! In his presence, however, in that studio full of painful memories, my self-assurance deserted me and I felt a sense of belated shame. I was with Lirat a quarter of an hour, without venturing to explain to him the thing that I expected of his friendship.﻿ ﻿… Of his friendship!﻿ ﻿… At last I made up my mind to go.

===Chinese===
灾祸接踵而至，很快就到了顶点。我开给朱丽叶特那家珠宝商的期票到期未付。我费了九牛二虎之力东挪西借，才凑上日常的开销。我父亲在圣米歇尔留下几笔没有讨还的债。他心地宽厚，喜欢周济一时周转不灵的小农户。我却毫不容情地把执达员派去追索这些可怜虫，逼得他们卖掉小屋，卖掉那一小块赖以糊口的地，卖掉他们省吃俭用攒下的一切。还在给我赊账的铺子里，我买进货物，转身就贱价卖掉。我堕落到去做最可疑的买卖。我脑子里塞满了种种别出心裁的敲诈计划；我缠着热瑟兰要钱，把他缠得筋疲力尽。终于有一天，我去找利拉。那天晚上我需要五百法郎，我去了利拉家——存心的，豁出去的！可是到了他面前，在那间盛满辛酸回忆的画室里，我的胆气泄了，一阵迟来的羞愧涌了上来。我在利拉家坐了一刻钟，却不敢向他开口，说明我指望他这份友谊给我什么。……他的友谊啊！……我终于拿定了主意要走。

===Original===
“Well, goodbye, Lirat!”

“Goodbye, my friend.”

“Ah! I forgot. Could you lend me five hundred francs? I am expecting my farm rents. They are overdue.”

And I added rapidly:

“I’ll give them back to you tomorrow﻿—tomorrow morning.”

Lirat fixed his glance on me for a moment. I can still see that glance. It was truly sorrowful.

===Chinese===
“那么，再见，利拉！”

“再见，我的朋友。”

“啊！我忘了。你能借我五百法郎吗？我等着田庄的租金呢。已经过期了。”

我又急急地补上一句：

“明天就还你——明天一早就还。”

利拉的目光在我身上定了一刻。我至今还看得见那目光。那目光里满是真正的悲哀。

===Original===
“Five hundred francs!” he said. “Where in the devil do you expect me to get them? Have I ever had five hundred francs?”

I insisted, repeating:

“I’ll give them back to you tomorrow﻿ ﻿… tomorrow morning.”

“But I haven’t got them, my poor Mintié. I have only two hundred francs left. Would that do you any good?”

I was thinking that these two hundred francs which he offered meant to him a whole month’s subsistence. I answered with a bleeding heart:

“Well, all right! All the same! I’ll give them back to you tomorrow﻿ ﻿… tomorrow morning.”

“That is all right!”

===Chinese===
“五百法郎！”他说。“你叫我上天入地哪儿去弄？我几时有过五百法郎？”

我还坚持着，一遍遍重复：

“明天就还你……明天一早就还。”

“可我没有啊，我可怜的曼蒂埃。我只剩两百法郎了。那对你有用么？”

我心想，他让给我的这两百法郎，是他整整一个月的生计。我心口淌着血，答道：

“好，行！就这么着！明天就还你……明天一早就还。”

“这就对了！”

===Original===
At that moment I would have wished to throw myself on Lirat’s neck, to beg his pardon, to shout: “No, no I don’t want this money!” And like a thief I took it away with me.

===Chinese===
那一刻，我真想扑到利拉的脖子上，求他饶恕，喊出声来：“不，不，这钱我不要！”可我像个贼一样，把钱拿走了。

## VII / 七

===Original===
My properties, the Priory itself, the old familiar house mortgaged several times, were sold!﻿ ﻿… Ah! the sad journey which I made on that occasion!﻿ ﻿… It was a long time since I had been to Saint-Michel! And yet in my hours of disgust and weariness, in the evil excitement of Paris, the thought of this peaceful little place was sweet and calming. The pure wafts of air which came to me from there had a refreshing effect upon my congested brains, they soothed my heart burned by the corrosive acids which are carried along by the infected air of cities, and I often promised myself that whenever I got tired of always chasing dreams, I would seek refuge there amid the peace and serenity of native objects.﻿ ﻿… Saint-Michel!﻿ ﻿… Never was the place so dear to me as after I had left it; it seemed to me that it contained riches and beauty such as I had never known how to enjoy and which I now suddenly discovered﻿ ﻿… I loved to direct my memories there, best of all I loved to recall the forest, the beautiful forest where, as a restless, dreamy child, I had lost my way so many times.﻿ ﻿… Inhaling with keen delight the aroma of the rich sap of trees, the ear enchanted by the harmonies of the wind which caused the underwood and forest trees to vibrate like harps and violin cellos, I lost myself in the large alleys overhung with trembling foliage, large, straight alleys which far, far away ended abruptly and opened up like a church bay upon the light of a pane of sky, arched and luminous.﻿ ﻿…

In these dreams I saw the branches of oak trees reach out their foliage greener than ever, happy to find me again; young staddles greeted me with a joyous rustle as I passed by; they seemed to say to me: “Look how big we grew, how smooth and strong our trunks are, how good the air in which we spread out our slender, swaying boughs, how bountiful the soil in which we sink our roots always full of life﻿—giving sap.” The moss and peat mould called me: “We have prepared a nice bed for you, little fellow, a nice fragrant little bed such as you won’t find in the miserably gilded houses of the big cities.﻿ ﻿… Stretch yourself out, roll on it if you are too warm, the fern will sway its gentle fans over your head, the beech trees will spread their branches open to let through a sunbeam which will gladden your heart.” Alas! ever since I fell in love with Juliette﻿—these voices have gradually become silent. These memories no longer came back like guardian angels to lull me to sleep and to gently stir their white wings in the agitated azure of my dreams!﻿ ﻿… My past had become estranged from me, ashamed of me!﻿ ﻿…

The train sped on; it had cleared the plains of Beauce, even more melancholy to look at than in the grim days of the war.﻿ ﻿… And I recognized the small, humpy fields, their hedges of brushwood, the scattered apple trees, the narrow valleys, the poplars with their tops bent in the shape of hoods, which in the fields resembled a strange procession of blue penitents, the farms with high mossgrown roofs, highways deep cut and rough, bordered with girdled trees, which slanted down in the midst of sturdy verdure, the woods down yonder, black against the setting sun.﻿ ﻿… It was getting dark when I arrived at Saint-Michel. I liked it better so.﻿ ﻿… To cross the streets in full daylight, under the gaze of all these excellent people who had known me as a child, would have been too painful for me.﻿ ﻿… It seemed to me that I was laden with so much shame that they would turn away from me with horror as from a mangy dog.﻿ ﻿… I quickened my pace, rolling up the collar of my overcoat.﻿ ﻿… The grocery owner, named Madame Henriette, who in the past used to stuff me with cake, was standing in front of her store and talking to her neighbors.﻿ ﻿… I was afraid they might be talking about me and, leaving the sidewalk I took to the roadway.﻿ ﻿… Fortunately a cart passed by, the noise of which drowned the words of these women: The Presbytery﻿ ﻿… the Convent of the Sisters﻿ ﻿… the church﻿ ﻿… the Priory!﻿ ﻿… At this hour the Priory was nothing but a huge black mass in the sky.﻿ ﻿… My heart failed me.﻿ ﻿… I had to lean against one of the posts of the gate to catch my breath.﻿ ﻿… A few steps away the forest murmured, its dull voice growing in amplitude, angry, like the raging roar of breakers.﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===
我的田产，连同修道院老宅本身——那座熟稔的、抵押过好几道的老宅——都卖掉了！……啊，那一回的旅程，何等凄凉！……我好久没有回圣米歇尔了！然而，每逢厌倦与腻烦袭来的时候，每逢在巴黎恶浊的亢奋里，一想到这处安宁的小地方，心里便是甜的、静的。从那边飘来的纯净气息，能叫我充血发胀的头脑为之一爽，能抚慰我这颗被城市秽气里的酸毒蚀烫了的心；我常常暗自许愿：一旦追逐幻梦追逐得倦了，就退到那里去，栖身于故乡风物的安宁与静谧之中。……圣米歇尔！……我离开它之后，它从未这样亲过；我总觉得那里藏着说不尽的富丽与美好，是从前的我不懂得消受、如今才蓦然发现的……我爱把回忆引到那里去，最爱回想那片森林，那片美丽的森林——小时候我是个躁动而多梦的孩子，多少次在那里迷了路。……我贪婪地吸着树木饱满汁液的芬芳，耳朵听着风的和鸣而心醉神迷——那风把灌木和乔木拨得像竖琴和大提琴一般震颤——我在绿荫摇曳的宽阔林荫道里走失了自己；那些道路又宽又直，远远地、远远地陡然收住，像教堂的一扇券窗，豁然敞向一弯拱形的、明亮的蓝天。……

在这些梦里，我看见橡树的枝条伸出比往日更绿的叶丛，为重又觅到我而欢喜；年轻的小树在我走过时沙沙作响，向我致意；它们仿佛对我说：“瞧我们长得多大了，树干多么光滑、多么结实；我们舒开纤细摇曳的枝条，这里的空气多么好；我们把根须扎进永远涌着生命之汁的沃土里，这土壤多么丰厚。”苔藓和泥炭也唤我：“小家伙，我们给你铺好了一张好床，一张香喷喷的小床，大城市里那些寒酸的金屋里可找不出这样的床。……躺下来吧，热了就在上面打个滚；羊齿草会朝你头顶轻轻摇起它温柔的扇子，山毛榉会敞开枝条，放进一缕阳光，叫你心里欢喜。”唉！自从我爱上朱丽叶特——这些声音便渐渐沉寂了。这些记忆再不像守护天使那般回来哄我入睡，再不在我梦境纷乱的碧空里轻轻扇动它们白白的翅膀了！……我的过去疏远了我，为我羞惭！……

火车飞驰，已经驶过了博斯（Beauce）平原——比起战争的惨淡岁月，眼前的平原看着愈发愁惨。……我认出了那些高低不平的小块田地、丛木编的篱笆、东一株西一株散立的苹果树、狭长的谷地；还有那些杨树，树顶弯成兜帽模样，立在田野里，像一队古怪的蓝衣苦修士；苔痕斑斑的高顶农舍；深深下切、坎坷不平的大路，路旁夹着被环剥过树皮的树，在茁壮的苍翠中间倾斜而下；远处的小树林，衬着落日，一片黢黑。……到达圣米歇尔时，天擦黑了。这样倒更好。……大白天里穿过街道，在看着我长大的善良乡邻的注视下走过，对我未免太痛苦了。……我自觉满身耻辱，他们准会骇然避开我，像躲一条癞皮狗似的。……我加快脚步，竖起大衣领子。……杂货店的女主人亨利埃特（Henriette）太太——从前她总拿糕点塞满我的口袋——正站在店门前同邻居们说话。……我怕她们谈的正是我，便离开人行道，走到了车行道上。……幸亏一辆大车驶过，轰隆声盖过了这些女人的话音：本堂神父住宅……修女院……教堂……修道院老宅！……这个时辰，修道院老宅只是天幕底下一大团黑影。……我的心一下沉了下去。……我只好扶住大门的一根门柱，喘一口气。……几步之外，森林在低语，那喑哑的声音愈来愈壮阔、愈来愈忿怒，像拍岸惊涛的怒吼。……

===Original===
Marie and Felix were waiting for me.﻿ ﻿… Marie older and more wrinkled, Felix, more stooping and shaking his head more than ever.﻿ ﻿…

“Ah! Monsieur Jean!﻿ ﻿… Monsieur Jean!﻿ ﻿…” And forthwith taking possession of my valise, Marie said:

“You ought to be pretty hungry by this time, Monsieur Jean!﻿ ﻿… I have some soup for you, the kind you used to like, and then I have put a nice chicken on the spit.”

“Thank you!” I said. “I shall not dine.”

I would have liked to embrace both of them, to open my arms for them, to cry upon their old, parched faces.﻿ ﻿… And instead! my voice was harsh, trenchant. I uttered “I shall not dine” in the manner of a threat. They looked at me somewhat frightened, but never stopped repeating:

===Chinese===
玛丽和费利克斯在等我。……玛丽老了，皱纹多了；费利克斯背更驼了，脑袋也比从前抖得更厉害了。……

“啊！让先生！……让先生！……”玛丽一边念叨，一边当即接过我的手提箱：

“您这会儿准饿坏了吧，让先生！……我给您备了汤，就是您从前爱喝的那种；还有一只嫩鸡，也已经在烤叉上烤着了。”

“谢谢！”我说，“我不吃晚饭了。”

我本想搂住他们两个，向他们张开臂膀，在他们干瘪苍老的脸上哭一场。……可我竟没有！我的嗓音又生硬又斩截，“我不吃晚饭了”几个字，说得像一句威胁。他们有些惊惶地望着我，嘴里却仍不住地念叨：

===Original===
“Ah! Monsieur Jean!﻿ ﻿… It has been such a long time!﻿ ﻿… Ah! Monsieur Jean!﻿ ﻿… What a handsome young man you are!﻿ ﻿…”

Then Marie, thinking that she would gain my interest thereby, began telling me the news of the place:

“That poor Monsieur the curé is dead, you know. The new one in his place don’t seem to be getting ahead at all, he is too young and anxious.﻿ ﻿… Baptiste has been crushed to death by a tree.”

I interrupted her:

“All right, all right, Marie.﻿ ﻿… You’ll tell me about it tomorrow.”

===Chinese===
“啊！让先生！……这么些年不见！……啊！让先生！……您出落成多俊的年轻人啦！……”

接着，玛丽以为这样能引起我的兴致，便给我讲起地方上的新闻来：

“您知道吧，可怜的本堂神父先生去世了。顶他位置的那位新的，好像怎么也撑不起来，人太年轻，又太性急。……巴蒂斯特（Baptiste）叫一棵树压死了。”

我打断了她：

“好了，好了，玛丽。……这些事明天再讲吧。”

===Original===
She took me to my bedroom and asked:

“Shall I bring you a bowl of milk, Monsieur Jean?”

“If you please!”

And closing the door, I flung myself on the lounge and sobbed for a long, long time.

The next day I got up at dawn.﻿ ﻿… The Priory had not changed much: there was only more grass in the alleys, more moss on the steps, and a few trees were dead. Again I saw the gate, the scurfy lawn, the puny looking sorbs, the aged chestnut trees. Again I saw the basin where the little kitten had been shot, the curtain of fir trees which hid the commons from view, the abandoned study; I saw the park, its twisted trees and stone benches that looked like ancient tombs.﻿ ﻿… In the kitchen garden Felix was digging a border bed for flowers.﻿ ﻿… Ah! poor man, how battered his frame was!

===Chinese===
她领我到卧房，问：

“给您端一碗牛奶来好吗，让先生？”

“劳驾！”

随后我关上门，扑倒在长榻上，呜呜咽咽哭了很久很久。

第二天我天不亮就起来了。……修道院老宅没怎么变样：只是小径上草多了，台阶上苔厚了，还有几棵树死了。我又看见那扇铁门、那块斑秃的草坪、那两棵寒碜的花楸、那些老迈的栗树。我又看见那只水池——那只小猫当初就是在这里挨的枪——看见遮住公地的那道杉树帷幕、荒废的事务所；我看见那座园子，树木虬曲，石凳东一处西一处地隆起，像一座座古墓。……菜园里，费利克斯正在给花坛翻土培边。……啊！可怜的人，他的身子骨磨毁成什么样子了！

===Original===
He showed me a hawthorn and said:

“That is where you used to come with your poor deceased father to lie in wait for the blackbirds.﻿ ﻿… Do you remember, Monsieur Jean?”

“Yes, yes, Felix!”

“And also the thrush?”

“Yes, yes, Felix!”

I walked away. I could not bear the sight of this old man any longer, this man who thought he was going to live to the end of his days at the Priory and whom I was about to drive out﻿ ﻿… and where was he to go?﻿ ﻿… He had served us faithfully, he was almost one of our family, poor, unable to gain a livelihood otherwise. And I was going to chase him out!﻿ ﻿… Ah! How could I bring myself to do that?

===Chinese===
他指给我看一株山楂，说：

“从前您常同您可怜的先父到这儿来，守着打乌鸫。……您还记得吗，让先生？”

“记得，记得，费利克斯！”

“还有斑鸫呢？”

“记得，记得，费利克斯！”

我走开了。我再也不忍看这老人一眼了——他以为自己会在修道院老宅待到寿终正寝，而我马上就要把他赶出门去……他能去哪儿呢？……他忠心耿耿地伺候了我们一辈子，差不多就算我们家的人了，又穷，除这个再没有别的生计。而我竟要把他赶出去！……啊！我怎么能狠得下这个心？

===Original===
At breakfast Marie seemed nervous. She walked around my chair, unusually excited.

“Beg pardon!” she said to me at last, “I must clear up all my doubts about this matter.﻿ ﻿… Is it true that you are selling the Priory?﻿ ﻿…”

“Yes, Marie.”

The old woman opened wide her eyes, stupefied, and, placing her hands on the table, repeated:

“You are selling the Priory?”

“Yes, Marie.”

===Chinese===
吃早饭时，玛丽显得心神不宁。她围着我的椅子转来转去，激动得反常。

“恕我多嘴！”她终于对我说，“这件事，我得问个明白。……您当真要把修道院老宅卖掉？……”

“是真的，玛丽。”

老太太瞪圆了眼睛，呆住了，双手按在桌上，又说了一遍：

“您要卖修道院老宅？”

“是真的，玛丽。”

===Original===
“The Priory where all your family was born?﻿ ﻿… The Priory where your father and your mother died?﻿ ﻿… The Priory, Holy Jesus!”

“Yes, Marie.”

She recoiled as if frightened.

“Then you are a wicked son, Monsieur Jean!”

I made no reply. Marie left the dining room and did not speak to me anymore.

===Chinese===
“您一家人出生的那座老宅？……您父亲、您母亲去世的那座老宅？……修道院老宅哪，圣耶稣啊！”

“是真的，玛丽。”

她直往后缩，像是吓着了。

“那您就是个忤逆的儿子，让先生！”

我没有回答。玛丽走出饭厅，再没同我说一句话。

===Original===
Two days later, my business having been attended to, the deed signed, I left.﻿ ﻿… My money was hardly enough to last me a month.﻿ ﻿… I was done for! Overwhelming debts, ignoble debts was all that was left to me!﻿ ﻿… Ah! if the train could only carry me on and on, always further on, never to arrive anywhere!﻿ ﻿… It was only in Paris that I reminded myself that I had not even gone to kneel down at the grave of my father and mother.

Juliette received me tenderly. She embraced me passionately.

“Ah! dear, dear!﻿ ﻿… I thought you would never come back!﻿ ﻿… Five days, just think of it!﻿ ﻿… Next time if you have to go again I want to go with you.”

She appeared so affectionate, so truly moved, her caresses gave me such confidence, and then the burden on my soul was so heavy, that I did not hesitate to tell her everything. I took her in my arms and put her on my lap.

===Chinese===
两天之后，事情办妥，契约签过，我便走了。……我手里的钱，勉强够撑一个月。……我算完了！留给我的是压垮人的债、卑贱的债，再没有别的！……啊！这火车要是能载着我一直开下去，愈开愈远，永远不到站，该多好！……直到回了巴黎，我才想起，我竟没有到父母坟前去跪一跪。

朱丽叶特温柔地迎住我，热烈地搂抱我。

“啊！亲爱的，亲爱的！……我还当你不回来了呢！……整整五天哪，你想想！……下回你再要出门，我要跟你一起去。”

她显得那样温存、那样真心动情，她的爱抚给了我那样的信心，加之我灵魂上的担子又那么沉重，我便毫不迟疑地把一切都告诉了她。我把她搂进怀里，抱到膝上。

===Original===
“Listen to me, my Juliette,” I said to her, “listen to me!﻿ ﻿… I am lost﻿ ﻿… ruined﻿ ﻿… ruined﻿ ﻿… do you hear, ruined!﻿ ﻿… We have only four thousand francs left!﻿ ﻿…”

“Poor boy!” Juliette sighed while placing her head on my shoulder, “poor boy!﻿ ﻿…”

I burst out sobbing, and cried out:

“You understand now that I must leave you.﻿ ﻿… And I am going to die if I do!”

“Come now, you are silly to talk that way.﻿ ﻿… Do you believe I could live without you, my dear?﻿ ﻿… Come now, don’t cry, don’t grieve so much.﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
“听我说，我的朱丽叶特，”我对她说，“听我说！……我完了……毁了……毁了……你听见没有，毁了！……咱们只剩四千法郎了！……”

“可怜的孩子！”朱丽叶特把头靠在我肩上，叹道，“可怜的孩子！……”

我放声抽噎起来，喊道：

“如今你明白了，我必须离开你。……可我一离开你，就活不成了！”

“得了，瞧你说这些傻话。……你以为离了你，我还活得下去吗，亲爱的？……好了，别哭了，别这样难过。……”

===Original===
She dried the tears from my eyes and continued in her voice which grew sweeter with every word.

“First of all we have four thousand francs.﻿ ﻿… We can live four months on that.﻿ ﻿… During these four months you’ll work.﻿ ﻿… Let us see if you can’t write a good novel in four months!﻿ ﻿… But don’t cry, because if you cry, I won’t tell you a great secret﻿ ﻿… a great, great secret.﻿ ﻿… Do you know what your little wifie did, who little suspected that herself﻿—do you know?﻿ ﻿… Well, for three days she went to the riding school, she took lessons in horsemanship﻿—and next year when she is well trained, Franconi will engage her.﻿ ﻿… Do you know what a woman rider in a fashionable riding school makes.﻿ ﻿… Two thousand, three thousand francs a month!﻿ ﻿… You see now, there isn’t much to grieve over, my poor little boy!”

All nonsense, all folly seemed logical to me. I clung to it desperately as a shipwrecked sailor clings to the insecure wreckage tossed by the waves. Provided it kept me afloat for an instant, I did not care toward what dangerous reefs, toward what blacker depths it swept me on. I also cleaved to that absurd hope of one doomed to perish, which even on the slaughter stake, which even under the knife, still expects the impossible to happen: a sudden change, an earthly catastrophe which will deliver him from death. I permitted myself to be deluded by the pretty purring of Juliette’s words! A firm resolve to work heroically filled my spirit and threw me into raptures.﻿ ﻿… I had visions of multitudes of people bending breathlessly over my books, of theatres where grave and painted men were coming forward and uttering my name to the boundless enthusiasm of the audience. Overcome with fatigue, worn out with emotion, I fell asleep.

===Chinese===
她拭去我眼里的泪，接着说下去，嗓音一字比一字温柔：

“首先，咱们有四千法郎。……够过四个月呢。……这四个月里，你就干活儿。……倒要看看，四个月里你能不能写出一部好小说！……可是别哭了，你一哭，我就不告诉你一个大秘密了……一个天大的秘密。……你可知道你的小媳妇儿干了什么？她自己都万万没想到呢——知道吗？……咳，这三天，她天天上骑术学校，学骑马——等明年练出来了，弗朗科尼（Franconi）就会雇她了。……你可知道，时髦骑术学校里的女骑手挣多少。……一个月两千法郎、三千法郎！……这下你明白了吧，没什么好伤心的，我可怜的小男孩！”

一切胡话、一切痴想，到了我这里都成了情理。我死命抓住它们，像落水的水手死命抓住波涛里颠簸的不牢靠的残骸。只要能让我浮起一时，随它把我冲向什么险礁、冲向什么更黑的深渊，我都不管了。我也像那注定丧命的人一样，抱着荒唐的希望——哪怕上了屠宰桩，哪怕刀已加颈，还在盼那不可能的事发生：来个突变，来场人间的浩劫，把他从死亡里救出来。朱丽叶特那套咕噜咕噜、娓娓动听的话，我便甘心受它的哄骗！一股英雄般发奋工作的决心充满我的胸襟，令我狂喜不已。……我幻想着无数的人屏息俯首读我的书，幻想着剧场里一些神情庄重、涂着油彩的男人走到台前，念出我的名字，博得全场无边的狂热。我累乏已极，又被情感磨得筋疲力尽，便睡着了。

===Original===
We finished dinner. Juliette was even more affectionate than at the time when I came back. Nevertheless, I noticed a sort of uneasiness, a preoccupied air in her. She was sad and gay at one and the same time: What was going on behind this forehead over which clouds were passing? Did she decide to leave me, in spite of all her protestations, and did she want to make our separation easier by lavishing on me all the treasures of her caresses?

“How annoying, my dear!” she said, “I have to go out.”

“What do you mean, you have to go out? Now?”

“Why yes, just think of it. That poor Gabrielle is very ill. She is alone﻿—I have promised to come to see her! Oh! but I won’t stay very long.﻿ ﻿… About an hour.﻿ ﻿…”

Juliette spoke very naturally. But I don’t know why, it seemed to me that she was lying, that she was not going to Gabrielle at all. And suspicion, vague, terrifying suspicion pierced my heart. I said to her:

===Chinese===
晚饭吃完了。朱丽叶特比我回来那时候还要温存。然而我觉察到她有一丝不安，一副心事重重的样子。她同时又是伤心又是快活：这片飘着云翳的额头后面，究竟在转着什么念头？莫非她打定主意要离开我——尽管有那么些信誓旦旦——莫非她想把这些爱抚的宝藏统统倾倒给我，好叫我们的分离好受一些？

“真烦人，亲爱的！”她说，“我得出去一趟。”

“怎么，你得出去？这时候？”

“可不是嘛，你想想。可怜的加布里埃尔病得厉害。她孤零零一个人在家——我答应过去看她的！哦！我不会待很久。……大概一个钟头。……”

朱丽叶特说得非常自然。可是不知为什么，我总觉得她在撒谎，觉得她根本不是去加布里埃尔家。一种疑心，一种隐隐约约、骇人的疑心，刺进了我的心。我对她说：

===Original===
“Can’t you wait till tomorrow?”

“Oh, that’s impossible! Don’t you understand, I have promised.”

“Please, do me a favor! Go tomorrow.﻿ ﻿…”

“That’s impossible! Poor Gabrielle!”

“All right!﻿ ﻿… I’ll go with you.﻿ ﻿… I’ll wait for you at the door!﻿ ﻿…”

Cunningly I studied her.﻿ ﻿… Her face was motionless.﻿ ﻿… No, really her muscles did not betray the least surprise. She answered gently:

===Chinese===
“你不能等到明天吗？”

“哦，那怎么行！你难道不明白，我答应过人家了。”

“求求你，就算帮我个忙！明天再去吧。……”

“不行！可怜的加布里埃尔！”

“好吧！……我陪你去。……我在门口等你！……”

我狡黠地端详她。……她脸上纹丝不动。……不，说真的，她面部的肌肉没露出一丝诧异。她柔声答道：

===Original===
“There is no sense in that!﻿ ﻿… You are tired.﻿ ﻿… Go to bed!﻿ ﻿…”

And forthwith I saw the train of her gown stream behind the drawn door curtain like a snake.﻿ ﻿… Juliette is in her dressing room.﻿ ﻿… And with eyes fixed upon the tablecloth where the red reflection of a bottle of wine is flitting, I recall that recently some women came to this house, fleshly squint-eyed women, women who had the air of dogs scenting ordure.﻿ ﻿… I remember I had asked Juliette who those women were. One time Juliette answered: “That’s the corset maker.” Another time she said: “That’s the embroiderer.” And I believed her! One day I picked up on the carpet a visiting card which read.﻿ ﻿… Madame Rabineau, 114 Rue de Sèze. “Who was this Mme. Rabineau?” Juliette answered: “That’s nothing﻿ ﻿… give it here.﻿ ﻿…” And she tore the card up.﻿ ﻿… And fool that I was, I did not even go to the Rue de Sèze to find out!﻿ ﻿… I recall all that.﻿ ﻿… Ah! how could I ever fail to understand?﻿ ﻿… Why didn’t I seize them by the neck, these vile dealers in human flesh?﻿ ﻿…

And suddenly a great veil is lifted from my eyes, behind it I see Juliette with defiled body, exhausted and hideous, selling herself to human vultures!﻿ ﻿… Juliette is there, putting on her gloves, in front of me, in a dark dress with a thick veil which hides her features.﻿ ﻿… The shadow of her hand dances upon the tablecloth, lengthens out, grows broader, shrinks again, disappears and comes back again.﻿ ﻿… I shall always see this diabolic shadow, always!﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===
“那有什么意思！……你累了。……去睡吧！……”

随即我看见她裙裾一甩，像一条蛇，从拉起的门帘后掠了过去。……朱丽叶特进了梳妆室。……我两眼盯着桌布——一瓶红酒的红影正在布上晃动——想起近来这家里来过的一些女人：肉感的、斜眼的女人，一身的神气像嗅着秽物的狗。……我记得我问过朱丽叶特那些女人是谁。有一回朱丽叶特答：“是做胸衣的。”另一回她说：“是绣花的。”我竟信了！有一天，我在地毯上拾到一张名片，上面写着……拉比诺（Rabineau）太太，塞兹街（Rue de Sèze）114 号。“这位拉比诺太太是谁？”朱丽叶特答道：“没什么……给我吧。……”说着她把名片撕掉了。……我这个傻瓜，竟没到塞兹街去探个明白！……这些我都想起来了。……啊！我当时怎么会不明白呢？……这些卑鄙的人肉贩子，我怎么就没掐住她们的脖子？……

骤然间，一幅大幕从我眼前揭开了：幕后，我看见朱丽叶特身子已遭玷污，憔悴而丑陋，正把自己卖给那些人形秃鹫！……朱丽叶特就在那儿，就在我眼前，戴着手套，一身黑裙，罩着遮住面目的大面纱。……她手的影子在桌布上跳舞，拉长了，变宽了，又缩回去，消失了，又回来。……这道魔鬼的影子，我永远看得见，永远！……

===Original===
“Kiss me, dearie!”

“Don’t go out Juliette, don’t go out, I implore you!”

“Embrace me﻿ ﻿… closer﻿ ﻿… closer yet.﻿ ﻿…”

She is sad.﻿ ﻿… Through the thick veil I feel on my cheek the moisture of a tear.

“Why do you cry, Juliette.﻿ ﻿… Juliette, for pity’s sake, stay with me!”

“Embrace me.﻿ ﻿… I adore you, my Jean.﻿ ﻿… I adore you!﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
“亲亲我，心肝！”

“别出去，朱丽叶特，别出去，我求求你！”

“抱着我……贴近些……再贴近些。……”

她伤心。……隔着厚厚的面纱，我觉出脸颊上一滴泪的湿意。

“你哭什么呀，朱丽叶特。……朱丽叶特，发发慈悲，留下来陪我吧！”

“抱着我。……我爱你，我的让。……我爱你！……”

===Original===
She is gone.﻿ ﻿… Doors open, close again.﻿ ﻿… She is gone.﻿ ﻿… Outside I hear the noise of a rolling carriage. The noise grows fainter and fainter and dies out.﻿ ﻿… She is gone!﻿ ﻿…

And here I, too, am on the street.﻿ ﻿… A cab passes by﻿—114, Rue de Sèze!

My mind was made up quickly.﻿ ﻿… I figured that I would come there before she could.﻿ ﻿… She perfectly understood that I was not taken in by that story of Gabrielle’s illness.﻿ ﻿… My anxiety, my eagerness no doubt inspired her with the fear of being spied after, followed, and most likely she would not go to the place immediately. But why did just this abominable thought flash through my mind like lightning?﻿ ﻿… Why only this possibility and no other?﻿ ﻿… I still hope that my presentiments have deceived me, that Madame Rabineau “is nothing,” that Gabrielle is really sick!

Some kind of a small hotel hedged in between two tall buildings, a narrow door hollowed out in the wall at the end of three steps; a dark façade, whose closed windows let no light penetrate.﻿ ﻿… It’s here!﻿ ﻿… It is here she is going to come, where she already came perhaps!﻿ ﻿… Rage drives me toward this door.﻿ ﻿… I should like to set this house on fire; I should like to make all those detestable ladies hidden there shriek and writhe in agony, in some hellish blaze.﻿ ﻿… Presently a woman enters, singing and swaying her body, her hands in the pockets of her light jacket.﻿ ﻿… Why did not I spit in her face?﻿ ﻿… An old man has come out of his coupé. He passed close to me, snorting, panting, supported under his arm by his valet.﻿ ﻿… His trembling feet are unable to carry him, between his flabby, swollen eyelids there glimmers a light of beastly dissipation.﻿ ﻿… Why did I not slash the hideous face of this profligate old faun?﻿ ﻿… Perhaps he is waiting for Juliette!﻿ ﻿… The door of the Inferno opened before him﻿—and for an instant my eyes plunged into the pits of hell.﻿ ﻿… I thought I saw red flames, smoke, abominable embraces, the tumbling down of creatures horribly twisted together.﻿ ﻿… But no, it is only a gloomy deserted hallway, lit by the pale shine of a lamp; then at the end of it there is something black like a dark hole, where one feels impure things are stirring.﻿ ﻿… And carriages are stopping in front of the building, dumping out their haul of human dung into this sink of love.﻿ ﻿… A little girl barely ten years old follows me: “Nice violets! Nice violets!”﻿ ﻿… I give her a gold piece. “Go away from here, little one, go away!﻿ ﻿… Don’t stay here. They will get you!﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
她走了。……门开了，又关上。……她走了。……门外传来马车滚动的声响。那声响愈来愈弱，愈来愈弱，终于消逝了。……她走了！……

于是我也上了街。……一辆出租马车驶过——塞兹街 114 号！

主意拿定得很快。……我盘算着，要抢在她前头赶到那里。……她完全明白，加布里埃尔生病那套话骗不过我。……我的焦灼、我的急切，无疑叫她起了戒心，怕被人盯梢、跟随，多半不会径直就去那个地方。可是，为什么偏偏这个可憎的念头像闪电一样掠过我的脑海？……为什么只有这个可能，再没有别的？……我仍然希望是我的预感骗了我，希望拉比诺太太真的“没什么”，希望加布里埃尔真的病了！

一家小客栈，夹在两幢高楼中间；三级台阶尽头，墙上开出一扇窄门；黑沉沉的门面，关死的窗子透不出一丝光。……就是这里！……她要来的就是这里，也许早已经来过！……狂怒推着我向这扇门走去。……我恨不得一把火点着这房子；恨不得叫藏在里面的那些可憎的女人，在炼狱般的烈焰里哀号打滚。……一会儿，一个女人进去了，唱着歌，扭着身子，双手插在轻便短外套的口袋里。……我为什么不啐她一脸？……一个老头儿从轿式马车上下来，紧挨着我走过，哼哧哼哧喘着，由仆人架着胳膊。……他那双打颤的脚都快支不住身子了，松弛浮肿的眼皮中间，闪烁着一种兽性纵欲的光。……我为什么不一刀划破这荒淫老牧神的丑脸？……说不定他等的就是朱丽叶特！……地狱之门在他面前开了——刹那间，我的眼睛坠进了地狱的深渊。……我仿佛看见红色的火焰、浓烟、可憎的拥抱，还有绞缠成一团的活物纷纷跌倒。……可是不，那不过是一条阴暗冷清的过道，一盏灯照出惨白的光；过道尽头，一个黑洞似的东西，让人觉出有腌臜的东西在里面蠕动。……马车一辆辆在楼前停下，把满载的人粪，倒进这座爱情的阴沟里。……一个顶多十岁的小姑娘跟着我：“好看的紫罗兰！好看的紫罗兰！”……我给她一枚金币。“快离开这儿，小家伙，快走！……别待在这儿。她们会把你抓去的！……”

===Original===
My mind is overexerted. A thousand-toothed sorrow gnaws at my heart, a thousand claws sink into it, tears it to pieces in a frenzy of grief.﻿ ﻿… A desire to kill is kindled in me and makes my arms go through murderous motions.﻿ ﻿… Ah, to rush, whip in hand, into the midst of this lustful crowd and lash their bodies until ineffaceable marks are left on them, cause their warm blood to spurt, and scatter pieces of their living flesh all over the mirrors, carpets, beds!﻿ ﻿… And nail that Rabineau woman to the door of this house of ill-fame, like an owl on the doors of farm barns, nail her stripped, disemboweled, with her vitals out!﻿ ﻿… A hackney coach has stopped: a woman steps out. I recognize the hat, the veil, the dress.

“Juliette!”

On seeing me, she utters a cry.﻿ ﻿… But she regains her composure quickly.﻿ ﻿… Her eyes defy me.

===Chinese===
我的脑子用力过了度。一种千齿的悲愁啃噬着我的心，一千只利爪扎进去，在癫狂的哀痛里把它撕成碎片。……一股杀欲在我身上点着了，逼得我的双臂做出杀伐的动作。……啊，真想扬着鞭子，冲进这群淫欲的人群当中，抽打他们的身子，直到留下洗不掉的鞭痕，叫他们温热的血迸溅，把他们活生生的肉一块块甩到镜子上、地毯上、床铺上！……再把那个拉比诺女人钉在这座窑子的大门上，像钉在农家谷仓门上的猫头鹰，剥光了钉，开膛破肚地钉，五脏拉在外面！……一辆出租马车停了下来：一个女人跨下车来。我认出了那顶帽子、那幅面纱、那身衣裙。

“朱丽叶特！”

一看见我，她叫出声来。……但她很快恢复了镇定。……她的眼睛挑衅地瞪着我。

===Original===
“Leave me alone,” she cries out to me. “What are you doing here?﻿ ﻿… Leave me alone!”

I almost crush her wrists, and in a suffocating voice which rattles:

“Listen now.﻿ ﻿… If you make another step﻿ ﻿… if you say another word﻿ ﻿… I’ll knock you dead right here﻿—on this sidewalk, and tramp you to death under my feet.”

With a heavy blow I strike her in the face and with my nails I furiously claw her forehead and cheeks from which blood is gushing.

“Jean! Oh! Jean!﻿ ﻿… Have mercy, please!﻿ ﻿… Jean, mercy; Mercy!﻿ ﻿… Have pity on me!﻿ ﻿… You are killing me.﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
“放开我，”她冲我喊道，“你在这儿干什么？……放开我！”

我几乎捏碎她的手腕，用一种透不过气来、嘶哑作响的嗓音说：

“你听着。……你要再走一步……再说一个字……我就当场打死你——就在这条人行道上，把你踩死在我脚下。”

我一拳重重打在她脸上，又用指甲狂怒地抓她的额头和双颊，血从抓破的地方直涌出来。

“让！啊！让！……行行好，求你了！……让，饶了我吧，饶了我吧！……可怜可怜我！……你要打死我了。……”

===Original===
Rudely I drag her toward the carriage﻿ ﻿… and we get in.﻿ ﻿… Huddled up in two, she sits there right close to me, sobbing.﻿ ﻿… What am I going to do now?﻿ ﻿… I don’t know.﻿ ﻿… In truth I don’t know. I don’t ask myself any questions. I don’t think of anything.﻿ ﻿… It seems as though a mountain of stone has descended upon me.﻿ ﻿… I feel the heavy rocks on which my neck has crashed, against which my flesh has been bruised.﻿ ﻿… Why, with all the black despair in which I find myself, do these high walls rise up towards heaven? Why these dismal birds flying about in unexpected sunshine?﻿ ﻿… Why is this thing crouched down beside me crying?﻿ ﻿… Why?﻿ ﻿… I don’t know.﻿ ﻿…

I am going to kill her.﻿ ﻿… She is in her bedroom without lights, in bed.﻿ ﻿… I am in the dressing room pacing up and down﻿ ﻿… I am walking back and forth with constrained breath, my head on fire, with clinched fists eager to inflict punishment.﻿ ﻿… I am going to kill her!﻿ ﻿… From time to time I stop near the door and listen.﻿ ﻿… She is crying.﻿ ﻿… And in a minute I will enter.﻿ ﻿… I will enter and pull her off the bed, drag her by the hair, knock her senseless, break her neck against the marble edges of the fireplace.﻿ ﻿… I want the room to be red with her blood.﻿ ﻿… I want to see her body beaten into lumps of battered flesh which I shall throw out with the rest of the rubbish and which the garbageman will take away tomorrow.﻿ ﻿… Cry, cry!﻿ ﻿… In a minute you’ll howl, my dearest!﻿ ﻿… Haven’t I been stupid!﻿ ﻿… To think of everything but that!﻿ ﻿… To fear everything except that!﻿ ﻿… To say to myself: “she will leave me” and never, never: “she will deceive me.﻿ ﻿…” To have failed to divine the nature of this den, this old man, all this filth!﻿ ﻿… Really I had never thought of it before, blind fool that I was. She must have laughed when I implored her not to leave me!﻿ ﻿… To leave me.﻿ ﻿… Ah! yes, to leave me!﻿ ﻿… She did not want to, of course.﻿ ﻿… Now I understand it.﻿ ﻿… I inspired her neither with probity of heart nor with decency of conduct; I was to her just a label, a trademark﻿ ﻿… a mark of superior value!﻿ ﻿… Yes, when they saw her in my arms and therefore priced her more highly, she could sell herself for much more than she would have received if, like a nocturnal ghoul, she had roamed the sidewalks and haunted the obscene shadows of the streets.﻿ ﻿… She had swallowed my fortune in one gulp.﻿ ﻿… Her lips had rendered my mentality sterile at the first touch﻿—. Now she is gambling with my honor, that is consistent.﻿ ﻿… With my honor!﻿ ﻿… How could she know that I had none left?﻿ ﻿…

But am I really going to kill her?﻿ ﻿… When one is dead, everything is forgotten!﻿ ﻿… One bares one’s head before the coffin of a criminal, one bows in sadness before the dead body of a prostitute.﻿ ﻿… In the churches, believers kneel down and pray for those who have suffered, for those who have sinned.﻿ ﻿… At the cemeteries reverence watches over the graves and the cross protects them.﻿ ﻿… To die is to be forgiven!﻿ ﻿… Yes, death is beautiful, holy, noble!﻿ ﻿… Death is the beginning of the great eternal light.﻿ ﻿… Ah, to die!﻿ ﻿… to stretch oneself out on a mattress softer than the softest moss in birds’ nests.﻿ ﻿… To think no more.﻿ ﻿… To hear the noise of life no longer!﻿ ﻿… To feel the infinite sweetness of nothingness!﻿ ﻿… To be a soul!﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===
我粗暴地把她拽向马车……我们上了车。……两个人蜷作一团，她紧挨着我坐在那儿，抽抽噎噎。……现在我该怎么办？……我不知道。……真的，我不知道。我什么也不问自己，什么也不想。……仿佛一座石头山压到了我身上。……我觉出压断我脖颈的沉重的岩石，磨烂我皮肉的岩石。……为什么，在我沉入的这漆黑的绝望里，这些高墙偏偏朝天空耸起？为什么这些阴郁的鸟偏在想不到的阳光里盘旋？……为什么这个蜷在我身边的东西在哭？……为什么？……我不知道。……

我要杀了她。……她在卧房里，没点灯，躺在床上。……我在梳妆室里踱来踱去……我来回走动，屏着呼吸，脑袋烧得发烫，攥紧的拳头一心只想惩罚。……我要杀了她！……我不时在门边停下，听一听。……她在哭。……再过一分钟我就进去。……我要进去，把她从床上拖下来，拽着她的头发，打得她不省人事，把她的脖子往壁炉的大理石棱上磕断。……我要让这房间染红她的血。……我要看着她的身子被打成一堆稀烂的肉，同其余的垃圾一起扔出去，明天让收垃圾的拉走。……哭吧，哭吧！……再过一分钟你就要嚎了，我的心肝！……我多蠢啊！……什么都想到了，就是没想到这个！……什么都怕，就是不怕这个！……只对自己说：“她会离开我”，却从来不、从来不说：“她会欺骗我。……”竟没看透这个贼窝的底细，没看透这老头儿、这满眼的腌臜！……真的，我从前想都没想过，我真是个瞎眼的傻瓜。我哀求她别离开我的时候，她心里一定在笑！……离开我。……啊！是啊，离开我！……她当然不愿意离开我。……如今我懂了。……我既没让她心里生出廉耻，也没让她行止懂得体面；在她眼里，我不过是一张标签、一个牌号……一件抬高身价的标记！……是啊，人家见她偎在我怀里，于是给她定的价就更高；比起像个夜行的食尸鬼那样在人行道上转悠、出没于街头淫秽暗影里所能到手的，她能把自己卖出多得多的钱。……她一口吞掉了我的财产。……她的嘴唇头一贴上来，就把我的心智吮成了不毛之地——。如今她拿我的名誉做赌注，这倒顺理成章。……拿我的名誉！……她哪里知道，我早已一钱不剩？……

可我真的要杀她吗？……人一死，什么都烟消云散了！……罪人的灵柩前，人们脱帽致哀；娼妓的尸身前，人们黯然躬身。……教堂里，信徒们跪下来，为受苦的人祈祷，为犯罪的人祈祷。……墓园里，肃穆守望着坟茔，十字架护佑着它们。……死，就是得到宽恕！……是啊，死是美的、神圣的、高贵的！……死，是伟大永恒之光的发端。……啊，死去吧！……把自己安放在一张床垫上，比鸟巢里最柔软的苔藓还要柔软。……再也什么都不想。……再也听不见生命的嘈杂！……去感受虚无的无穷甘美！……化作一个灵魂！……

===Original===
I shall not kill her.﻿ ﻿… I shall not kill her because she has to suffer﻿ ﻿… terribly, always.﻿ ﻿… Let her suffer in all her beauty, in all her pride, in her exposed carnality of a prostitute!﻿ ﻿… I shall not kill her, but I shall disfigure her to such an extent, I shall make her look so repulsive that people, frightened, will flee at the sight of her.﻿ ﻿… And every evening I shall compel her to appear on the streets, at the theatre, everywhere with her nose crushed, her eyes bulging out from under eyelids fringed with black rings, without a veil!﻿ ﻿…

Suddenly sobs from my throat.﻿ ﻿… I fling myself on the couch, biting the cushion, and cry and cry!﻿ ﻿… Minutes, hours pass and I am still crying!﻿ ﻿… Ah! Juliette, vile Juliette!﻿ ﻿… Why did you do that?﻿ ﻿… Why?﻿ ﻿… Could you not say to me: “Here now, you are not rich anymore and all I want of you is money.﻿ ﻿… Leave me!” That would have been cruel, it might have meant my death.﻿ ﻿… But what of it?﻿ ﻿… It would have been better.﻿ ﻿… How can I look into your face now?﻿ ﻿… How can our mouths ever touch each other?﻿ ﻿… There is now between us the thick wall of that wicked place!﻿ ﻿… Ah! Juliette!﻿ ﻿… Wretched Juliette!﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===
我不杀她。……我不杀她，因为她得受罪……受可怕的罪，受一辈子。……让她带着她全部的美、全部的傲，带着她那卖笑女人袒露的肉欲去受罪吧！……我不杀她，但我要把她毁容，毁得那样厉害，把她弄得那样丑恶，叫人们一见她就惊骇奔逃。……每天晚上，我要逼她抛头露面，上街、上剧院、去哪儿都行，塌着鼻子，眼睛从镶着黑圈的眼皮下凸出来，不许罩面纱！……

忽然，我喉咙里涌上一阵呜咽。……我扑到长榻上，咬着坐垫，哭啊，哭啊！……一分钟、一个钟头过去，我还在哭！……啊！朱丽叶特，卑贱的朱丽叶特！……你为什么要那样做？……为什么？……你难道不能对我说：“你听着，你如今不再有钱了，我要你的只是钱。……放开我吧！”那样固然残忍，也许就等于要我的命。……可那又怎样？……那样倒好些。……如今我还能看你的脸吗？……我们的嘴还能相触吗？……如今横在我们中间的，是那座下贱地方的一堵厚墙！……啊！朱丽叶特！……苦命的朱丽叶特！……

===Original===
I remember her going out.﻿ ﻿… I recollect everything!﻿ ﻿… I recall how she was dressed in her gray dress, the shadow of her hand dancing strangely on the back of her neck.﻿ ﻿… I see her as clearly as if she were before me now, and even more so.﻿ ﻿… She was sad, she was crying.﻿ ﻿… I am sure it was not mere imagination on my part﻿ ﻿… she was actually crying, for my cheek was wet with her tears! Whom was she crying over, me or herself? Ah!﻿ ﻿… who knows?﻿ ﻿… I remember.﻿ ﻿… I said to her: “Don’t go out, my Juliette!﻿ ﻿…” She replied: “Embrace me closely, very closely, more closely yet!﻿ ﻿…” And her caresses had the passion of despair in them, a kind of shrivelling grip, a sort of fear as if she had wanted to cling to me, to seek tremblingly protection in my arms.﻿ ﻿… I can see her eyes, her beseeching look.﻿ ﻿… They seemed to implore me: “Something abominable is drawing me on.﻿ ﻿… Hold me back!﻿ ﻿… I am close to your heart﻿ ﻿… do not let me go!﻿ ﻿…” And instead of taking her in my arms, carrying her away, hiding her and loving her so as to make her giddy with happiness, I opened up my arms and let her go!﻿ ﻿… She sought refuge in my love, and I denied it to her.﻿ ﻿… She cried to me: “I adore you, I adore you!﻿ ﻿…” And I stood there like a fool, amazed as is a child at the unexpected flapping of the wings of a captive bird that has just escaped.﻿ ﻿… I did not understand that sadness, those tears, those caresses, those words more tender than usual, that trembling.﻿ ﻿… It is only now that I hear those silent, melancholy words: “My dear Jean, I am a poor little woman, a little foolish and so weak!﻿ ﻿… I had no idea of anything big or worth while.﻿ ﻿… Who was there to teach me what chastity, duty, virtue meant!﻿ ﻿… When I was a child yet, evil surroundings contaminated me, and vice was taught to me by the very people who were supposed to be my guardians.﻿ ﻿… Still I am not wicked and I love you.﻿ ﻿… I love you more than I ever loved you!﻿ ﻿… My beloved Jean, you are strong, you know many beautiful things which I don’t.﻿ ﻿… Well, protect me!﻿ ﻿… An overpowering desire draws me there.﻿ ﻿… The trouble is I have seen too much jewelry, too many gowns and other exquisite and expensive trifles which you can’t buy me any longer but which others have promised to get me!﻿ ﻿… I have an instinctive feeling that it’s wrong and that it will cause you suffering.﻿ ﻿… Well, subdue me!﻿ ﻿… I ask for no other chance than to be good and virtuous.﻿ ﻿… Teach me how!﻿ ﻿… Beat me﻿ ﻿… if I resist!﻿ ﻿…”

Poor Juliette!﻿ ﻿… It seems to me that she is down on her knees before me, with clasped hands.﻿ ﻿… Tears are rolling from her eyes, from her big eyes downcast and sweet.﻿ ﻿… Tears are streaming from her eyes endlessly as they used to stream from the eyes of my mother in the past.﻿ ﻿… And at the thought that I wanted to kill her, that I wanted to disfigure her delightful and sorrowful face through horrible mutilation, I am seized with remorse and my wrath gives way to pity.﻿ ﻿… She goes on.﻿ ﻿… “Forgive me!﻿ ﻿… Oh! my Jean you must forgive me.﻿ ﻿… It is not my fault, I assure you.﻿ ﻿… Try to recall.﻿ ﻿… Did you ever warn me, even once?﻿ ﻿… Did you ever show me even once the way which I should follow? Through weakness, through fear of losing me, through excessive and criminal kindness, you have yielded to all my whims, even the most wicked ones.﻿ ﻿… How could I know that it was wrong, when you have never told me anything?﻿ ﻿… Instead of stopping me on the brink of the precipice where I was headed, you yourself have pushed me into it.﻿ ﻿… What example have you placed before my eyes?﻿ ﻿… Whither have you led me?﻿ ﻿… Have you ever tried to take me out of this alarming atmosphere of debauchery?﻿ ﻿… Why didn’t you chase Jesselin or Gabrielle out of our house, all those degenerates whose very presence only helped to increase my wickedness?﻿ ﻿… To breathe into me a particle of your own soul, to send a ray of light into the darkness of my brains﻿—that is what you should have done!﻿ ﻿… Yes, you should have given me another life, you should have made me over again!﻿ ﻿… I am guilty, my Jean!﻿ ﻿… And I am so ashamed of myself that I can never hope to be able to atone for the infamy of this evil hour even with a whole life of sacrifice and repentance.﻿ ﻿… But you!﻿ ﻿… Is your conscience satisfied that you have done your duty?﻿ ﻿… I dread not the expiation of my sins.﻿ ﻿… On the contrary I welcome it, I want it.﻿ ﻿… But you?﻿ ﻿… Can you sit in judgment over a crime which I admit I have committed, but in which you, too, have had a part since you have not done anything to prevent it!﻿ ﻿… My dear beloved, listen to me.﻿ ﻿… This body which I have attempted to defile horrifies you; hereafter you will not be able to look at it without rage and anguish.﻿ ﻿… All right then, let it perish!﻿ ﻿… Let it rot in the oblivion of a graveyard!﻿ ﻿… There shall be left to you my soul, it belongs to you, for it has never forsaken you, for it loves you.﻿ ﻿… See how white and pure it is.﻿ ﻿…”

A knife glistens in Juliette’s hands.﻿ ﻿… She is going to kill herself with it.﻿ ﻿… I grasp her arms, I shout: “No, no, Juliette, no, I don’t want you to!﻿ ﻿… I love you!﻿ ﻿… No, no.﻿ ﻿… I don’t want you to!”

===Chinese===
