我记得她出门的情形。……我什么都记得！……我记得她穿着那身灰裙，她的手的影子在她颈后古怪地跳舞。……我看见她，清清楚楚，仿佛她此刻就在我眼前，甚至比眼前更真切。……她伤心，她哭着。……我敢肯定那不是我的凭空想象……她确实哭了，因为我的脸颊还沾着她的泪，湿着呢！她哭的是谁？是我，还是她自己？啊！……谁知道呢？……我记得。……我对她说：“别出去，我的朱丽叶特！……”她答道：“抱紧我，紧紧的，再紧一些！……”她的爱抚里带着绝望的激情，一种攥得人发皱的紧搂，一种恐惧，仿佛她想攀住我，想颤颤地在我怀里寻求庇护。……我看得见她的眼睛，她那哀恳的目光。……那目光仿佛在恳求我：“有种可憎的东西在拉我往前去。……拦住我！……我贴着你的心……别放我走！……”而我非但没有把她搂进怀里，把她带走，把她藏起来，用爱把她爱得幸福得发晕，反倒张开两臂，放她走了！……她到我的爱情里来避难，我却把它拒之门外。……她向我喊：“我爱你，我爱你！……”而我呆站在那里，像个孩子，眼见笼中鸟冷不防扑棱着翅膀逃走了，只顾发怔。……我没能懂得那份悲伤、那些眼泪、那些爱抚、那些比平时更温存的话、那阵战栗。……直到如今，我才听见那些无声的、忧郁的话：“亲爱的让，我是个可怜的小女人，有点儿傻，又这样软弱！……我从来不懂什么大事、什么正经事。……又有谁教过我，贞洁是什么、本分是什么、美德是什么！……我还是个孩子的时候，坏环境就把我污染了，教我学坏的，恰恰是那些本该做我监护人的人。……可我并不坏，我爱你。……我比从前任何时候都爱你！……我亲爱的让，你强壮，你懂得许多我不懂的美好的东西。……那么，保护我吧！……一股压不住的欲望把我往那儿拽。……难就难在，我见过的珠宝太多、裙子太多，还有那么多精美昂贵的小玩意儿——你再也买不起给我了，别人却答应给我！……我凭本能觉出这是错的，觉出这会让你受苦。……那么，制住我吧！……我只求做个又好又贞洁的人，别的什么都不要。……教教我该怎么做！……我要是不肯听……就打我！……”

可怜的朱丽叶特！……我仿佛看见她跪在我面前，双手合十。……泪水从她眼睛里滚下来，从她那双低垂着的大眼睛里滚下来，那双眼睛多么温柔。……泪水从她眼里流个不住，就像从前从我母亲眼里流个不住一样。……一想到我竟想杀她，竟想用可怕的残毁糟蹋她那张又可爱又悲愁的脸，悔恨便攫住了我，我的忿怒化作了怜悯。……她接着说下去。……

“饶恕我！……啊！我的让，你务必饶恕我。……这不是我的错，我向你保证。……你好好回想回想。……你可曾劝诫过我，哪怕一次？……你可曾哪怕一次指给我该走的路？因为软弱，因为怕失去我，因为那过分的、有罪的仁慈，你依了我所有的任性，连最坏的任性也依了。……你从来没对我说过什么，我又怎么会知道那是错呢？……你非但没有在我正走向的悬崖边上拦住我，反倒亲手把我推了进去。……你在我眼前立下的是什么榜样？……你把我领到了什么田地？……你可曾设法把我从这种骇人的放荡空气里拉出去过？……你为什么不把热瑟兰、把加布里埃尔赶出我们家门，赶走那些堕落的家伙？他们一来，只会添长我的恶。……把你灵魂的一星半点吹进我胸中，往我脑子的黑暗里送进一线光——那才是你该做的！……是啊，你本该给我另一种生活，本该把我重新造过！……我有罪，我的让！……我羞惭到这地步：就算拿整整一生的牺牲和忏悔，我也不敢指望赎净这恶时辰的耻辱。……可是你呢！……你的良心可安宁，说你尽了本分？……我不怕为我的罪受罚。……恰恰相反，我欢迎它，我要它。……可是你呢？……这桩罪，我承认我犯了；可你也有份，因为你什么都没有做去阻止它——这样的罪，你还能坐在审判席上审我吗！……亲爱的，你听我说。……这具我糟蹋了的身子叫你憎厌；往后你再看它，只能又恨又痛。……那好，就让它毁灭吧！……让它在墓园的湮没无闻中腐烂吧！……留给你的还有我的灵魂，它是你的，因为它从不曾背弃你，因为它爱你。……你瞧，它多么洁白，多么纯净。……”

===Original===
My arms are brought together in an embrace, but I enclose nothing but space.﻿ ﻿… I look around me, frightened, the place is empty!﻿ ﻿… I look again.﻿ ﻿… The gas is burning with a yellow flame over the dressing table﻿ ﻿… rumpled skirts are strewn all over the carpet﻿ ﻿… shoes lie scattered about.﻿ ﻿… And pale daylight is stealing into the room through the open spaces in the shutters.﻿ ﻿… I begin to fear in earnest that Juliette may kill herself, for otherwise why should this vision arise before me?﻿ ﻿… On tiptoe, quietly I walk toward the door and listen.﻿ ﻿… A feeble sigh reaches my ear, then a wailing, then a sob.﻿ ﻿… And like a fool I rush into the room.﻿ ﻿… A voice speaks to me in the darkness, the voice of Juliette:

“Ah! my Jean! My dear little Jean!”

And chastely, as Christ kissed Magdalene, I kissed her on the forehead.

===Chinese===
朱丽叶特手里亮起一把刀。……她要用它自杀。……我抓住她的双臂，喊道：“不，不，朱丽叶特，不，我不要你这样！……我爱你！……不，不。……我不要你这样！”

“啊！我的让！我亲爱的小让！”

于是我像基督亲吻抹大拉的马利亚那样，贞洁地吻了她的额头。

## VIII / 八

===Original===
“Lirat! Ah, at last it is you! For a week I have been looking for you, have been writing to you, have been calling you, have been waiting for you.﻿ ﻿… Lirat, my dear Lirat, save me!”

“What? My God! What’s wrong?”

“I want to kill myself.”

“Kill yourself! Well, that’s an old story. Come, there is no danger.”

“I want to kill myself! I want to kill myself!﻿ ﻿…”

Lirat looked at me, blinked his eyes and paced up and down the study with long strides.
===Chinese===
“利拉！啊，总算找到你了！整整一个星期，我到处找你，给你写信，一遍遍喊你，等你。……利拉，我亲爱的利拉，救救我吧！”

“怎么？我的天！出什么事了？”

“我想自杀。”

“自杀！嗨，这是老掉牙的故事了。来吧，没什么大不了的。”

“我想自杀！我想自杀！……”

利拉望着我，眨了眨眼睛，迈开大步在画室里走来走去。

===Original===
“My poor Mintié!” he said, “if you were a statesman, a stockbroker or.﻿ ﻿… Well, I don’t know﻿ ﻿… say a grocer, an art critic, or a journalist, I would say to you: ‘You are unhappy and you have had enough of life, my boy! Go ahead, kill yourself!’ And with these words I would leave you. But here you have that rare opportunity of being an artist, you possess that divine gift of seeing, understanding, feeling things which others can’t see, can’t understand and can’t feel! There are harmonies in nature which exist only for you and which others will never hear﻿ ﻿… you have all the real joys of life, the only joys, the noble, grand and pure ones, the joys which make you forget men and which render you almost Godlike. And because some woman has deceived you, you want to renounce all that? She has deceived you; it is evident that she has deceived you.﻿ ﻿… Well, what else did you expect her to do? And what concern is it of yours, even if she has?﻿ ﻿…”
===Chinese===
“我可怜的曼蒂埃！”他说，“倘若你是个政客、个证券经纪人，或者……唉，我也说不上……就算是个食品杂货商、个美术评论家，或者个新闻记者吧，我就会对你说：‘你活得不快活，也活够了，小伙子！那好，去自杀吧！’说完这几句，我掉头就走。可你呢，你偏偏有那份难得的造化，是个艺术家；你有那分神赐的天赋，能看见、能领会、能感受那些旁人看不见、领会不了、也感受不到的东西！自然界里有一些和声，单单为你存在，旁人永远听不见……人世间真正的好处你都有了——那唯一的好处，高贵、宏大而纯粹的好处，那叫你忘掉众人、叫你几乎同神明一样的欢喜。如今就因为一个女人欺骗了你，你就要把这一切统统舍弃么？她欺骗了你；明摆着，她是欺骗了你。……那又怎么样呢，你还能指望她做出别的样子来？就算她骗了你，又与你什么相干？……”

===Original===
“Please don’t jeer at me. You don’t know anything, Lirat. You don’t suspect anything. I am lost, dishonored!”

“Dishonored, my friend? Are you sure of it? Do you have unclean debts? You’ll pay them off!”

“It is not a question of that! I am dishonored! dishonored, do you understand? It has been four months since I have given Juliette any money﻿ ﻿… four months! And here I live, I eat, have my amusements. Every evening﻿ ﻿… before dinner﻿ ﻿… late at night.﻿ ﻿… Juliette reenters the house. She is worn-out, pale, her hair disheveled. From what dens, what alcoves, what arms is she returning? Upon what pillows has her head reclined! Sometimes I see pieces of bedclothes insolently hanging on the top of her hair.﻿ ﻿… She no longer feels ashamed of it, she does not even take the trouble to lie about it﻿ ﻿… one might think it had been arranged between us. She undresses, and I believe she takes a perverse delight in showing me her ill-fastened skirts, her unlaced corset, all the disorder of her rumpled clothes, of her loosened garments which come off, falling to the ground about her, and lie conspicuously on the floor, filling the bedroom with the breath of other people!
===Chinese===
“请你别讥笑我。你什么都不知道，利拉。你什么都没想到。我完了，我丢了脸！”

“丢了脸，我的朋友？你拿得准么？你欠了什么不干净的债么？还上就是了！”

“不是那么回事！我是丢了脸！丢了脸，你懂不懂？我已经四个月没有给朱丽叶特钱了……四个月！可我还在这儿活着，吃着，玩乐着。每天晚上……晚饭前……深夜里。……朱丽叶特回到家里。她筋疲力尽，脸色苍白，头发散乱。她是从什么黑窝、什么床帐、什么怀抱里回来的？她的头枕过什么样的枕头！有时我看见被褥的碎屑大模大样地挂在她发顶上。……她连羞耻也不觉得了，甚至懒得为这事撒个谎……人家简直要以为，这是我们俩商量好了的。她宽衣解带，我相信她还怀着一种变态的快意，故意让我看她那没有系牢的裙子、解了带子的胸衣，看她那皱巴巴一身衣衫的狼藉、那一件件松脱的衣裳，褪下来，散落在她脚边，赫然摊在地板上，满屋子充溢着旁人的气息！

===Original===
“I tremble with rage and want to sink my teeth into her body; my wrath is kindled to a frenzy and boils within me﻿—I feel like killing her. And I say nothing! Often I even come up to her to embrace her﻿ ﻿… but she pushes me away: ‘No, leave me alone, I am tired!’ At first, when this abominable life started, I used to beat her﻿ ﻿… for you must know, Lirat, there isn’t a disgraceful act that I have not committed. I have exhausted every form of indecency﻿—yes, I beat her! She bent her back﻿ ﻿… and hardly uttered a complaint. One evening I seized her by the throat, I threw her to the ground. Oh! I had quite made up my mind to finish her. While I was strangling her, I turned my head away for fear that I might be moved to pity, fixed my gaze upon a flower design on the carpet, and in order to hear nothing, neither her wailing nor rattling, I shrieked out inarticulate words, like a possessed one. How long did it last? Soon she ceased struggling﻿ ﻿… her muscles relaxed.﻿ ﻿… I felt her vitality giving out under my fingers﻿ ﻿… a few more convulsions﻿ ﻿… and that was the end.﻿ ﻿… She did not stir anymore. And suddenly I saw her black-blue face, her contracted eyes, her mouth, large and wide open, her rigid body, her motionless arms. And like a madman I rushed into every room of the apartment, calling the servants: ‘Help, help, I have killed Madame! I have killed Madame!’
===Chinese===
“我气得发抖，真想一口咬进她的肉里；我的怒火烧到狂乱的地步，在胸中翻腾——我觉得自己真会杀了她。可我一个字也不说！常常我甚至还走上前去搂她……她却把我推开：‘不，别缠我，我累了！’起初，这种可憎的日子刚开始的时候，我还打过她……因为你该如何道，利拉，天下没有一件下作的勾当是我没有干过的。种种无耻的样式我都使尽了——是的，我打过她！她弯下背去……几乎不哼一声。有一天晚上，我掐住她的喉咙，把她摔在地上。啊！我是打定了主意要结果她的。我扼着她的时候，把头扭向一边，生怕自己动了怜悯；眼睛盯住地毯上的一处花纹，为了什么都听不见——她的哀号也好，喉咙里的格格声也好——我像个着了魔的人，扯着嗓子喊出不成语句的喊叫。那样持续了多久？不久她就不挣扎了……肌肉松了下来。……我觉出她的生机在我指缝间消逝……又抽搐了几下……就完了。……她一动也不动了。冷不防我看见她那张青黑的脸，抽紧的眼睛，那张大张着的嘴，僵直的身体，不动的双臂。我像个疯子，冲进公寓的每一个房间，喊仆人们：‘救命，救命，我把太太杀死了！我把太太杀死了！’

===Original===
“I fled, tumbling down the stairway, without a hat, dashed into the caretakers: ‘Go upstairs quickly, I have killed Madame!’ Then I darted out on the street, in a frenzy. The whole night I was running without knowing whither, rushing along the boulevards, crossing bridges, dashing against benches in the parks and mechanically turning back toward the house. It seemed to me that through its closed shutters there penetrated the light of wax tapers; priests’ vestments, surplices, eucharists passed before me in confusion; it seemed to me that I could hear funeral chants, the rumble of organs, the noise of ropes rubbing against the wood of the coffin. I pictured Juliette stretched out on the bed, dressed in a white robe, her hands clasped, a crucifix on her breast and flowers about her. And I was surprised not to see black draperies on the door, or a hearse with flowers and wreaths at the entrance outside, or people in mourning fighting for a chance to be sprinkled with holy water.

“Oh, Lirat, what a night that was! How did I ever manage not to throw myself under the wheels of the carriages, crash my head against the housewall, or plunge into the Seine. I don’t know!﻿ ﻿… Day came.﻿ ﻿… I had a notion to surrender to the police. I wanted to go up to a policeman on the street and say to him: ‘I have killed Juliette.﻿ ﻿… Arrest me!’ But thoughts, each wilder than the other, came to my mind, clashed and yielded to others. And I ran and ran as if pursued by a pack of barking hounds.﻿ ﻿… It was Sunday, I remember. There were many people on the streets flooded with sunshine. I was sure that all looked at me, that these people, seeing me run, cried out in horror: ‘Here is Juliette’s murderer!’
===Chinese===
“我逃了出来，跌跌撞撞滚下楼梯，帽子也没戴，一头闯进门房：‘快上楼，我把太太杀死了！’随后我蹿到街上，没头没脑地狂奔。一整夜我都在跑，不知跑向哪里，冲过一条条林荫大道，跨过一座座桥，撞上公园里的长椅，又身不由己地折回那座房子。我仿佛看见，闭着的百叶窗缝里透出细蜡烛的光；神父的祭袍、白色圣衣、圣体在我眼前纷乱地晃过；我仿佛听得见安魂的经歌、管风琴的轰鸣、绳索在棺木上摩擦的声响。我想象朱丽叶特直挺挺躺在灵床上，穿着白袍，双手交叠，胸前放着一个十字架，四周缀满了花。而叫我诧异的是，门上看不见黑纱，门外入口处看不见一辆缀着鲜花和花圈的灵车，看不见争着抢着要沾一掬圣水的送丧的人们。

“啊，利拉，那是怎样的一夜啊！我居然没有扑到马车的轮子底下，没有拿脑袋去撞房屋的墙，没有一头栽进塞纳河（Seine）——这是怎么熬过来的，我自己也不知道！……天亮了。……我起意去投案自首。我想走到街上的一个警察跟前，对他说：‘我杀死了朱丽叶特。……逮捕我吧！’可是一个比一个更疯狂的念头涌上心头，彼此冲撞，又被后来的压倒。我就这么跑啊跑啊，仿佛身后跟着一群狂吠的猎狗。……那天是星期天，我记得。满街的人，街道浸在阳光里。我断定人人都在看我，这些人见我奔跑，都吓得喊出声来：‘朱丽叶特的凶手在这儿！’

===Original===
“Toward evening, worn out, on the verge of collapsing on the sidewalk, I met Jesselin! ‘I say,’ he exclaimed, ‘you have done a nice thing, you have!’ ‘Do you already know it?’ ‘Why, all Paris knows it, dear friend. A little while ago, at the races, Juliette showed us her neck and the marks which your fingers had left on it. She said: “Jean did this to me.” Why, man you are getting on fine!’ And while parting, he added: ‘For the rest, she has never been more beautiful. And such a success!’ And so you see that while I believed her to be dead, she was promenading at the racetrack. I had left the house and she could have thought that I would never come back again, and yet she went to the races﻿ ﻿… prettier than ever!”
===Chinese===
“傍晚时分，我筋疲力尽，眼看就要瘫倒在人行道上，偏偏遇见了热瑟兰！‘喂，’他嚷道，‘你可干了件漂亮事，真漂亮！’‘你已经知道了？’‘咳，全巴黎都知道了，亲爱的朋友。方才在赛马场上，朱丽叶特给我们看她的脖子，看你手指掐出的印子。她说：“这是让干的好事。”哎呀，老兄，你可真出息！’临分手时，他又添了一句：‘说真的，她从来没这么漂亮过。而且大出风头！’你瞧，正当我以为她死了的时候，她却在赛马场上兜风。我离了家，她尽可以想我再也不回去了，可她照旧去赛马……比先前任何时候都俏丽！”

===Original===
Lirat gravely listened to me. He was not pacing about anymore; he seated himself and shook his head.

“What do you want me to tell you? You must go away.”

“Go away?” I rejoined. “I should go away? But I don’t want to! An adhesive force like glue which is getting thicker every day holds me fast to her carpets, a chain growing heavier every day holds me riveted to her walls. I can’t leave her! Look, at this very moment I am dreaming of committing all sorts of mad, heroic acts. To cleanse myself of all this baseness, I am ready to throw myself in front of the fire-spitting muzzles of a hundred cannons. I feel myself strong enough to crush whole formidable armies single-handed. When I walk on the street I look for runaway horses, fires or any other dangerous adventure where I can sacrifice my life. There is not a perilous or superhuman deed that I have not the courage to perform. But, that! I can not do!
===Chinese===
利拉神情凝重地听我说。他不再踱步了；他坐下来，摇着头。

“你要我对你说什么好呢？你必须走。”

“走？”我应道。“我该走？可我不想走！有一股胶水似的黏力，一天稠似一天，把我死死黏在她的地毯上；有一条锁链，一天重似一天，把我牢牢铆在她的墙上。我离不开她！你瞧，就在这会儿，我还梦想着去干种种疯狂的、英雄的壮举。为了洗净这一身的卑污，我可以纵身扑到一百门大炮喷吐火光的炮口前面。我自觉有力气单枪匹马踏平整支整支可怕的大军。我在街上走的时候，专找脱缰的马、失火的房子，找一切能拿性命去拼的危险勾当。天下没有一桩凶险的、超人的事，是我没有胆量去干的。可是那一件事！我做不到！

===Original===
“At first I offered myself the most ridiculous excuses, I gave myself the most illogical reasons for not leaving her. I said to myself that if I left her, Juliette would sink to even lower depths; that my love for her had in some manner been her last vestige of decency which I should finally succeed in restoring by saving her from the mire in which she wallowed. Truly I had been repaid by the luxury of pity and self-sacrifice. But I was lying! I simply can’t leave her! I can’t because I love her, because the more depraved she is the more I love her. Because I want her, do you hear, Lirat? And if you only knew what it means to me, this love, what frenzies, what shame, what tortures? If you only knew to what depths of Hell passion can sink, you would be horrified! At night when she is asleep, I prowl about in her dressing room, opening drawers, digging among the cinders of the fireplace, putting together pieces of torn letters, smelling the linens which she has just removed, devoting myself to the vilest spying, to the most shameful searching! It was not enough for me to know; I had to see as well! I have no longer a mind, a heart, or anything. I am just the embodiment of disordered, raving, famished sex, which demands its share of living flesh, like the fallow deer that howl in their frenzy on rutting nights.”
===Chinese===
“起初我给自己编造最可笑的借口，给自己找最不合逻辑的理由，赖着不肯离开她。我对自己说，我若走了，朱丽叶特会陷得更深；我对她的爱，多少还算她身上最后一星体面的残迹，把她从她打滚的泥坑里搭救出来，我终归还能把那点体面恢复。说真的，怜悯与自我牺牲这份奢华，我也总算领受过它的报偿。可我是在撒谎！我就是离不开她！离不开，因为我爱她，因为她越堕落我越爱她。因为我渴望她，你听见没有，利拉？这爱情对我是怎么回事——怎样的癫狂、怎样的耻辱、怎样的煎熬，你若知道就好了！情欲能一直沉到地狱的哪一层，你若知道，你准会吓坏！夜里她睡熟了，我在她的梳妆室里潜行，拉开一个个抽屉，在炉膛的灰烬里扒寻，把撕碎的信一片片拼起来，嗅她刚刚换下的贴身衣衫，沉溺于最卑污的窥探、最见不得人的搜检！光是知道还不够；我还要亲眼看见！我再也没有头脑，没有心，什么都没有了。我只剩下一副错乱、癫狂、饥渴的性欲的化身，讨要它那一份活生生的血肉，像发情之夜在狂乱中嘶吼的黇鹿。”

===Original===
I was exhausted﻿ ﻿… the words came out of my throat with a hissing sound﻿ ﻿… still I continued.

“Ah! It is beyond all comprehension! Sometimes it happens that Juliette is sick. Her members, overstrained by pleasure, refuse to obey her; her constitution, worn out by nervous shocks, revolts. She takes to her bed. If you could only see her then? A child, Lirat, a sweet and touching child! She dreams only of the country, little brooks, green prairies, simple joys: ‘Oh, my dear,’ she exclaims, ‘with ten thousand francs of income, how happy we should be!’ She makes all sorts of Virgilian and charming plans. ‘We ought to go far, far away, to live in a house surrounded by tall trees. She will raise chickens which will lay eggs she herself will take out of the hatching place every morning; she will make cream, cheese; and she will wear aprons like this and straw hats like that, jogging along pathways astride a donkey that she will call Joseph. Geeho! Joseph, Geeho! Ah, how nice it will be!’

“When I hear her say that, I feel hope returning and I let myself be taken in by that impossible dream of a rustic life with Juliette disguised as a shepherdess. Quiet landscapes like places of refuge, enchanting like a paradise, unroll before us﻿ ﻿… and we grow exalted and enthusiastic. Juliette cries: ‘My poor little thing, I have caused you suffering, but now it’s all over. I promise you. And then I am going to have a trained ram, am I not? A beautiful ram, very big, all white, around whom I shall tie a bow of red ribbon, and who will follow me everywhere together with Spy, not so, dear?’ She insists that I have my dinner in front of her bed, on a little table, and she coddles me like a nurse and caresses me like a mother; she makes me eat as one does a child, repeating without end and with agitation in her voice: ‘My poor little thing!’
===Chinese===
我已经精疲力竭……话从喉咙里挤出来，带着嘶嘶的声响……我仍旧往下说。

“啊！这真是没法理解！有时朱丽叶特会病倒。她那被欢愉过度劳损的肢体，不肯听她使唤了；她那被神经的震荡磨坏了的体质，起来造反。她卧床不起。那时候你要是能看看她才好呢！一个小孩子，利拉，一个又甜又惹人疼的小孩子！她一心只梦想乡下，梦想小溪、绿草地、简简单单的快乐：‘啊，亲爱的，’她嚷道，‘有一万法郎的年金，我们会多幸福啊！’她盘算出种种维吉尔式（Virgilian）的、迷人的打算。‘我们该走远些，远远的，住到一座四面围着大树的房子里去。她要养母鸡，母鸡下蛋，每天早晨由她亲手从鸡窝里掏出来；她要做奶油，做奶酪；她要围这样的围裙，戴那样的草帽，骑一头驴在小路上一路颠儿颠儿，管它叫约瑟夫（Joseph）。驾！约瑟夫，驾！啊，那该多好！’

“听她这样说，我觉得希望回来了，任凭自己沉进那个不可能实现的梦里：朱丽叶特扮成牧羊女，同我一起过田园日子。宁静的风景像避难的处所，像乐园一样迷人，在我们面前铺展开来……我们变得意气昂扬、兴高采烈。朱丽叶特喊道：‘我可怜的小宝贝，我让你受了苦，可现在全过去了。我答应你。对了，我还要养一只调教好的公羊，是不是？一只漂亮的公羊，个儿很大，浑身雪白，我在它脖子上系一个红缎带的蝴蝶结，让它跟着斯派陪我到处走，好不好，亲爱的？’她定要我就在她床前一张小桌上吃晚饭，她像看护一样宠我，像母亲一样抚我；她像喂孩子似的喂我吃，一遍又一遍、声音里带着激动地重复：‘我可怜的小宝贝！’

===Original===
“At other moments she becomes thoughtful and grave: ‘My dear, I would like to ask you something that has been worrying me for a long time; promise that you’ll tell me.’ I promise. ‘Well, when one is dead, in the coffin, is it true that one’s feet rest against the board?’ ‘What an idea! Why do you speak of it?’ ‘Tell me, please tell me!’ ‘But I don’t know, my dear Juliette!’ ‘Don’t you know? Although it is true that you never know anything when I am serious﻿ ﻿… because﻿ ﻿… you see?﻿ ﻿… I don’t want my feet to rest against the board. When I am dead﻿ ﻿… you shall put a cushion inside and my white dress﻿ ﻿… you know the one with pink flowers﻿ ﻿… the dress for which I won the first prize! You’ll be very sorry, my poor little thing, won’t you? Embrace me! Come over here, closer to me, still closer. I adore you!’
===Chinese===
“另一些时候，她变得若有所思、正正经经：‘亲爱的，我想问你一件让我烦了很久的事；你答应要告诉我真话。’我答应了。‘那么，人死了，装进棺材里，脚真是顶着那块挡板的么？’‘亏你想得出！说这个干什么？’‘告诉我，求你告诉我！’‘我可不知道，亲爱的朱丽叶特！’‘你不知道？不过也难怪，我说正经话的时候，你从来什么都不知道……因为……你明白么？……我不愿意我的脚顶着那块挡板。等我死了……你要在里头垫一个靠枕，给我穿那条白裙子……你知道，有粉红花的那条……就是靠它得过头奖的那条！你会很伤心的，我可怜的小宝贝，是不是？抱抱我！到这儿来，靠近我，再靠近些。我爱你！’

===Original===
“And I used to wish that Juliette were sick all the time! But as soon as she recovers she does not remember anything; her promises, her resolutions are gone and our life of hell begins again, more violent and exasperating than ever. And from that little bit of heaven to which I have held on for a while, I tumble down again into the filth and crime of this love even more frightfully maimed in spirit! Ah! that is not all, Lirat! I should have stayed in that apartment to brood over my shame, don’t you think! I should have withdrawn into obscurity and oblivion sufficient to make people believe that I am dead. And instead of that! Well! Go to the Bois and you see me there every day. At the theatre it is I whom you will find in the stage box, in a dress suit, with a flower in my buttonhole, always I! Juliette is resplendent amidst flowers, plumes and gems. She is exquisite, she has a new dress which everyone admires, a stock of smiles each more modest than the other, and the string of pearls, for which I have not paid, which she toys gracefully with the tips of her fingers and without the least remorse. And here I have not a sou, not a sou! And I am at the end of my rope, having exhausted all my swindling tricks and crooked schemes! Often I tremble. It seems to me that the heavy hand of a gendarme is bearing down upon me. Already I hear the painful whisper, I catch the stealthy looks of contempt.
===Chinese===
“我竟常常盼着朱丽叶特一直病下去！可她一好，就什么都不记得了；她的许诺、她的决心统统烟消云散，我们的地狱日子重新开场，比先前更狂暴、更气人。我刚刚在那一小片天上攀附了片刻，又跌进这场爱情的污秽和罪孽里，精神上摔得比先前更遍体鳞伤！啊！这还没完呢，利拉！我本该守在那套公寓里，闷着头咀嚼我的耻辱，你说是不是！本该退进足够的昏暗和遗忘里去，好叫人家相信我已经死了。可实际上呢！好啊！你到布洛涅林园去，天天都碰得见我。上剧院，在舞台包厢里找到的准是我，一身礼服，钮扣眼里插一朵花，回回都是我！朱丽叶特在花丛、羽饰和珠光宝气里光彩夺目。她风致绝佳，她有一条人人夸的新裙子，一整套微笑，一副比一副娴静，还有那串我没付账的珍珠项链，她用指尖优雅地拨弄着，没有半点愧色。而我呢，一个苏也没有，一个苏也没有！我已经山穷水尽，坑蒙拐骗的花招、歪门邪道的算计全都使尽了！我常常发抖。我觉得宪兵那只沉重的手正朝我压下来。刺人的低语我已经听见了，偷偷投来的轻蔑眼色，我也已经捉住了。

===Original===
“Little by little emptiness broadens and recedes all around me as around a pestiferous person. Old friends pass by, turn their heads away, avoid me in order not to greet me.﻿ ﻿… And unwillingly I assume the sly and servile manner of disreputable people who walk with eyes asquint and cringing back in search of an outstretched hand! The horrible thing about it, you see, is that I am perfectly conscious of the fact that it is Juliette’s beauty that protects me. It is the desire which she awakens, it is her mouth, it is the mystery of her nude and defiled body which in this pleasure-seeking world shields me with a false esteem, with a lying semblance of respect. A handshake, a grateful look seems to say: ‘I have been with your Juliette, and I owe that to you. Perhaps you prefer money? Do you want it?’ Yes, just let me quit Juliette and with one kick I shall even be thrown out of this crowd, this facile, fawning and perverted crowd and shall be reduced to sordid association with gamblers and pimps!”
===Chinese===
“渐渐地，我四周空了出去，人人往后退，仿佛围着一个带疫的人。旧日的相识打旁边走过，把头扭开，躲着我，免得同我打招呼。……我不知不觉也摆出那班不体面人的狡黠和奴态，斜着眼、缩着背走路，指望寻到一只伸过来的手！这事情的可怕之处，你瞧，就在于我心知肚明：庇护着我的，正是朱丽叶特的美貌。是她唤起的欲念，是她的嘴，是她那裸露而遭蹂躏的肉体的神秘，在这个寻欢作乐的世界里，拿一种虚假的敬重、一种说谎似的尊敬皮面，遮护着我。一次握手、一道感激的眼风，仿佛在说：‘我同你的朱丽叶特好过，这份人情我欠你的。兴许你宁愿要钱？你要么？’是啊，只消我一离开朱丽叶特，人家一脚就会把我从这班人里踢出去，从这班轻浮、逢迎而邪僻的人物里踢出去，我就只得同赌棍和拉皮条的为伍，陷进肮脏的交往里去！

===Original===
I burst out sobbing. Lirat did not stir, did not raise his head. Motionless, with clasped hands he was looking at something I knew not what﻿ ﻿… nothing, I suppose. After a few moments of silence I continued:

“My good Lirat, do you remember our talks in your studio! I used to listen to you, and what you told me was so beautiful! Without suspecting it, perhaps, you awoke noble desires and sublime raptures in me. You breathed into me a little of the belief, ambition and lofty flights of your soul. You taught me how to read nature, to understand her passionate tongue, to feel the emotions latent in things. You proved to me the existence of immortal beauty. You said to me: ‘Love, why it is in the earthenware pitcher, it is in the verminous rags which I paint. To take a feeling, a joy, a moment of suffering, of palpitation, a vision, a shudder﻿—anything, no matter how fugitive an experience of life it may be﻿—and recreate it, fix it in colors, in words or sounds, means to love! Love is a man’s yearning to create!’

“And I dreamed of becoming a great artist! Ah! my dreams, my delights in being able to perceive things, my doubts, my sacred agonies, do you remember them? Look what I have done with all that! I wanted to love and I went to a woman who kills love. I started with wings, drunk with the air, with the azure, with light! And now I am nothing but a dirty hog, sunk in its filth, with greedy snout and sides shaking with impure rutting. You can see for yourself, Lirat, that I am lost, lost, lost!﻿ ﻿… and that I must kill myself.”
===Chinese===
我放声呜咽起来。利拉一动不动，也没有抬头。他合着双手，直勾勾望着一样我说不上是什么的东西……我想，多半什么也没有。沉默了片刻，我又接着说：

“我的好利拉，你还记得在你画室里的那些长谈么！我一向听着你说，你讲的话多美啊！也许你自己也没有觉察，你在我心里唤醒了高贵的欲望和崇高的狂喜。你朝我心里吹进了一丝你灵魂里的信念、抱负和凌云的高翔。你教我怎样读自然，听懂她热情的言语，感受事物中潜伏的情绪。你向我证明了不朽的美是有的。你对我说：‘爱么，它就在那只陶土罐子里，就在我画的那些生满虱子的破衣烂布上。取一种感情、一回欢乐、一刻苦楚、一阵心跳、一个幻影、一阵战栗——什么都行，无论它是对人生多么倏忽的一番经历——把它再造出来，用颜色、用文字或音响固定下来，这就叫作爱！爱，就是人想要创造的那种渴望！’

“我还梦想过做个大艺术家呢！啊！我的那些梦想，我感知万物时的种种欢悦，我的疑虑，我神圣的苦闷，你还记得么？瞧瞧我把这一切糟蹋成了什么样子！我本想要爱，却投到一个扼杀爱的女人怀里去了。我是张着翅膀起程的，醉在空气里、蓝天里、光明里！如今我不过是一头脏猪，陷在自己的污秽里，贪馋的鼻子拱来拱去，两肋随着不洁的兽欲直打颤。你自己也看得出来，利拉，我是完了，完了，完了！……我非自杀不可。”

===Original===
Then Lirat approached and put both hands on my shoulders:
===Chinese===
这时利拉走上前来，把两只手按在我的肩上：

===Original===
“You say you are lost! Let us see now; when one is of your stock, can one say that a man’s life is lost? You say you must kill yourself? Does a man who has typhoid fever say: ‘I must kill myself?’ He says: ‘I must cure myself!’ You have typhoid fever, my poor child﻿ ﻿… cure yourself. Lost! Why, there is not a crime, do you hear me, there is not a crime, no matter how monstrous and vile, that can not be redeemed by forgiveness. I don’t mean God’s forgiveness or man’s forgiveness, but one’s own forgiveness, which is much more difficult and more worth while to obtain. Lost! I was listening to you, my dear Mintié, and do you know what I was thinking? I was thinking that you had the noblest and most beautiful soul that I ever knew. No, no﻿ ﻿… a man who accuses himself as you do﻿ ﻿… who puts into his confession of sin the heartrending accents which you have put in yours just now﻿ ﻿… why no﻿—that man is never lost. On the contrary, he finds himself again and he is near redemption. Love has passed over you and has left all the more filth in its wake because of your extremely delicate nature. Well! You must wash this filth off﻿—and I know where the water is that will wash it off. You are going to leave this place﻿ ﻿… leave Paris.”
===Chinese===
“你说你完了！我们来想想看；一个人既然生就你这样的根器，还能说他的一生是完了的么？你说你非自杀不可？一个人害了伤寒，难道他会说：‘我非自杀不可？’他说的是：‘我得把病治好！’你害的是伤寒，我可怜的孩子……把病治好吧。完了！咳，天底下没有一桩罪，你听见没有，没有一桩罪，不管它多么骇人、多么卑污，是不能靠宽恕来赎的。我说的不是上帝的宽恕，也不是旁人的宽恕，而是一个人自己的宽恕——那才是难得多、也更值得去求得的。完了！我方才听着你说，亲爱的曼蒂埃，你知道我在想什么？我在想，你有着我平生所知最高贵、最美的灵魂。不，不……一个像你这样控诉自己的人……一个像你方才那样，在认罪的供词里倾进那样摧心裂肺的声调的人……绝不会完的。恰恰相反，他会重新找回自己，他就站在得救的门口了。爱情从你身上碾过去了，只因为你天性极端娇嫩，它一路才留下这么多的污秽。好啊！你得把这污秽洗掉——我知道哪儿有能洗掉它的水。你要离开这个地方……离开巴黎。”

===Original===
“Lirat!” I entreated, “don’t ask me to leave! I have tried it twenty times and I cannot do it.”

“You are going away,” repeated Lirat, whose face suddenly darkened. “Or else I am mistaken about you, and you are a scamp!”

He resumed:
===Chinese===
“利拉！”我恳求道，“别叫我走！这话我试过二十回了，可就是做不到。”

“你非走不可。”利拉重说了一遍，脸色忽然阴沉下来。“不然的话，就是我看错了你，你就是个无赖！”

他又接着说：

===Original===
“In the heart of Brittany there is a fishing village, which is called Le Ploch. The air there is pure, nature is superb, man rugged and kind. It is there that you are going to live three months, six months, a year if necessary. You will walk along the sandy shore, across the heath, through pine forests, over rocks; you will dig the soil, you will catch sea wrack, you will lift logs, you will shout in the wind. There, at last, you will subdue this poisoned body insane with love. In the beginning it will be hard for you and you will perhaps feel homesick﻿ ﻿… you will rebel, you will be seized with passionate desires to return. Don’t be discouraged, I beseech you. On days especially hard to bear, walk all the more﻿ ﻿… spend nights out on the sea with the brave people of the place﻿ ﻿… and when your heart is heavy, weep, weep. Above all, keep from leading an indolent life, from dreaming, from reading, from carving your name on the rocks and tracing it on the sand. Don’t think of anything, don’t think at all! On such occasions, literature and art are poor counsellors, they are apt to bring you back to love again. Incessant activity of your body, hard physical labor, your flesh worn out by crushing fatigue, your head lashed and made giddy by the wind, by the rain, by storms! I tell you, you will come back from that place not only cured but stronger than ever and better armed for struggle. And you shall have paid your debt to that monster. You say, you shall have paid it with your fortune? Well what of it, that’s nothing. Why, I envy you and wish I could go with you. Come, my dear Mintié, a little courage! Go!”
===Chinese===
“在布列塔尼（Brittany）腹地有一个渔村，叫勒普洛什（Le Ploch）。那儿的空气纯净，自然壮美，人粗犷而厚道。你就要到那儿去住上三个月，六个月，必要的话住上一年。你要沿着沙滩走，穿过石楠荒原，穿过松林，翻过岩石；你要掘地，要捞海藻，要搬木头，要顶着风喊叫。在那儿，你终会制伏这具中了毒、为爱发狂的身体。开头你会觉得难熬，也许会想家……你会抗拒，会一阵阵按捺不住地想回来。别灰心，我求你。日子格外难挨的时候，就格外多走路……跟当地那些勇敢的人们出海过夜……心里沉重的时候，就哭，哭个够。千万要紧的是：别过懒散的日子，别做梦，别读书，别在岩石上刻你的名字、在沙上描你的名字。什么都别想，干脆什么都别想！在这种时候，文学和艺术是蹩脚的参谋，它们很容易把你重新带回爱情里去。让身体不停地动，干重体力活，让肌肉被压垮人的疲乏磨乏，让脑袋被风、被雨、被风暴抽打得发昏！我告诉你，你从那儿回来的时候，不但病好了，还会比从前更强壮，更经得起斗争。到那时，你欠那怪物的债也算还清了。你自己说的，这笔债你是拿家财还的？那算得了什么，不值一提。咳，我还眼红你呢，恨不得跟你一块儿去。来吧，亲爱的曼蒂埃，拿出点勇气来！去吧！”

===Original===
“Yes, Lirat, you are right. I must go away.”

“Well go then!”

“I am going away tomorrow, I swear!”

“Tomorrow? Ah, tomorrow! She is going to come back, isn’t that the idea? And you will throw yourself in her arms again. No, go now!”

“Let me write to her. I can’t leave her like this, without a word, without saying goodbye to her. Lirat just think! In spite of all this suffering, in spite of all this shame, there still are happy memories, blissful hours. She is not wicked﻿ ﻿… she simply does not know﻿ ﻿… that’s all﻿ ﻿… but she loves me. I shall go away, I promise you I shall. But give me just one more day! One more day! One day is not much, especially since I shall not see her anymore! Ah, one more day!”

“No, go now!”

“Lirat! My good Lirat!”

“No!”
===Chinese===
“是的，利拉，你说得对。我必须走。”

“那就走啊！”

“我明天就走，我发誓！”

“明天？啊，明天！她就要回来了，你心里打的是这个主意吧？你又要扑进她的怀抱里去了。不行，现在就走！”

“让我给她写封信。我不能就这样走，一句话不留，连个再见也不说。利拉，你想想！尽管受了这许多苦、这许多耻辱，到底还有过幸福的回忆、极乐的时辰。她不坏……她只是不知道……就是这么回事……可她是爱我的。我会走的，我答应你一定走。可再给我一天吧！就一天！一天不算什么，何况往后我再也见不着她了！啊，再给一天！”

“不行，现在就走！”

“利拉！我的好利拉！”

“不行！”

===Original===
“But I have no money! How do you expect me to go without money?”

“I have enough left to last you over the trip; I’ll send it to you there. Go!”

“At least let me get my things ready!”

“I have some wool stockings and caps; that’s what you need. Go!”

He hurried me away. Without seeing anything, without realizing anything, I went through the apartment, bumping into pieces of furniture. I did not feel any pain, for I was insensible to everything; I was walking behind Lirat with the heavy step and the passive gait of a beast led to slaughter.
===Chinese===
“可我没有钱！没有钱，你叫我怎么走？”

“我手头剩的钱还够供你这趟路程；到了那边我给你汇去。走！”

“至少让我把行李收拾收拾！”

“我有几双毛线袜子和几顶便帽；你要用的就是这个。走！”

他催着我走。我什么也看不见，什么也明白不了，一路穿过公寓，撞在家具上。我并不觉得疼，因为我对一切都麻木了；我跟在利拉身后走着，脚步沉重，神情驯顺，像一头牵去宰杀的牲口。

===Original===
“Well where is your hat?”

“That’s right! I went out without a hat. I did not think that I was abandoning, that I was leaving behind anything that was a part of me; that the things which I saw, in the midst of which I lived, were dying one after another as soon as I passed by them.”
===Chinese===
“嗳，你的帽子呢？”

“对了！我出来时没戴帽子。我没有想到，我这是在抛弃、在丢下一样曾是我身上一部分的东西；也没有想到，我看见的这些、我置身其间的这些东西，随着我一步步走过去，正一件接着一件地死去。”

===Original===
The train left at eight o’clock in the evening. Lirat did not leave me all day. Wishing, no doubt, to occupy my mind and to keep my willpower at its highest pitch, he spoke to me with broad gestures; but I did not hear anything except a confused noise, annoying me and buzzing about my ears like pestering flies. We dined in a restaurant near the Mont Parnasse railroad station. Lirat continued to talk, stupefying me with gestures and words, tracing strange geographic lines with his knife on the table.

“Look, there’s where it is! Then you will follow this side﻿ ﻿… and﻿ ﻿…”

I believe he was giving me instructions about my trip to the place of exile I was bound for﻿ ﻿… told me the names of villages, persons. The word “sea” recurred again and again with the rumble of pebbles, washed by the waves and rubbing against one another.
===Chinese===
火车晚上八点开。利拉一整天都没有离开我。他多半是想占据住我的心思，让我的意志一直绷得紧紧的，说话时做出大幅的手势；可我什么也没听见，只听见一片嘈杂，在我耳边嗡嗡盘旋，像扰人的苍蝇。我们在蒙帕纳斯（Mont Parnasse）火车站附近一家饭馆里吃了晚饭。利拉还是说个不停，用手势和言语把我轰得发懵，还用刀子在桌上划出一条条奇怪的地理线路。

“瞧，就在这儿！然后你顺着这边……再……”

我想，他这是在给我交代去那处流放地的路程……报着村名、人名。“海”这个词一遍又一遍地出现，带着卵石翻滚的声响——浪头冲刷着卵石，卵石彼此摩着肩。

===Original===
“Will you remember?”

And without knowing exactly what he referred to, I answered:

“Yes, yes, I’ll remember.”

It was only at the station, this vast building, filled with noise and bustle, that I realized my situation. I felt terribly downhearted. And so I was going away! It’s all over then! Never again shall I see Juliette, never again! At this moment I forgot all my suffering, my shame, my ruin, the irreparable conduct of Juliette and remembered only our brief moments of happiness, and I rebelled against the injustice of being separated from my well-beloved. Lirat meantime was saying:
===Chinese===
“你记得住么？”

我不很明白他指的是什么，只答道：

“记得，记得，我记住了。”

直到进了车站，进了那座人声嘈杂、熙来攘往的庞大建筑，我才明白自己的处境。我心里沮丧到了极点。这么说，我真的要走了！那就全完了！我再也见不到朱丽叶特了，再也见不到了！这时候，我把自己的苦楚、耻辱、倾家荡产，把朱丽叶特那无可挽回的所作所为，统统忘掉了，只记起我们那些短促的幸福时光，我为硬生生拆散我和心爱的人这不公道而愤愤不平。利拉这时候还在说：

===Original===
“And then, if you only knew what a bliss it is to live among the lowly, to study their poor but worthy life, their resignation of martyrs, their.﻿ ﻿…”

I had a notion to escape his surveillance, to flee then and there. A foolish hope kept me from doing that. I said to myself: “Celestine will no doubt bring word to Juliette that Lirat has been at the house, that he has led me away by force; she will understand at once that something horrible is happening, that I am at this station, that I am going to leave. And she will come running.” I really believed she would. So strong was my faith that through the large open windows, I watched the people who were entering; I searched among the various groups, examined closely the dense crowd of passengers standing in front of the track gate. And whenever some elegant lady appeared I gave a start, ready to dart toward her. Lirat went on:
===Chinese===
“再说，你要是知道生活在卑贱的人们中间是多大的福气就好了——去体察他们贫穷却正直的生活，他们殉道者一般的顺从，他们的……”

我起意要摆脱他的看管，当场就逃。一个痴愚的指望拦住了我。我对自己说：“塞莱斯特准会把话捎给朱丽叶特，说利拉到家里来过，把我硬架走了；她立刻就会明白出了不得了的事，明白我就在这个车站，马上要走了。她就会跑来的。”我真信她会来。我的信念那么强，以至隔着敞开的大窗，我盯着进站的人们；我在一堆一堆的人里搜寻，细细打量站台栅栏口前密密匝匝的旅客。每逢哪位衣着雅致的太太一露面，我心里就是一跳，随时要向她扑过去。利拉还在往下说：

===Original===
“And to think that there are some people who consider them brutes, these heroes! Ah! you will see those magnificent brutes with their horny hands, their eyes full of infinitude, and their backs which make one weep.”

Even on the platform I was still hoping for Juliette’s arrival. Surely in a second she will be here, pale, vanquished, suppliant, with outstretched arms: “My Jean, my Jean, I was a bad woman, forgive me! Don’t bear me ill will on account of that, don’t forsake me. What do you expect me to become, without you? Oh, come back, my Jean, or else take me along!” And silhouettes flitted and disappeared in the cars; fantastic shadows crept along and split against the walls; long whitish columns of smoke spread out under the vaulted roof.﻿ ﻿…
===Chinese===
“可你想想，竟有人说他们是畜生，这些英雄！啊！你会看见那些气概非凡的畜生：手上长着老茧，眼里盛着无穷，还有那叫人落泪的脊梁。”

直到站台上，我还在盼着朱丽叶特来。再有一秒钟她准到了，脸色苍白，败下阵来，苦苦哀求，张开双臂：“我的让，我的让，我是个坏女人，饶了我吧！别为这个记恨我，别抛下我。没有你，你要我变成什么样子呢？啊，回来吧，我的让，不然就带我一起走！”一条条人影在车厢里掠过、消逝；一片片怪影贴着墙爬行、碎裂；一柱柱发白的浓烟在穹顶下散开。……

===Original===
“Embrace me, my dear Mintié. Embrace me!”

Lirat drew me close to his breast. He was crying. “Write to me as soon as you get there. Goodbye!”

He pushed me into a car and drew the door curtain.

“Goodbye!”
===Chinese===
“拥抱我一下吧，亲爱的曼蒂埃。拥抱我！”

利拉把我搂到胸前。他哭了。“一到那边就给我写信。再见！”

他把我推进一节车厢，拉上了车门的帘子。

“再见！”

===Original===
A whistle, then a dull rolling﻿ ﻿… then lights chasing one another﻿ ﻿… things receding somewhere﻿ ﻿… then nothing﻿ ﻿… except black night. Why did Juliette not come? Why? And in the midst of rumpled skirts on the carpets, in her dressing room, in front of her looking glass, I clearly see her, bare-shouldered, applying rice powder to her face. Celestine with her soft flaccid fingers is sewing on a band of crepe at the bottom of the low-cut waist, and a man whom I don’t know, reclining on the sofa, with crossed legs, watches Juliette with eyes in which desire is gleaming. The gas is burning, candlelights are blazing, a bouquet of roses which someone has just brought, mingles its more delicate perfume with the violent odors of dresses! And Juliette takes a rose, twists its stem, straightens out its petals and sticks it in the buttonhole of the man with a tender smile. A bonnet with hanging strings is perched on top of a chandelier.﻿ ﻿…

And the train is moving on, puffing, panting. The night is ever black, and I am plunging into nothingness.﻿ ﻿…
===Chinese===
一声汽笛，随后是沉闷的滚动……随后是互相追逐的灯火……向什么地方退远的事物……随后什么也没有了……只有黑黢黢的夜。朱丽叶特为什么不来？为什么？而在地毯上揉皱的裙裾中间，在她的梳妆室里，在她的穿衣镜前，我清清楚楚看见她：光着肩膀，往脸上扑着米粉香粉。塞莱斯特用她那双绵软松弛的手指，在低胸衣的下缘缝一道绉纱带子；一个我不认识的男人，斜倚在沙发上，架着腿，望着朱丽叶特，眼睛里闪着欲念。煤气灯燃着，烛火通明，一束人家刚送来的玫瑰，把它清幽的香气，混进裙衣浓烈的气味里！朱丽叶特拿起一朵玫瑰，捻着花茎，抚平花瓣，带着温存的微笑，把它插进那男人的钮扣眼。一顶垂着系带的软帽，端端正正架在枝形吊灯顶上。……

火车一路开下去，喷着气，喘着息。夜永远是黑的，我正一头扎进虚无之中。……

## X / 十

===Original===

It is a week since I have been able to sleep. There is a hood of red hot iron upon my head. My blood thickens, one might say that my dilated arteries were bursting, and I have the sensation of tongues of fire licking my loins. Whatever human qualities there still remained in me, what little shame, remorse, self-respect and vague hopes buried under the heap of filth have been left in me by moral suffering, the little that has still held me bound by a thread, be it ever so weak, to thinking creatures﻿—all this has now been destroyed by the madness of a frenzied brute. No longer do I entertain thoughts of Good, Truth, Justice, the inflexible laws of nature. I am no longer conscious of the sexual aversion which exists between the various species in the animal kingdom, keeping the world in constant harmony: everything is in a whirl, everything is confused into one tremendous and sterile carnal essence and, in the delirium of my senses, I rave only of unnatural embraces. Not only does the image of prostituted Juliette no longer torment me, but on the contrary it excites my passions! And in my mind I seek, I cling to her, I try to fix her in my memory by ineffaceable marks, I confound her with things, with beasts, with monstrous creatures and I myself lead her to criminal debauchery, spurred on by burning pains. Juliette is no longer the only image that tempts and haunts me. Gabrielle, the Rabineau woman, Mother Le Gannec, Demoiselle Landudec, pass before my eyes in wanton postures. Neither virtue, nor goodness, nor unhappiness, nor sacred old age holds me back, and for the scene of these frightful frenzies I purposely choose holy and hallowed places, altars in churches, tombs at the cemeteries. I no longer suffer in my soul; I suffer only in my flesh. My soul died in Juliette’s last kiss, and now I am nothing but a form of foul, sensuous flesh, into which demons have been furiously at work pouring streams of molten, seething metal. Oh! I could never have forseen such castigation!

The other day I met a fisherwoman on the strand. She was black, dirty, foul-smelling, like a heap of putrified sea wrack. I made advances to her with silly gestures. And suddenly I fled, for I felt a diabolic temptation to rush upon her and throw her down amid the pebbles and small pools of water. I roamed and tramped across the country with dilated nostrils, taking in, like a harrier, the odor of sex.﻿ ﻿… One night, with burning throat, driven mad by abominable visions, I found my way into the crooked alleys of the village and rapped at the door of a loose woman. And I went into this den. But as soon as I felt the unknown contact I uttered a cry of rage; I wanted to leave; she held me back.

===Chinese===

一个星期以来，我一直无法入睡。有一顶烧红的铁盔箍在我头上。我的血液变稠了，简直可以说，我那涨粗的动脉就要爆裂，我觉出一条条火舌在舔着我的腰。我身上还剩下的那点人性——那一点点羞耻、悔恨、自尊，还有那一点点埋在污秽堆底下、由精神的苦痛留给我的模糊希望，还有那仍旧靠一根线——哪怕细得不能再细——把我同有思想的人们牵在一处的那一点点——这一切，如今都被一头发狂的畜生的疯狂毁掉了。我心里不再存着善、真理、正义，不再存着自然那些不可变更的法则。我再也意识不到动物界各物种之间存在的那种性的排斥——正是它使世界保持着恒久的和谐：一切都在打旋，一切都混淆成一种巨大的、不能孕育的肉欲本质，在感官的谵妄中，我满口呓语的尽是些悖逆自然的拥抱。堕落为娼的朱丽叶特的形象非但不再折磨我，反倒撩拨起我的情欲！我在心里寻觅她、抓住她不放，想用抹不掉的印记把她固定在记忆里；我把她同器物、同畜生、同种种怪物搅在一起，我自己也在灼痛的鞭策下，把她引向罪恶的放纵。朱丽叶特已不再是唯一诱惑我、纠缠我的形象。加布里埃尔、拉比诺家的那个女人、勒加内克大妈（Mother Le Gannec）、兰迪德克小姐（Demoiselle Landudec），都以淫荡的姿态从我眼前走过。无论是美德、善良、不幸，还是神圣的老年，都拦不住我；而且我还故意拣选圣洁的处所——教堂里的祭坛、墓地里的坟茔——来做这等骇人狂乱的场面。我不再在灵魂里受苦；我只在肉体里受苦。我的灵魂死在了朱丽叶特最后的一吻里，如今我不过是一具污秽而耽于淫乐的皮囊，恶魔们正发疯似的往里面倾灌一股股熔化沸腾的金属。啊！我从来不曾料到会有这样的酷刑！

前几天，我在海滩上遇见一个渔妇。她黑黢黢、脏兮兮，浑身恶臭，像一堆腐烂的海草。我做着愚蠢的手势向她挑逗。可我突然逃开了，因为我觉出一种魔鬼般的诱惑，想朝她扑上去，把她按倒在卵石和一洼洼积水中间。我张着鼻孔在乡野间游荡奔波，像一只鹞子似的嗅着性的气味。……一天夜里，我喉咙火烧火燎，被可憎的幻象逼疯了，摸进村里弯弯曲曲的小巷，敲响了一个私娼的门。于是我走进了这个窝。可刚一触到那陌生的肉体，我就发出一声狂怒的叫喊；我要走；她拉住了我。

===Original===

“Let me go!” I shouted.

“Why are you going away?”

“Let me go!”

“Stay here. I’ll love you. I often followed you on the beach. I often roamed about the house where you are staying. I wanted you. Stay here!”

“Let me go, I tell you! You don’t know how disgusting you are to me!”

===Chinese===

「放开我！」我喊道。

「你为什么要走？」

「放开我！」

「留下吧。我会爱你的。我常常在海滩上跟着你。我常在你住的那所房子周围转悠。我想要你。留下吧！」

「放开我，我跟你说！你不知道你让我有多恶心！」

===Original===

And when she hung on my neck, I struck her. She groaned.

“Ah! My God! He is mad!”

Mad! Yes, I am mad! I have looked at myself in the mirror and I am afraid of my own image. My distended eyes shine from the midst of their orbits which are hollow; my bones protrude from under the yellow skin; my mouth is pale, trembling, hanging like the mouths of lascivious old men. My gestures are erratic, and my fingers, constantly agitated by nervous shocks, crack, seeking a prey in the air.

Mad! Yes, I am mad! Whenever Mother Le Gannec is moving about me, when I hear her slippers dragging on the floor, when her dress brushes against me, criminal notions come and take possession of me; they pursue me and I cry:

“Go away, Mother Le Gannec, go away.”

===Chinese===

可当她搂上我的脖子时，我打了她。她呻吟了一声。

「啊！我的上帝！他疯了！」

疯了！是的，我疯了！我在镜子里照过自己，我怕自己的影子。我那双睁大的眼睛从深陷的眼窝里闪着光；骨头从发黄的皮肤底下凸出来；我的嘴苍白、颤抖、耷拉着，像好色的老头们的嘴。我的动作慌乱无定，手指头让神经的震颤抖个不停，噼啪作响，在空中摸索着搜寻猎物。

疯了！是的，我疯了！每当勒加内克大妈在我身旁走动，当我听见她的拖鞋在地板上拖沓作响，当她的裙子擦到我身上，犯罪的念头便袭来，攫住我；它们追逼着我，我就喊：

「走开，勒加内克大妈，走开。」

===Original===

Mad! Yes, I am mad! Often at night I stand for hours at the door of her room, my hand upon the knob, ready to plunge into the darkness of the room. I don’t know what is holding me back. Fear, no doubt, for I say to myself: “She will struggle, cry, call for help and I shall be compelled to kill her!” Once, alarmed by the noise, she got up, bare-legged; she was dumbfounded for a moment, upon beholding me.

“What is the matter! It’s you, friend Mintié? What are you doing here? Are you ill?”

I stammered some incoherent words and went upstairs to my room.

Ah! Let them drive me out, beat me, with forks, stakes, scythes. Is it possible that men will not come in here in a moment, rush upon me, gag and drag me into the eternal night of the dungeon?

I must go away! I must find Juliette again! I must vent this accursed madness upon her!

When dawn came I went downstairs and said to Mother Le Gannec: “I must leave! Let me have some money. I shall pay it back to you later. Let me have some money. I must leave!”

===Chinese===

疯了！是的，我疯了！常常在夜里，我在她房门口一站就是几个钟头，手搭在门把上，随时准备一头扎进房里的黑暗。我不知道还有什么在拦着我。多半是害怕吧，因为我对自己说：「她会挣扎，会喊叫，会呼救，那我就不得不杀了她！」有一回，她被响动惊起，下了床，光着两条腿；一见我，她愣了好一会儿。

「怎么啦！是您哪，曼蒂埃朋友？您在这儿做什么？您病了吗？」

我结结巴巴说了几句不连贯的话，上楼回自己房里去了。

啊！让他们来赶我走吧，用叉子、木桩、长柄镰刀打死我吧。难道就不会马上有人闯进这儿来，扑到我身上，堵住我的嘴，把我拖进地牢那永恒的黑夜里去吗？

我必须离开！我必须再找到朱丽叶特！我必须把这该诅咒的疯狂发泄到她身上去！

天一亮，我就下了楼，对勒加内克大妈说：「我得走了！给我些钱吧。日后我会还给您的。给我些钱吧。我得走了！」

## XI / 十一

===Original===
Juliette had chosen a room for me on the second floor of a furnished house in the Faubourg Saint Honoré near the Rue de Balzac. The furniture of the room was rickety, the tapestry worn, the drawers creaked when opened, the pungent odor of decaying wood and accumulated dust filled the window curtains and bedstead hangings; but by placing knickknacks here and there, she succeeded in imparting an air of intimacy to this banal, cold place, where so many unknown lives had been spent without a trace being left behind. Juliette reserved to herself the task of arranging my things in the hanging-press which she filled with bunches of fragrant flowers.

“You see, my dear, here are your socks, and there are your nightshirts. I put your neckties in the drawer; your handkerchiefs are there. I hope your little wifie has put everything in order. And every day I’ll bring you a sweet-smelling flower. Now don’t be sad. Tell yourself that I love you, that I love no one but you, that I shall come often. Oh, I have forgotten a few things! Well, I’ll send them to you with Celestine, together with my pictures in the beautiful red plush frames. Don’t feel lonesome, my poor, little thing! You know, if I am not here at half-past twelve tonight don’t wait for me. Go to bed. Sleep well. Promise me?”

And casting a last glance about the room, she left. Indeed, Juliette came every day, while going to the Bois and on her way home before dinner. She never remained more than two minutes at a time. Excited, impelled by a feverish desire to be outside, she would stay long enough to embrace me and to open the drawers to see whether my things were in order.
===Chinese===
朱丽叶特在圣奥诺雷区（Faubourg Saint Honoré）巴尔扎克街（Rue de Balzac）附近一家带家具出租的公寓里，替我挑了二楼的一个房间。屋里的家具摇摇晃晃，壁布破旧，抽屉一拉就咯咯作响，朽木和积尘的呛人气味浸透了窗帘和床帏；可是她东一件西一件地摆上些小玩意儿，竟让这间平庸、冷清的屋子有了几分亲切的气息——不知多少陌生的生活在这里流逝过，没有留下一丝痕迹。把我那些衣物归置进壁柜，这件事朱丽叶特留给了自己，她还往柜子里插满了一束束芬芳的花。

“你瞧，亲爱的，这儿是你的袜子，那儿是你的睡衣。我把你的领带放进抽屉了；手帕在那边。但愿你家小媳妇儿把什么都归置好了。往后我每天都给你带一朵香喷喷的花来。好了，别难过。你要这样想：我爱你，我只爱你一个，我会常来。啊，有几样东西我忘了！没关系，我让塞莱斯特给你送来，连同我那些装在漂亮红丝绒框里的相片。别孤单单的，我可怜的小宝贝！你知道，今晚要是十二点半我还没到，就别等我了。上床去。好好睡。答应我？”

说完，她朝屋里环顾了最后一眼，走了。果然，朱丽叶特每天都来，去布洛涅森林（the Bois）时顺路，晚饭前回家的路上也顺路。她每回从不多待两分钟以上。她心绪亢奋，被一股非到外面去不可的狂热欲望驱赶着，停留的时间刚够搂我一下、拉开抽屉看看我的东西归置好了没有。

===Original===
“Well I am going. Don’t be sad. I see you have been crying. That is not nice at all! Why cause me aggravation?”

“Juliette! Will I see you tonight? Oh! please, tonight!”

“Tonight?”

She reflected for a minute.

“Tonight, yes, my dear! But still do not wait for me too long. Go to bed. Sleep well. Above all, don’t cry. You drive me to despair! Really, I don’t know what to do with you!”
===Chinese===
“好了，我走了。别难过。我看你哭过了。这可一点都不好！干吗要惹我烦心呢？”

“朱丽叶特！今晚我能见到你吗？啊！求你了，就在今晚！”

“今晚？”

她思量了一分钟。

“今晚，行，亲爱的！不过也别等我太久。上床去。好好睡。尤其别哭。你真要把我逼疯了！说真的，我都不知道拿你怎么办！”

===Original===
And so I lived here, stretched out on the sofa, never going out, counting the minutes which slowly, slowly, drop by drop, vanished into the eternity of waiting.

The frenzied excitement of my senses was succeeded by a period of great depression. I spent whole afternoons apathetically, without stirring, my body lifeless, my limbs hanging, my brains in a state of torpor, like the morrow of a day of drunkenness. My life resembled a heavy slumber disturbed by painful dreams, interrupted by sudden awakenings even more painful than the dreams; and in the annihilation of my willpower, in the blotting out of my intellect, I again felt, but more keenly than ever, the horror of my moral decay. In addition, Juliette’s life caused me perpetual anguish. As in the past, on the dune of Ploch, I could not dismiss from my mind the loathsome vision which grew, intensified and assumed even more cruel forms.﻿ ﻿… To lose a person whom you love, a person who has been the source of all your joys, the memory of whom is associated with happiness only, is a heartrending sorrow. But where there is sorrow there is also consolation, and suffering is eventually put to sleep, lulled in some way by its own tenderness. But here I was losing Juliette, losing her daily, every hour, every minute; and with this chain of successive deaths, with this process of impenitent dying, I could only associate memories of torture and disgrace.

No matter how eagerly I searched in the stirred-up depths of our two hearts for a flower bud, for a tiny blossom whose fragrance it would have been so sweet to inhale, I could not find it. And yet I could not conceive anything dissociated from Juliette. All my thoughts had Juliette for their starting point and for their final goal, and the more she escaped me the more fiercely obdurate I grew in my absurd desire to win her back. I had no hope at all that she would ever stop, carried away as she was by this life of evil pleasure; yet, in spite of myself, in spite of her, I was planning for a better future. I said to myself: “It is impossible that some day disgust will not seize her, that some day sorrow will not awaken remorse and pity in her heart. Then she will return to me. Then we will move into a plain workman’s house and I shall work like a galley-slave. I’ll enter journalism, I’ll publish novels, I’ll ask for a job as a plain copyist.” Alas! I forced myself to believe all this so as to accentuate the state of misery into which I had fallen. With the proceeds from the sale of two sketches by Lirat, of a few jewels I still had, of my books, I had realized a sum of four thousand francs, which I was saving like a treasure for that chimeric eventuality.
===Chinese===
我就这样在这里过活：直挺挺躺在长沙发上，从不出门，数着一分一秒——那么慢，那么慢，一滴一滴——没入等待的永恒之中。

感官那阵颠狂的亢奋过去之后，接踵而来的是一段深重消沉的日子。整个整个的下午，我麻木地捱着，一动不动，身子没有生气，四肢耷拉着，脑子昏沉僵滞，就像大醉一场后的第二天。我的生活好比一场沉甸甸的酣睡，被痛苦的梦搅扰着，又不时被骤然的惊醒打断，醒来的苦楚比梦境更甚；而在意志的泯灭里、理智的昏聩里，我比以往任何时候都更尖锐地感到自己道德沦丧的可怖。加之，朱丽叶特的生活叫我无时不在煎熬。一如从前在勒普洛什的沙丘上，我无法把那可憎的幻象从脑子里赶出去——它滋长着、加剧着，显出越来越残忍的形态。……失去一个你爱的人，一个曾是你全部欢乐之源、回忆里只连着幸福的人，是撕心裂肺的悲痛。但有悲痛处便有慰藉，苦难终会被哄入睡乡，被它自身的温存以某种方式抚平。可我这里，是在失去朱丽叶特，天天在失去她，每个钟头、每一分钟都在失去她；而伴着这一连串相继的死亡、这不知悔改的弥留过程，我能联想起来的，只有折磨与耻辱的记忆。

无论我怎样急切地在我们两颗心搅乱的深处搜寻一朵花苞、一朵小小的花——吸一口它的芬芳本该多么甜美——我都没能找到。然而我又无法设想任何与朱丽叶特不相干的东西。我全部的念头都以朱丽叶特为起点、为归宿；她越是挣脱我，我那把她赢回来的荒唐欲望就越执拗、越炽烈。她被这种邪恶行乐的生活裹挟着，我本不指望她有罢手的一天；可我仍旧身不由己，也不管她愿不愿意，一味盘算着一个较好的将来。我对自己说：“总有一天厌倦会攫住她，总有一天悲伤会在她心里唤醒悔恨与怜悯，这不可能不发生。到那时她就回到我身边来了。到那时我们就搬进一所普普通通的工人住宅，我要像苦役犯一样干活。我去干新闻这一行，我去出版小说，我去求一份抄写员的差事。”唉！我强迫自己相信这一切，好把我坠入的悲惨境地衬托得更深。凭着卖掉利拉的两幅速写、我尚存的几件首饰和我的那些书，我凑足了四千法郎，像守着财宝似的攒着，留给那个虚无缥缈的将来。

===Original===
One day when Juliette was pensive and tenderer than usual, I ventured to lay my project before her. She clasped her hands.

“Yes! Yes! Ah! Won’t that be nice! A little bit of an apartment, a tiny one. I’ll do the housekeeping. I’ll have pretty bonnets, a pretty apron! But with you it’ll be impossible! What a pity! It’s impossible!”

“Why is it impossible?”

“Because you won’t work and we’ll starve. That’s your nature! Did you work at Ploch. Are you working now? Why, you have never worked!”
===Chinese===
有一天，朱丽叶特心事重重，比平时温存，我壮起胆子把我的打算向她和盘托出。她拍起巴掌来。

“好呀！好呀！啊！那该多好！一小套房子，小小的一套。我来管家。我要戴漂亮的小帽，系一条好看的围裙！可是跟你嘛，那是办不到的！多可惜！办不到呀！”

“为什么办不到？”

“因为你不想干活，我们就得挨饿。你的本性就这样！在勒普洛什你干活了吗？现在你在干活吗？哼，你从来没干过活！”

===Original===
“How can I? Don’t you know that the thought of you never leaves me for a moment? It is the uncertainty of your life, it is the cruel anguish of everything I feel, of everything I suspect about you that gnaws at my heart, devours me, sucks my brains! When you are not here, I don’t know where you are! And still I am always with you wherever you are! Ah! if you only wished! To know that you are near me, loving and tranquil, far from everything that besmirches, from everything that torments. Why, I could then have the strength of a God in me! Money! Money! Why, I’ll make it for you by the shovelful, by the cartful! Ah! Juliette if you only wished.﻿ ﻿…”

She looked at me, excited by the great noise of gold which my words caused to ring in her ears.

“Well! Make it right away, dearie. Yes, make a lot of it, piles of it! And don’t think about those vile things which make you suffer! Men are so funny! They don’t want to understand anything!”
===Chinese===
“我怎么干得了？你难道不知道，你一刻也没有离开过我的心？是你生活的没个定准，是我对你的一切感受、一切猜疑带来的残酷煎熬，在啃噬我的心，把我吞掉，吸干我的脑髓！你不在跟前，我就不知道你在哪儿！可无论你在哪儿，我又总陪着你！啊！只要你肯！只要知道你在我身边，爱我、安安静静，远离一切玷污人的、折磨人的东西。那样我浑身就有神一般的力量！钱！钱！哼，我会成铲成车地给你挣来！啊！朱丽叶特，只要你肯。……”

她望着我，我的话在她耳畔敲出的这一片黄金的巨响，叫她兴奋起来。

“那好！这就去挣吧，宝贝儿。对，多多地挣，成堆地挣！别再想那些叫你受罪的脏事了！男人真是滑稽！他们什么都不肯明白！”

===Original===
Tenderly, she sat down on my lap.

“Why, I adore you, my dear little thing! Why, I detest the others and I give them nothing of myself, do you hear, nothing. I am very unhappy!”

With tear-filled eyes, she tried to nestle near me, repeating: “Yes very, very unhappy!”

I was seized with fear and pity.

“Ah! He thinks it is a pleasure!” she cried sobbing, “he thinks so! But if I did not have my Jean to console me, my Jean to lull me to sleep, my Jean to give me courage, I could not stand it any longer. I could not stand it any longer.﻿ ﻿… I would rather die.”
===Chinese===
她温柔地坐到我的膝头上。

“说真的，我崇拜你，我亲爱的小宝贝！说真的，我厌恶那些别的男人，我一点儿自己都不给他们，你听见没有，一点儿也不。我很不幸！”

她噙着满眶泪水，竭力往我怀里偎，一遍遍念叨：“是的，非常、非常不幸！”

我心里涌起了恐惧和怜悯。

“啊！他还以为那是快活事呢！”她哭着喊道，“他真这么想！可要不是有我的让安慰我，有我的让哄我入睡，有我的让给我打气，我再也撑不住了。再也撑不住了。……我宁可死掉。”

===Original===
Suddenly, changing the subject, and with a voice in which I seemed to hear a plaint of regret:

“First of all, you need money for that﻿—for the little apartment, I mean﻿ ﻿… and you haven’t got it!”

“Why yes, yes, my dear,” I exclaimed triumphantly, “I have some money. We have enough to live on for two months, three months while I make my fortune!”

“You have money? Let me see it.”

I showed her four one thousand franc bills. Juliette greedily snatched them one after another, counted them, examined them. Her eyes shone, surprised and delighted.
===Chinese===
突然，她岔开话头，声音里我仿佛听出一缕悔恨的哀怨：

“头一样，干那个得有钱——我是说那套小房子……可你没有钱！”

“有的，有的，亲爱的，”我得意扬扬地嚷道，“我有钱。够我们过上两个月、三个月，等我发迹！”

“你有钱？拿来我看看。”

我拿出四张一千法郎的钞票给她看。朱丽叶特贪馋地把它们一张张抢了过去，数了数，又翻来覆去地查验。她眼睛放光，又惊又喜。

===Original===
“Four thousand francs, dear! you really have four thousand francs! Why, you are rich! Well, well!”

She hung on my neck, caressed me.

“Well now,” she resumed, “since you are so rich, I should like to have a little traveling dressing case that I saw at the Rue de Paix. You will buy it for me, won’t you, dearie?”

I felt a tug at my heart so painful that I nearly fell to the floor, and a well of tears blinded me. Still I had the courage to ask:
===Chinese===
“四千法郎，亲爱的！你当真有四千法郎！哎呀，你发财啦！好呀，好呀！”

她勾住我的脖子，爱抚我。

“那么，”她又接上说，“你既然这么有钱，我想买一只小小的旅行梳妆盒，我在和平街（Rue de Paix）上看见的。你给我买下来，好不好，宝贝儿？”

我的心被揪得那么痛，几乎跌倒在地，一眶泪水模糊了我的眼睛。可我还是鼓起勇气问：

===Original===
“How much does your dressing case cost?”

“Two thousand francs, my dear.”

“All right! Take two thousand francs out of that. You’ll buy it yourself.”

Juliette kissed my forehead, took the two bills which she quickly hid in her coat pocket, and her gaze fixed on the two bills which still remained and for which she no doubt regretted she had not asked, she said:

“Really? Do you want me to? Ah! that’s nice! That will give me a chance to come to see you with my new dressing case, if you should return to Ploch.”
===Chinese===
“你的梳妆盒多少钱？”

“两千法郎，亲爱的。”

“行！从那笔钱里拿两千法郎去。你自己去买。”

朱丽叶特吻了吻我的额头，拿走两张钞票，飞快地塞进外衣口袋；她的眼睛还盯着剩下的那两张——没把它们也要过来，她八成觉得可惜——嘴里说：

“真的？你当真让我拿？啊！太好了！要是你回勒普洛什去，我正好带着我的新梳妆盒来看你。”

===Original===
When she was gone, I abandoned myself to an outburst of anger against her, above all against myself, and when the anger subsided I suddenly realized to my astonishment that I no longer suffered. Yes, I breathed more freely, I was able to stretch out my arms with greater vigor, I felt a new buoyancy in my limbs; at last, one might say, someone had removed the crushing weight which for so long a time I had borne on my shoulders. I experienced a keen joy in moving my limbs, in exercising my muscles and joints, in setting my nerves into vibration, when it thus came upon me one morning, in a leap from my bed. Was I not really awakening from a slumber as deep as death? Was I not recovering from a sort of catalepsy, in which my whole being, sunk in torpor, had known the horrible nightmare of nonexistence? I was like one buried who finds the light of day again, like one famished who is given a piece of bread, like one sentenced to death who receives his pardon.﻿ ﻿… I went to the window and looked out into the street. The slanting rays of the sun were flooding the houses in front of me with a golden mist; on the sidewalk people were hurriedly passing, preoccupied, with a happy gait; carriages joyously crossed each other’s path. The hustle and bustle and noise of life intoxicated, stirred, carried me away, and I cried out:

“I don’t love you anymore! I don’t love you anymore!”

In the space of a second I had a very clear vision of a new life of work and happiness. I was to cleanse myself of this filth, to seize my interrupted dreams; not only did I want to redeem my honor, but I wanted also to achieve a glory so great, so undisputed, so universal, that Juliette would burst with spite for having lost a man like me. I already saw myself perpetuated in bronze and marble by posterity, placed upon columns and symbolic pedestals, filling the centuries to come with my immortalized image. And what gave me particular pleasure was the thought that Juliette would not share a particle of this glory, and that I pitilessly pushed her off my lofty plane entirely.
===Chinese===
她走后，我任着自己对她、尤其对我自己大发了一通脾气；等怒气平息下去，我愕然发觉：我竟不再痛苦了。是的，我呼吸畅快了些，我能更带动地伸展双臂了，四肢里有了一种新的轻快；可以说，那副我扛了许久、压得我直不起腰的重担，终于有人从我肩上卸下去了。一天早晨，这感觉乘着我从床上一跃而起朝我袭来——活动活动四肢、运转运转肌肉和关节、让神经震颤起来，我从中尝到一种锐利的欢快。我这不是从一场深得像死亡的酣睡里醒过来了吗？我这不是从一种僵住症里缓过来了么——在那病症里，我整个身心沉陷于麻木，尝尽了不复存在的骇人噩梦？我像一个被活埋的人重见天日，像一个饿到极点的人得了一块面包，像一个死囚得了赦书。……我走到窗前，朝街上望去。斜照的阳光把金色的薄雾漫上我对面的房屋；人行道上行人匆匆来去，心事重重，脚步却轻快；马车欢欢喜喜地交错而过。生活的熙攘与喧声使我陶醉、使我振奋、把我卷了去，我喊出声来：

“我不爱你了！我不爱你了！”

转瞬之间，我清清楚楚看见了另一种劳作与幸福的新生活。我要洗净这一身污垢，重拾中断的梦想；我不但要挽回我的名誉，还要赢得一场那么盛大、那么无可争辩、那么普天同仰的荣耀，叫朱丽叶特为失掉我这样一个男人气得发狂、悔恨交加。我已经看见后世用青铜和大理石把我传之久远，把我安上圆柱和象征的基座，让我不朽的形象充塞未来的世纪。而尤其叫我受用的，是想到朱丽叶特在这荣耀里连一星半点儿也分不到，想到我毫不留情地把她从我高峻的境地上整个儿推了下去。

===Original===
I went down, and for the first time in two years felt a delicious pleasure in being on the street. I walked fast, with supple movements, a victorious gait, interested in the simplest things about me which seemed so new. And I asked myself with amazement how in the world I could have been unhappy so long, why my eyes had not opened to the truth much sooner than they did.﻿ ﻿… Ah, that despicable Juliette! How she must have laughed at my submission, my blindness, my pitifulness, my inconceivable folly! No doubt, she told her casual lovers of my idiotic grief. But I was going to have my revenge and it would be terrible! Juliette would soon lie prostrate at my feet begging my pardon.

“No, no, you miserable creature, never!﻿ ﻿… When I cried, did you comfort me?﻿ ﻿… Did you spare me a single suffering, a single one? Did you ever for a moment consent to share my misery, to live my life with me? You don’t deserve to share my glory. No﻿ ﻿… go!”

And to show my absolute contempt for her, I would throw millions in her face.

“Here are your millions! You said you wanted millions? Here are some more!”
===Chinese===
我下了楼，两年来头一回在街上尝到一种甜丝丝的愉快。我走得飞快，动作轻捷，步态像个得胜者，连身边顶寻常的事物也叫我看得入迷，样样都那么新鲜。我诧异地问自己：我究竟怎么会不幸这么久，我的眼睛为什么没有早得多就睁开、认出真相。……啊，那个可鄙的朱丽叶特！她一定狠狠笑话过我的卑顺、我的瞎眼、我的可怜相、我那份难以想象的痴愚！不用问，她把我的痴憨的伤心事讲给那些萍水相逢的情夫们听了。可我就要复仇了，那复仇可怕得很！朱丽叶特很快就会拜倒在我脚下，乞求我的饶恕。

“不，不，你这下贱东西，休想！……我哭的时候，你可曾安慰过我？……你可曾饶过我一丝苦楚，一丝一毫？你可曾有一刻肯分担我的困厄，跟我一起过我的日子？你不配分享我的荣耀。不……滚吧！”

为了表示我对她的绝对轻蔑，我要把几百万甩到她脸上去。

“这是你的几百万！你不是说过要几百万吗？再给你一些！”

===Original===
Juliette would wring her arms in despair.

“Have mercy, Jean! Have pity on me! I don’t want your money! What I want is to live in obscurity and humbly in your shadow, happy if a single ray of light surrounding you will some day come to rest upon your poor Juliette. Have pity on me!”

“Did you have pity on me when I asked for it! No! Women like you should be killed with blows of gold. Here! Have some more! Here! Some more still!”

I was walking with long strides, talking aloud, moving my hands as if throwing millions into space. “Here, wretch, here!”
===Chinese===
朱丽叶特会绝望地绞着双臂。

“发发慈悲，让！可怜可怜我吧！我不要你的钱！我只想低微地藏在你影子里的暗处，若有一天，环绕着你的哪一线光亮落定在你可怜的朱丽叶特身上，便是我的幸福。可怜可怜我吧！”

“我求你可怜我的时候，你可曾可怜过我！没有！你们这样的女人，就该用黄金砸死。拿去！再给你一些！拿去！再拿一些！”

我大步流星地走着，高声自语，双手乱挥，仿佛正把几百万抛向空中。“拿去，贱货，拿去！”

===Original===
Nevertheless, my insusceptibility to everything else when preoccupied with the thought of Juliette was not so complete as to preclude my getting uneasy at the sight of any woman, and scrutinizing with an impatient glance the inside of the carriages which endlessly passed by on the street. On the boulevard my assurance left me, and anguish again seized my whole being. I felt an unbearable burden upon my shoulders, and the devouring beast driven off but a moment ago, rushed on me more ferociously than ever, sinking its fangs into my flesh deeper than ever. It was enough for me to see the theatres, the restaurants, those evil places full of the mystery of Juliette’s life, to make me feel this. The theatres were saying to me: “She was here that night; while you were moaning, calling her, waiting for her﻿—she was promenading in her stage box, with flowers on her bosom, happy, without the slightest thought of you.” The restaurants were saying: “That night your Juliette was here.﻿ ﻿… With eyes drunk with lust she was rolling on our broken sofas, and men who smelled of wine and cigars possessed her.” And all the agile, handsome young men I met on the street seemed to say to me: “We know your Juliette. Does she give you any of the money she charges us?” Every house, every object, every manifestation of life cried with a frightful chuckle: “Juliette! Juliette!” The sight of roses at the florist’s was painful, and I felt rage boil within me each time I looked at the shopwindows with their display of inviting things. It seemed to me that Paris was spending all its power, using all its seduction, to rob me of Juliette, and I wished to see it perish in some catastrophe; I regretted that the rigorous days of the Commune were over, when one could pour petroleum and scatter death upon the streets! I returned home.
===Chinese===
然而，我一门心思在朱丽叶特身上时对身外万事的浑然不觉，还没到瞧见随便哪个女人都不动声色的地步——我照旧惴惴不安，照旧焦灼地扫视一辆辆在街上不住驶过的马车的车厢。一走上林荫大道，我的笃定便弃我而去，苦痛重新攫住我的全身。我感到肩头又压上了那副难以承受的重担，方才被驱走的那头噬人的野兽，比先前更凶猛地朝我扑来，獠牙扎进我的肉里比任何时候都深。只消看见那些戏院、那些餐馆、那些浸透朱丽叶特生活之谜的邪恶去处，就够叫我生出这感觉了。戏院在对我说：“那天晚上她就在这儿；你呻吟、呼唤、等她的时候——她正在包厢里招摇，胸前簪着花，快活得很，压根儿没想起你。”餐馆在对我说：“那天晚上，你的朱丽叶特就在这儿。……她眼里燃着醉醺醺的欲火，在我们塌陷的沙发上打滚，浑身酒气和雪茄味的男人们占有了她。”我在街上遇见的所有那些身手矫健、相貌英俊的年轻人，仿佛都在对我说：“我们认识你的朱丽叶特。她收我们的钱，分给你一点儿没有？”每一座房子、每一样东西、每一阵生命的动静，都发出可怕的窃笑，喊道：“朱丽叶特！朱丽叶特！”花店里玫瑰的姿影刺痛我，每回望见橱窗里陈列的那些诱人的物事，我都觉得怒火在胸中翻滚。在我看来，巴黎正使出浑身解数、用尽全部魅惑来夺走我的朱丽叶特，我巴不得看着它葬身在某场大灾大难里；我惋惜巴黎公社（the Commune）那些严酷的日子已经过去——那时候，人还可以朝街上浇石油、播撒死亡！我回家了。

===Original===
“Did anyone call?” I asked the caretaker.

“No, Monsieur Mintié.”

“No letters either?”

“No, Monsieur Mintié.”

“Are you sure nobody went up to my room while I was away?”

“The key was not touched.”
===Chinese===
“有人来找过我吗？”我问门房。

“没有，曼蒂埃先生。”

“也没有信？”

“没有，曼蒂埃先生。”

“您能肯定，我不在家的时候没人上过我的房间？”

“钥匙没人动过。”

===Original===
I scribbled the following words on my card: “I want to see you.”

“Take this over to the Rue de Balzac.”

I waited in the street, impatient, nervous; the caretaker was not long in returning.

“The maid told me that Madame had not yet come back.”

It was seven o’clock. I went to my room and stretched out on the sofa.
===Chinese===
我在名片上潦草写下几个字：“我要见你。”

“把这个送到巴尔扎克街去。”

我在街上等着，心急火燎，浑身紧张；门房没过多大工夫就回来了。

“女仆告诉我，太太还没有回来。”

七点钟了。我回到自己房间，直挺挺躺在长沙发上。

===Original===
“She won’t come. Where is she? What is she doing?”

I did not light the candles. The window, illuminated by the street, shone in the room with a dark glimmer, reflected a yellow shine upon the ceiling, where appeared the trembling shadow of the curtains. And the hours passed, slow and endless, so endless and so slow that one might say the flow of time had suddenly stopped.

“She won’t come!”

From the street, the intermittent noise of vehicles reached me; the buses rolled heavily, the closed carriages passed by lightly and rapidly. When one of them passed close to the sidewalk or slowed down I would rush to the window, which I had left half-open, to look into the street.﻿ ﻿… No one alighted.
===Chinese===
“她不会来了。她在哪儿？她在干什么？”

我没有点蜡烛。让街灯映亮的窗子在房间里泛着幽暗的微光，往天花板上投下一片黄色的光晕，帘子的影子在那上面颤动。一个个钟头挨过去，缓慢无尽，无尽又缓慢，简直可以说时间的流泻猛然停住了。

“她不会来了！”

街上车辆的声响断断续续传上来；公共汽车沉沉地滚过，封闭的马车轻快地疾驰。每当有一辆贴着人行道驶过、或是慢了下来，我就扑向那扇留了半开的窗子，朝街上张望。……没有人下车。

===Original===
“She won’t come!”

And while saying to myself: “She won’t come,” I hoped that Juliette would be in shortly. Oh, how many times I had rolled on the sofa, crying: “She won’t come!” And Juliette always came. Always at the moment when I most despaired, I heard a carriage stop, then steps on the stairway, a creaking noise in the hallway, and Juliette would appear smiling, adorned with plumes, filling the room with a strong odor of perfume and the rustling of silk in motion.

“Come on, get your hat, my dear.”
===Chinese===
“她不会来了！”

我一边对自己说“她不会来了”，一边又盼着朱丽叶特转眼就到。啊，我曾多少回在沙发上翻滚，哭喊着：“她不会来了！”可朱丽叶特总是来。总在我最绝望的当口，我听见一辆马车停下，接着楼梯上响起脚步声，门厅里一阵窸窣，朱丽叶特便笑吟吟地出现了，头上插着羽毛，满屋子顿时充溢着浓烈的香水气和走动时丝绸的窸窣声。

“来吧，拿上你的帽子，亲爱的。”

===Original===
Irritated by her smile, by her dress, by the perfume, exasperated by the long waiting, I used to upbraid her severely:

“Where have you been? In what joints have you been? Yes, tell me, in what joints?”

“Ah! You are trying to make a scene. Well, thanks! I am leaving. Good night! And here I have taken all the pains in the world to snatch a moment to look you up!”

Then pointing my finger to the door, my muscles contracted, I would burst out:

“Well, go ahead! Go to the devil! And never come back again, never!”

With the door scarcely shut behind Juliette, I would run after her.
===Chinese===
她的笑、她的裙子、她的香水惹恼了我，漫长的等待更叫我火冒三丈，我总要狠狠地数落她：

“你上哪儿去了？进了哪些下等场所？说呀，告诉我，哪些场所？”

“啊！你是存心吵架。好啊，多谢！我走了。晚安！我千辛万苦挤出工夫来看你，倒落得这般下场！”

于是我用手指着门，肌肉绷紧，勃然发作：

“好啊，走哇！见鬼去吧！再也别回来了，永远别回来！”

朱丽叶特身后的门还没关严，我就追了出去。

===Original===
“Juliette! Come back, please! Juliette! Wait.﻿ ﻿… I am going with you.”

She would still be descending the stairs, without turning her head. I would catch up with her.

Near her, near this dress, these plumes, these flowers, these jewels, fury would again seize me:

“Come right up with me or I’ll crack your head against these steps!”

And when in the room I would throw myself at her feet.

“Ah, my little Juliette, I am wrong, I know I am wrong. But I suffer so much! Have pity on me! If you only knew in what a hell I am living! If you could only tear my breast open and see what is going on in my heart! Juliette! Oh, I can’t, I can’t go on living like this anymore! Even a beast would have pity on me. Yes, a wretched beast would have pity on me!”
===Chinese===
“朱丽叶特！回来吧，求求你！朱丽叶特！等一等。……我跟你一起去。”

她还在下楼梯，头也不回。我追上她。

一挨近她，挨近这身裙子、这些羽毛、这些花、这些珠宝，狂怒便重新攫住我：

“马上跟我上楼，不然我就把你的脑袋往这些台阶上磕碎！”

回到房间里，我便扑倒在她脚下。

“啊，我的小朱丽叶特，是我错了，我知道我错了。可我太痛苦了！可怜可怜我吧！你要知道我活在怎样一座地狱里就好了！你能撕开我的胸膛、看看我心里翻腾着什么就好了！朱丽叶特！啊，我受不了了，再也这样活不下去了！连一头畜生都会可怜我。是啊，一头再卑贱不过的畜生都会可怜我！”

===Original===
I would press her arms, cling to her dress.

“My Juliette! I have not killed you, though I have a perfect right to, I swear. I have not killed you! You should have given an account of yourself. I must make inhuman efforts to control myself, for you don’t know what terrible and vengeful things a man who suffers and is lonely can conceive. I have not killed you! I have been hoping!﻿—I am still hoping! Come back to me. I’ll forget everything, I’ll erase everything from my memory, my sorrow and my shame.﻿ ﻿… You will be to me the purest, the most radiant of virgins. We’ll go away, far, far away from here. Wherever you wish. I shall marry you! Don’t you want me to? Do you think I am telling you this in order to have you with me again? Swear to me that you will change your mode of life and I’ll kill myself here in front of you! Listen, I have sacrificed everything for you! I am not talking of my fortune, but of what was formerly the pride of my life, my manly honor, my dream of an artist, all this I have given up for you, without the least regret. You should make some sacrifice for me in turn. And pray, what is it I ask of you? Nothing﻿ ﻿… except the gladness of being honest and good. To devote, to consecrate oneself to something, why that’s so grand, so noble! Oh, if you only knew the infinite pleasure of sacrifice? Look now﻿ ﻿… Malterre is rich. He is a good fellow, better than the others, he loved you! I’ll go to him, I’ll say to him: ‘You alone can save Juliette, you alone can save Juliette, you alone can bring her back from the life she is living. Go back to her, and don’t be afraid of me. I am going out of her life.’ Do you want me to do that?”

Juliette would look at me, greatly astonished. An uneasy smile would play on her lips. She would murmur:

“Come, my dear, you say silly things. Don’t cry, come!”

While going out, I would continue to lament: “A beast would have pity on me! Yes, a beast!”
===Chinese===
我攥紧她的胳膊，揪住她的裙子不放。

“我的朱丽叶特！我没有杀死你，尽管我完全有这个权利，我发誓。我没有杀死你！你本该对自己有个交代。我必须作出非人的努力来克制自己，因为你不知道一个痛苦而孤独的男人，心里能盘算出多么可怕、多么挟怨报复的事情。我没有杀死你！我一直存着指望！——现在还存着指望！回到我身边来吧。我会忘掉一切，把一切从记忆里抹去，我的悲伤和我的耻辱。……你在我眼里，将是最纯洁、最光艳照人的处女。我们远走，离这儿远远的。你愿意去哪儿就去哪儿。我要娶你！你不愿我这样吗？你以为我说这些话，只是为了把你重新留在身边吗？你向我发誓改换你的生活方式，我就当着你的面死给你看！听着，我为你牺牲了一切！我说的不是钱财，而是我从前的骄傲——我男子汉的名誉、我的艺术家之梦，这一切我都为你舍弃了，没有一丝惋惜。你也该反过来为我作点儿牺牲。再说，我求你的究竟是什么？什么也没有……不过是做一个正直的好人的欢喜罢了。把自己奉献给什么、献身给什么，那是多么宏大、多么高贵的事啊！啊，你要是知道牺牲的无穷乐趣就好了！你看……马尔特尔有钱。他是个好人，比别的男人强，他爱过你！我这就去找他，对他说：‘只有你能救朱丽叶特，只有你能救朱丽叶特，只有你能把她从她正过着的生活里拉回来。回到她身边去吧，别怕我。我就从她的生活里退出去。’你要我这样做吗？”

朱丽叶特会万分诧异地望着我。一抹不安的微笑浮上她的唇边。她会喃喃地说：

“得啦，亲爱的，你在说傻话。别哭了，来吧！”

出门的时候，我还要一路哀叹：“连畜生都会可怜我！是啊，畜生！”

===Original===
At other times, she would send Celestine for me, and I would find her in bed, cold, sad and tired. I could see that someone had been there just a moment ago, someone who had just left; I could see it in everything that surrounded me﻿—in the bed just made, in the toilette articles arranged with overscrupulous care, in all the carefully removed traces which in my imagination reappeared again in all their hidden and sorrowful reality. I would linger in the dressing room, rummage among the drawers, examine objects, even lower myself to a shameful scrutiny of her personal belongings.﻿ ﻿… Juliette would call me:

“Come over here, my dear! What are you doing there?”

Oh! If I could only reconstruct his image, find the least trace of that man! I inhaled the air, inflated my nostrils, hoping to come upon the strong male scent, and it seemed to me that the shadow of a mighty torso spread itself over the hangings, that I distinguished huge, athletic arms, quivering thighs with bulging muscles.

“Are you coming?” Juliette would repeat.
===Chinese===
另一些时候，她打发塞莱斯特来叫我，我便看见她躺在床上，冷淡、忧伤、疲惫。我看得出来，刚刚有人来过，刚走；从围着我的一切上我都看得出来——在才铺好的床上，在收拾得过分仔细的梳妆用具上，在那些被小心清除的痕迹上；而在我的想象里，那些痕迹又以它们全部隐蔽而伤心的本相重新浮现出来。我会在梳妆室里流连，翻检一个个抽屉，端详一件件东西，甚至堕落到可耻地窥探她的贴身物件。……朱丽叶特会喊我：

“到这儿来，亲爱的！你在那儿干什么呢？”

啊！要是我能复原那人的形象、找出那个男人最细微的一点痕迹就好了！我吸着气，鼓起鼻孔，指望撞上那股浓烈的男性气味；我仿佛看见一副雄壮躯干的暗影在床帏上铺展开来，分辨出巨大的、运动员般的臂膀，肌肉隆起、微微颤动的大腿。

“你到底来不来？”朱丽叶特会再问一遍。

===Original===
On those nights Juliette would talk of nothing but the soul, the sky, the birds, telling me that she was in need of an ideal, of celestial dreams. Huddled in my arms, chaste as a child, she would say, with a sigh:

“Oh, how nice it is to sit like this! Tell me something beautiful, my Jean, some such thing as one reads about in poetry. I love your voice so much; it is so musical﻿ ﻿… speak to me long. You are so good, you comfort me so well! I would like to live all my life like this, always in your arms, without stirring, listening to you! Do you know what else I would like to have? Ah, I am dreaming of it all the time! I would like to have a nice little baby girl who should be like a cherub, all pink and blonde! I would nurse her myself and you would sing her some pretty little songs to put her to sleep! My Jean, when I am dead you will find in my jewelry case a little pink writing book with gold ornaments. That’s for you. Take it. There I have written down my thoughts, and you’ll see whether I loved you or not! You’ll see! Ah! Tomorrow one must get up again, go out﻿ ﻿… how annoying! Rock me, speak to me, tell me that you love my soul﻿ ﻿… my soul!﻿ ﻿…”

And she would fall asleep, and in her sleep look so white, so pure, that the bed curtains would seem like wings attached to her.
===Chinese===
那样的夜晚，朱丽叶特满口只谈灵魂、天空、小鸟，对我说她需要一种理想，需要天国的梦。她蜷在我怀里，像孩子一样贞洁，叹口气说：

“啊，这样坐着多好！给我讲点儿美的东西吧，我的让，讲点儿像诗里写到的那样的事。我多爱你的声音；它那么悦耳，像音乐……多给我讲一会儿吧。你这么好，你安慰得我这么熨帖！我愿意一辈子都这样，永远在你怀里，一动不动，听你说话！你知道我还想要什么吗？啊，我时时刻刻都在梦想这个！我想要一个粉团儿一样、金头发的小女儿，像个小天使！我要亲自奶她，你呢，给她唱些好听的小曲儿哄她睡觉！我的让，等我死了，你会在我的首饰盒里找到一本带金饰的粉红色小笔记本。那是给你的。拿去吧。那里头我写下了我的心思，你就会明白我到底爱不爱你了！你会明白的！啊，明天又得起身、出门……多烦人！摇摇我，跟我说话，告诉我你爱我的灵魂……我的灵魂！……”

她就这样睡去，睡梦中的脸色那么白、那么纯净，床帐竟像安在她身上的一双翅膀。

===Original===
Night came on, the suburb grew quiet. From afar, belated carriages were returning, and on the sidewalk two policemen paced with heavy, dragging strides, keeping in step.﻿ ﻿… Several times the door of the furnished house opened and closed; I heard some creaky noise, the rustling of a woman’s dress, whispering voices in the hallway. But it was not Juliette. The silent house seemed to have been asleep a long while. I left the sofa, lit my lamp, looked at the clock; it was three o’clock.

“She won’t come! Now it’s all over. She won’t come!”

I stood at the window. The street was deserted, the dark sky hung over the houses like a leaden lid. Over yonder in the direction of Boulevard Haussman large vehicles were coming downhill, shaking the night with their loud jolting.﻿ ﻿… A rat darted from one sidewalk to the other and disappeared into a hole in the gutter.﻿ ﻿… I saw a homeless dog with hanging head and tail between its hind legs passing, stopping at the doors, smelling the gutter, dolefully walking away.

I shook with fever, my brain was inflamed, my hands were moist and again I felt a stifling sensation in my chest.
===Chinese===
夜深了，这一带街区静了下来。远处，迟归的马车正往回赶；人行道上，两名警察迈着沉重拖沓的步子来回踱着，步子踩得整整齐齐。……这栋带家具的公寓，大门几次开了又关；我听见吱吱呀呀的响动、女人裙裾的窸窣、门厅里的低语。可那不是朱丽叶特。沉寂的房子仿佛早已睡熟。我离开沙发，点起油灯，看了看钟；三点了。

“她不会来了！这回全完了。她不会来了！”

我站在窗前。街上空荡荡的，黑魆魆的天空像一面铅盖扣在房屋上。远处奥斯曼大道（Boulevard Haussman）那个方向，大车辆正隆隆驶下坡来，颠簸声震得夜直颤。……一只老鼠从街这边的人行道窜到那边，钻进阴沟的一个洞里没了影。……我看见一条无家可归的狗，耷拉着脑袋，尾巴夹在后腿间，一路走过，在一家家门前停下，嗅嗅阴沟，凄凄惶惶地走远了。

我发着寒热直打颤，脑子里像烧着火，两手潮湿，胸口又感到一阵憋闷。

===Original===
“She won’t come! Where is she? Did she go back to her house? Where, in what filthy hole of this great impure night is she wallowing?”

What made me particularly angry was that she did not let me know ahead of time. She had received my card. She knew she was not coming. And she did not send me a single word! I had cried, implored, begged her on my knees﻿ ﻿… and not a word from her! How many tears, how much blood must one shed to soften that heart of flint? How could she run after pleasure with her ears still full of the echoes of my sobs, her mouth still moist with my entreating kisses? The most wretched women, the most detestable creatures at some time or other call a temporary halt to their life of dissipation and prey; there are moments when they permit the sun to penetrate their chilled hearts, when turning their eyes to heaven they pray for love that pardons and redeems! But Juliette﻿ ﻿… never! Something more insensible than fate, something more relentless than death was driving her, was eternally drawing and spurring her on without respite, without pause, from impure to criminal love, from that which dishonors to that which kills! The more days passed, the more marks of infamy debauchery left on her. With her passion, formerly so normal and healthy, were now mingled a depraved inquisitiveness and that savage unsatiableness, that overstimulation of irrepressible lust which comes as result of excessive and sterile pleasures. Except on the nights when exhaustion invested the sordid reality of her existence with unexpected forms of the purest ideal, one could see upon her the imprints of a thousand different and refined corruptions, of a thousand grotesque perversions practiced upon her by those palled by vice and age. Words and cries often escaped her which suddenly lit up her whole life and opened up vistas of frenzied sensuality, and although she would thereby communicate to me the consuming passion of her depravity, although I myself relished in all this a sort of infernal criminal voluptuousness, I could not look at Juliette without a shudder!﻿ ﻿… And when leaving her embrace, ashamed and disgusted, I felt the need, often experienced by reprobates, of looking at tranquil, restful sights, and I envied﻿—oh, with what keen regret!﻿—the superior beings who had made purity and virtue the inflexible laws of their life!﻿ ﻿… I dreamed of convents where one spent one’s life in prayer, of hospitals where one devoted oneself to others.﻿ ﻿… I was seized with a mad desire to enter the disreputable joints and preach the gospel to the unfortunate people who wallow in vice there, never hearing a single word of kindness; I promised myself to follow the prostitutes at night, into the shadow of public squares, to console them, to speak to them of virtue with such passionate earnestness, in accents so touching that they would be moved, would burst into tears and would say to me: “Yes, save us.﻿ ﻿…” I liked to spend hours in the Monceau park, watching the children play, discovering a paradise of goodness in the glances of young mothers; I was moved to reconstruct their lives so remote from my own; to live through, while near them, their sacred joys forever lost to me.﻿ ﻿… On Sundays I used to loiter at the railway stations where I mingled with the merry crowds, among petty officials and workingmen leaving town with their families to get a little fresh air for their affected lungs, to gather a little strength to be able to withstand the fatigue of their work during the week. I followed the steps of some laborer whose face interested me; I would have liked to possess his bent back, his deformed hands turned brown through hard work, his stiff walk, his trusting eyes of a house dog.﻿ ﻿… Alas!﻿ ﻿… I would have liked to have everything I did not have, to be everybody that I was not!﻿ ﻿… These wanderings which rendered the realization of my downfall even more painful, did me some good, however, and I used to come home each time with all sorts of courageous resolutions.﻿ ﻿… But in the evening I would see Juliette again, and Juliette was to me the oblivion of all honor and all duty.
===Chinese===
“她不会来了！她在哪儿？她回自己家了吗？在哪儿？在这庞大而不洁的黑夜里，她在哪个污秽的坑里打滚？”

尤其叫我怒不可遏的是，她竟不提前知会我一声。她收到了我的名片。她知道自己不来了。却连一个字也不捎给我！我哭过，哀告过，跪着乞求过她……而她一个字也没有！要软化那颗燧石般的心，得流多少眼泪、淌多少血才够？她怎能耳朵里还回响着我的呜咽，嘴上还留着我哀求的亲吻的湿气，转身就去追逐欢乐？最不幸的女人、最可憎的造物，也总有停下荒淫噬人的日子歇口气的时候；总有那样的时刻，她们容许阳光透进自己冰冷的心，抬眼望天，祈求那宽恕而救赎的爱！可朱丽叶特……从来没有！有什么比命运更无感的东西、比死亡更无情的东西在驱赶着她，不休不止地拽着她、策着她，从不洁的爱奔向罪恶的爱，从玷辱人的奔向戕害人的！日子过得越久，荒淫在她身上留下的耻辱印记就越多。她那原本正常而健康的情欲里，如今掺进了一种变态的好奇，掺进了那种野性的不知餍足、那种过度的享乐催出来的、按不住的情欲亢奋。除非在精力耗竭的夜晚，她那生存的肮脏现实才会意外地披上最纯洁的理想的形貌；除此之外，谁都能在她身上看出千百种各色各样、精巧入里的腐蚀的印痕，看出千百种怪诞的变态——那是些被恶行和年岁磨得没了胃口的人在她身上试演过的。话语和呼喊常常不由自主地从她口里逸出，猝然照亮她的整个生活，敞开一片颠狂肉欲的远景；尽管她由此把她堕落的那种噬人的激情传给了我，尽管我自己在这一切里也尝到一种地狱般的、罪恶的肉欲之甜，可我看朱丽叶特一眼，仍禁不住打个寒噤！……而离开她的怀抱时，我又羞又恶心，感到一种堕落者常有的需要：去看看安宁、休憩的景致；我艳羡——啊，怀着怎样痛切的懊悔啊！——那些把纯洁与美德立为生活铁律的超凡之人！……我梦想修道院，在那里人的一生在祈祷中度过；我梦想医院，在那里人为他人献身。……我起过疯狂的念头，要走进那些声名狼藉的下等场所，向里面沉陷于罪恶、从没听过一句好话的不幸人们宣讲福音；我打定主意夜里跟着妓女们走，跟到广场的暗影里去，安慰她们，怀着炽热的恳切、用那么动人的声调对她们讲美德，叫她们感动，叫她们热泪滚滚，对我说：“是的，救救我们。……”我喜欢在蒙梭公园（Monceau park）一待几个钟头，看孩子们玩耍，在年轻母亲们的目光里发现一个善良的乐园；我动情地在想象里重建她们那种与我的生活何其遥远的日子；挨在她们身旁，把我永远失去了的神圣欢乐重新体味一遍。……礼拜天，我常在火车站里闲逛，混在快活的人群中间，混在小职员和工人中间——他们携家带口出城，让吃坏了的肺透一口新鲜空气，攒一点儿气力，好扛得住一星期劳作的疲乏。我跟在一个面相叫我感兴趣的工人身后；我多想拥有他那副佝偻的背、那双被苦活磨得又粗又黑、变了形的手，那种僵直的步态，那种家犬一般信赖人的眼睛。……唉！……我多想拥有我所没有的一切，成为我所不是的一切人！……这些游荡固然叫我更痛切地意识到自己的沉沦，却到底对我有些好处，每回我都揣着种种勇敢的决心回家。……可一到晚上，我又见到朱丽叶特，而朱丽叶特之于我，就是对一切名誉、一切义务的遗忘。

===Original===
Above the houses the sky was brightened by a feeble light announcing the approaching dawn, and at the end of the street, in the shadow, I noticed two glaring points, the two lights of a carriage, vacillating, swerving, approaching, which resembled two errant gas lamps.﻿ ﻿… Hope revived in me for a moment﻿ ﻿… the carriage came nearer, dancing on the pavement, the lights grew larger, the rattling quickened.﻿ ﻿… I thought I recognized the familiar trundling of Juliette’s brougham!﻿ ﻿… But no!﻿ ﻿… Suddenly the carriage turned to the left and disappeared.﻿ ﻿… Within an hour it would already be day!

“She won’t come!﻿ ﻿… This time it is all over, she won’t come!”

I closed the window, lay down again on the sofa, blood surging in my temples, all my members aching.﻿ ﻿… In vain I tried to sleep.﻿ ﻿… I could not do anything but weep, cry out:

“Oh! Juliette! Juliette!”
===Chinese===
房屋上方的天空被一线微光照亮，预告黎明将临；街尽头，暗影里，我注意到两个刺目的亮点——一辆马车的两盏灯，摇曳着、拐闪着，渐渐逼近，像两盏走失的煤气灯。……希望在我心里复活了一瞬……马车近了，在路石上颠跳着，灯影越来越大，辚辚声越来越急。……我以为认出了朱丽叶特那辆四轮轿式马车熟悉的辘辘声！……可是不！……马车突然向左一拐，不见了。……再过一个钟头，天就要亮了！

“她不会来了！……这回全完了，她不会来了！”

我关上窗子，重新躺到长沙发上，血往太阳穴里涌，周身的肢节都在酸痛。……我想睡，白费劲。……我只会哭，只会喊：

“啊！朱丽叶特！朱丽叶特！”

===Original===
My chest was burning, I felt the sensation of boiling lava swirling in my head. My thoughts were in confusion, turning into hallucinations. Along the walls of my bedroom weasels were chasing one another, jumping, abandoning themselves to obscene frolics. I was hoping that I would succumb to fever, that it would chain me to my bed, that it would cause my death. To be sick! Ah!﻿ ﻿… yes, to be sick, long, forever! I had visions of Juliette installing herself in my room.
